It still reads as a card: `#ECE9E4` on the `#F7F5F2` page is a **1.11:1** step, small but a real edge once the 16px radius cuts the corner, and the rows keep their own `#D9D5CE` hairlines (**1.21:1** on the card). Nothing measurable is lost — a card-to-page boundary is a decorative surface edge, not text (1.4.3) and not a control or state indicator (1.4.11), so no minimum applies to it, and every text pair on the card is untouched: key `--slate` **5.95:1**, value `--ink` **14.37:1**. Applied to **tracking's ETA card in the same pass**: it is the same component drawn from the same two lines of CSS (concept `.card` surface + hairline rows), and letting delivery go flat while tracking stayed raised would put one component at two values — the thing rev 25 was built to avoid. `delivery-error` / `delivery-loading` carry the rule but not the element (their states show the message block and the skeleton instead); the declaration still moves with them, because the three delivery `<style>` blocks are byte-identical by contract, as are the four tracking ones — re-verified after this edit (delivery 10 352 chars ×3, tracking 11 848 ×4). The other shadowed surfaces are left alone: `.msg-block`, the operator card and the map keep `--sh-card` / `--sh-raised`, so the screen still has a depth order — this card simply stops competing with the photograph above it.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 29)** — Two more designer notes in the same breath — `tracking`'s `main`: *"remove the shadows from the cards"*, and `delivery-error`'s alert: *"remove the shadow from the card"* — so rev 28's single card becomes the rule for the whole painted set: **card surfaces are flat**. `--sh-card` is gone from `.op-card`, `.msg-block` (tracking, delivery and the order-history empty block), `article.card` and the loading `.sk-card`. Order-history's **hover lift** goes with it: `article.card:hover` was raising to `--sh-raised`, and a card that casts no shadow at rest cannot grow one under the pointer — hover is desktop-only besides, and what marks the card as reachable is the controls inside it and the keyboard focus ring. Geometry is untouched, because a shadow is not layout: the skeleton card still measures **263.0px against the delivered card's 263.6px**, so rev 24's no-reflow claim holds. **What still casts a shadow, and why:** the live map — `--sh-raised`, concept `.mapc`'s own value; it is media, not a card, and it is now the *only* raised surface on the tracking screen, which turns depth from decoration into a single deliberate cue — and the green primary, `--sh-sm`, concept `.btn.primary`'s own value. The 2px `--media` rings on the status indicators and the 4px green ring on the active milestone are cutouts and state rings, not shadows, and are untouched. Verified by computed style across all ten painted pages: no `box-shadow` survives on any card surface, and both shared blocks stay byte-identical (delivery 10 392 chars ×3, tracking 11 928 ×4).
- **2026-08-01 (rev 30)** — Rev 29 flattened the ten painted product pages but left the **testbed contradicting them**: `concept/concept.html` — the file that *defines* the card — still drew `.card`, the live-component wrapper `.comp .cc` and the drone-in-context `.dctx figure` with `--sh-card`, its Elevation block still showed a `card` swatch, and its §04 and card notes still read *"separation by soft shadow"* / *"soft shadow (no border)"*. One card at two values, with the source of truth holding the wrong one. All four surfaces are now flat, and the prose says what the build does. The card demo also loses its inline `style="box-shadow:none; background:var(--card)"` — a local override that had been patching the class from the outside, and is redundant now that the class itself is right. **Elevation is re-stated as a two-step ramp of what is actually spent:** `sm · primary` and `raised · media`, with a third swatch showing the flat card so the 1.11:1 tone step is visible next to them rather than merely asserted. `--sh-card` stays declared — in the testbed and in the ten painted pages — as a recorded rung of the ramp, commented as applied to nothing; deleting the declaration would have rewritten the shared `<style>` blocks and invalidated rev 29's byte-identity counts for a dead line. Re-verified after this pass: `var(--sh-card)` appears **0** times across the whole repo, the four card families still carry no `box-shadow`, and the two survivors are unchanged — `--sh-sm` on `.btn.primary`, `--sh-raised` on `.mapc`. §5 gains a standing **Elevation** bullet so the rule has a home in the spec instead of living only in the change log.

Once the photo covers the whole surface, the well is no longer chrome that survives the load; it is the thing being replaced. So rev 24's rule — *chrome stays solid, only the content pulses* — now points the other way on this screen: the well **is** the content, and the square inside it was rehearsing a layout the screen had stopped using. The `::after` is deleted and the pulse moves onto `.sk-block` itself, same 1.4s `skPulse`, same `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback (verified: `animation-name: none` under reduce). Measured against the loaded state: the skeleton is **341 × 180** and the photo it waits for is **341 × 180** — the swap is exact, not approximate. The general lesson, worth applying before the next `-loading` page is reviewed: **a skeleton is a claim about the loaded screen, so it expires when the loaded screen changes treatment** — every future change to a media treatment has to be walked into its skeleton in the same commit. `<style>` blocks re-verified byte-identical across the three delivery states (10 449 chars).
- **2026-08-01 (rev 31)** — Designer note on `tracking`'s map card, *"and on the empty state too"*: the **live map loses `--sh-raised`**. That was the last raised surface in the product, so rev 29's "the map is the only raised surface, which turns depth into a single deliberate cue" is now superseded — **no surface casts a shadow at all, card or media.** Applied to all four tracking states, not the two named, because the four `<style>` blocks are byte-identical by contract; the block also sheds `.map-ph.acquiring`'s `box-shadow:none`, a dead override once the base rule carries no shadow. The edge is still measured, not assumed: the map is a photograph and *is* its own boundary, and in the loading state, where the well shows instead of the city, `#E4E1DA` on `#F7F5F2` is a **1.20:1** step — slightly stronger than the card's 1.11:1, and on the same footing: a surface boundary is a decorative edge, not text (1.4.3) and not a control or state indicator (1.4.11). The testbed moves in the same pass so it cannot contradict the build the way rev 30 caught it doing: `concept.html`'s `.mapc` is flat, §04's Elevation block now reads **one** spent step — `sm · primary`, beside flat card and flat media swatches so the two tone steps are visible rather than asserted — and the `:root` comment says which rungs are recorded and unspent. `--sh-card` and `--sh-raised` both stay declared and applied to nothing. **What still carries `--sh-sm`, and why:** the green primary, which is now the only thing on a product screen with any depth; and, on the spec page only, its own documentation chrome and the ETA chip that floats over the map — a chip on a photograph is legibility over arbitrary pixels, not decoration, and it has no product counterpart yet (tracking's ETA sits in its own row, not on the image). Verified after the pass: `var(--sh-raised)` appears **0** times in the repo, no `box-shadow` survives on any card or media surface across the ten painted pages, and the tracking block stays byte-identical at 11 878 chars ×4.

Card height is **263.0px against the delivered card's 263.6px** (227.0 / 227.6 under 390px), so rev 24's no-reflow claim survives the simplification. **The well does not pulse here, and that is deliberately the opposite of `delivery-loading` (rev 30):** there the photograph covers the well, so the well *is* the content and pulses; here the drone is a transparent cutout contained **inside** the well, so the well is chrome that survives the load and stays solid — which is rev 24's rule read literally. The content on this card is the bars, the arrow and the two action discs: 30 elements still on the 1.4s `skPulse`, 0 under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, both measured. The two loading screens now show the same rule producing opposite answers, and that is the point — the question is never "should the placeholder pulse", it is **"does what loads replace this surface, or sit inside it"**.

Verified: card heights unchanged at **388.7px live / 263.6px delivered ×3**, well box 96 × 72, render 88 × 64, all four images loading — a background never was layout, and this proves it. **The three contrast pairs this moves, all better than before:** the live green dot went from **1.41:1** on the well to **1.52:1** on the card, the delivered `--btn2` dot from **1.16:1** to **1.25:1**, and the cutouts themselves changed ground by **1.03:1** — `#E4E1DA` to `#ECE9E4` — which is nothing, so §6.3's "verified on the light well" holds unchanged. Each dot's 2px ring is `--media`: it used to be exactly the well's colour and therefore invisible, and now sits on the card at **1.08:1**, which is still under the threshold of noticing. **Left alone deliberately, because the note said to:** the ring is not restyled to the new ground, the frame keeps its `--r-media` radius (inert now, one property to restore if the well comes back), and the loading skeleton keeps its filled well — there is no drone image there, the block *is* the placeholder. §6.3's first rule is rewritten to match: the cutout has no well in the product, and keeps one on a spec page, where the asset is the subject and the well is what separates one catalogue cell from the next.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 32)** — Designer note on `tracking`: **the operator avatar moves to the far right of the text**. Done in the markup — the `.op-avatar` span now follows `.op-info` — rather than with `order` or `row-reverse`, so DOM order and visual order still agree, and the reading order that results is the better one: the photo is decorative (`alt=""`), so the name and the verification badge are announced first. Measured after the move: the avatar sits flush at the card's **16px right inset** while the text sits flush at the **16px left inset**, its top is level with the name's top (**0.0px** delta — rev 23's top-alignment rule survives the flip), and the trust chips still hold **one row** (143 + 85 in a 253px column at 375, and in the 234px column at the narrow tier, which is exactly the 234 the pair needs). Applied to the three states that draw the card — `tracking`, `-empty`, `-error`; `-loading` has no operator card, only the shared rule. `<style>` blocks re-verified byte-identical (12 304 chars ×4). **Left alone, and worth deciding before those screens are painted:** `account`, `contact-support`, `operator-account`, `order-confirmed` and `order-confirmed-empty` still draw the base wireframe's avatar-left operator block. **One factual note against the change, recorded rather than argued:** Apple HIG puts a row's leading image on the left, and a right-hand photo reads as an accessory to the text rather than as its subject — and this card is the trust surface for RJ-C1, the highest drop-off in the CJM. The designer has the call; this line exists so the trade stays visible if the card is revisited.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 33)** — Designer note on `order-history`, two cards, two Figma nodes: the **aerial photo & video** card takes `drone-flight-camera` (node `50:3`, the unit with the big gimbal camera) and the **roof inspection** card takes `drone-flight-clean` (node `50:4`, carrying nothing) — and the second is stated as standing: that picture is inspection's from now on. Both are §6.2 in-flight cutouts already in the repo, cut from the same three-drone sheet the linked nodes crop, so nothing new was imported. **This deliberately reopens rev 17's "one register per screen"**: the two delivery cards keep their §6.1 fleet renders (`drone-08`), the two service cards now carry §6.2 cutouts. rev 16 chose cutouts for exactly this reason — in the fleet set every unit carries the same lime cargo box, so a mixed list reads as three deliveries, whereas in the cutout set **the payload is the service**. The screen now takes that benefit only where it is needed and pays for it with a register seam; the seam is the cost, and it is recorded here rather than discovered later. **The size trap, and the fix:** the fleet thumbs are 256² canvases whose subject spans 228px (0.891–0.895 of the width, vertically centred), while a cutout is a tight crop — dropped into the same 96 × 72 box, `drone-flight-camera` drew **86.2 × 64** against its neighbours' 64 × 64, about 1.35× too big. Rather than special-case the CSS, both cutouts are re-cut onto the fleet canvas at the fleet's own subject width — 392 × 291 → 228 × 169 at 14,44 and 409 × 174 → 228 × 97 at 14,80, on 256² transparent ground. Measured after: **all four cards draw 64 × 64** through one `object-fit: contain`, and both re-cuts are downscales, so §6.2's no-upscale rule holds. Thumb weight 54 KB and 36 KB, in line with the fleet's 37–46 KB. **One correction carried in the same commit:** rev 32 took the `--media` ground out from under the drone, which turned `order-history-loading`'s square from chrome into a placeholder — what loads there is now the render alone, nothing survives the swap — so `.sk-well` joins the 1.4s `skPulse` and the reduced-motion fallback, which rev 31 had explicitly ruled out on the old grounds. **Housekeeping, for a human to settle:** two sessions have been appending to this log in parallel and the numbering has collided — there are now two entries each at rev 26, 30, 31 and 32. The text of each is intact; only the labels clash.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 34)** — Designer note on `order-history-empty`: **no ground under the drone** on the empty-state banner. Same call as rev 32 made on the list cards, reached a different way, and the difference is worth writing down. The list cards carry transparent PNGs, so removing `background:var(--media)` was enough. This banner is an inline **JPEG** — no alpha — and the only reason its white rectangle is not visible today is `background-blend-mode: multiply` against the colour underneath it. Delete that colour and the white slab returns; there is nothing left to multiply into. So the colour moves to `--card` instead: the multiply resolves into the card itself, the white disappears, and the soft drop shadow under the cargo box becomes a faint tint of the card rather than a grey blob floating on it. `--card` `#ECE9E4` is *lighter* than the `--media` `#E4E1DA` it replaces, so the render comes out a shade cleaner than before, not darker; box, `--r-media` radius and the 78% / center-92% placement are untouched, so nothing moves. **This leaves the one image in the product that is not a transparent PNG**, which §6.3's own first rule forbids — the honest fix is to re-cut that render as a PNG with alpha and drop the 26 KB base64 blob out of the HTML at the same time. Recorded in §6.3 as a live exception rather than left to be rediscovered.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 34)** — Designer note on `tracking`: *"put the avatar back on the left, but make it the height of the whole card — the width changes proportionally."* This **reverses the avatar-right move** logged the same day (that entry is also numbered rev 32, written by a parallel pass on this repo while the drone-well rev 32 was being written; read it as the avatar entry, this one supersedes it) and retires the 44px well of rev 23. The photo is now a **full-bleed panel on the card's left edge**: `align-self:stretch` sizes its margin box to the card's content height and `-16px` top / bottom / left margins push the border box back out over the card's own inset, so the panel is exactly the card's height and flush with its left edge without a hard-coded height that copy could invalidate. `.op-card` gets `overflow:hidden`, so the card radius cuts the panel's two left corners. Measured: panel **117 × 138.1px**, left offset **0.0**, top offset **0.0**, height delta to the card **0.0**. **The width is a number, 117px, and that is a limitation worth stating.** It is the asset's own 595 × 702 ratio at the height this card resolves to — 117 / 138.1 = **0.847** against the file's **0.848**, so the operator is drawn whole, uncropped and undistorted. It cannot be `aspect-ratio`: the card's height comes from the text column, the text column's width comes from the panel, and a panel sized from the height closes that loop — tried, and the browser resolved it by falling back to the image's natural 595px and blowing the card to 702px tall. `object-fit:cover` means a later copy change crops the photo rather than letterboxing it, and 117 has to be recomputed if the card's copy changes materially. No ground, per §6.3 as amended by rev 32: the same asset class as the drone on `order-history`, so the cutout sits straight on the card. **The cost, stated rather than hidden: the trust chips no longer share one row.** They need **233.9px** and the text column is now **181.2px**. The two constraints are exclusive at this card width — the panel would have to be **≤ 64px** to leave the chips their row, and at 64 × 105.3 its ratio is 0.61 against the asset's 0.85, i.e. the photo is cropped hard on both sides. The newest instruction is the specific one, so the panel wins and the chips wrap; the alternative is one word away, and shortening *"Verified by DRON"* would also buy the row back but that is a `voice.md` / `microcopy.md` decision, not a layout one. The span is moved in the markup rather than with `order` / `row-reverse`, so DOM order and visual order agree again — the photo carries `alt=""` and is skipped by a screen reader either way, which is why the reading-order argument recorded against the right-hand placement does not apply in reverse. Applied to `tracking`, `-empty`, `-error`; `-loading` has no operator card but carries the rule, the four `<style>` blocks being byte-identical by contract — re-verified at **12 963 chars ×4**. `account`, `contact-support`, `operator-account`, `order-confirmed` and `order-confirmed-empty` still draw the base wireframe's avatar and are still waiting for this card.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 35)** — Designer note on `order-history-empty`: *"a bit more padding under the button — 8 to 16px, whichever is right by HIG."* The block's bottom inset goes **20 → 32px**. Of the three candidates the range allows — 28 / 32 / 36 — only **32 sits on the 8-pt grid** the language is built on (§5's 4/8/12/16/24/32/48 scale), and it is that scale's next step up from the 16px inset rather than a one-off number, which is the HIG reading of the question: Apple's own layout guidance is a rhythm, not a table of paddings, so the right answer is the one the rhythm already contains. Measured after: **32.0px** under the button against **16.0px** above it, side inset **16.0**, button height **44.0** — still the HIG target. Sides and top are untouched; this is a bottom inset on a card whose last element is a primary action, not a new card inset, and §5's *Card inset* bullet now records it as such. **Scoped to this page, deliberately.** The `.msg-block` on `tracking` and `delivery` sits at `22px 18px` and has a different job — it carries an alert or a status, not a banner image and a single call to action — so the two were already different values and this does not widen the gap by any principle; unifying them is one word away if the difference is not wanted.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 36)** — Designer note on `tracking-error`: **the alert glyph becomes an empty wi-fi signal.** It was a map pin struck through — a location mark denying itself, which said "we lost the pin", not "we lost the signal", and the body copy says *"we've lost the live signal from the operator's device."* The replacement is not a new drawing: **this page's own status bar already carries the wi-fi glyph** (two concentric arcs about one centre plus a filled source dot), so the state icon is that construction at content-icon scale on the 24-grid, **with the dot removed**. The absence of the source *is* the empty signal — no slash, because a slash says "disabled" and this is not switched off, it is not arriving. Five renderings were drawn at 26px on the danger wash and compared side by side before choosing: outer arc alone (reads as a curve, not wi-fi), outer arc + hollow dot (reads as weak signal), the house arcs with the dot removed (legible but sits high in the box, the dot having been what anchored it), three arcs with the innermost standing in for the dot (indistinguishable from full signal at this size), and the chosen two-arc fan on the full 24-grid, which is the only one that reads as wi-fi *and* as nothing. Nothing else moves: same `.msg-ph.danger` well, same `--danger-wash` / `--danger-ink` pair, same 1.7 stroke, `currentColor`, `fill:none`, and the glyph stays inside an `aria-hidden` well — the meaning is carried by the title *"Live tracking lost"*, so removing an element costs a screen reader nothing (1.4.1 unaffected). Single instance, so there is no repeat to keep in step: the struck-through pin appeared on this page only. `<style>` blocks untouched and re-verified byte-identical at **12 963 chars ×4**. §0.2's inventory gains a **States** group so the glyph has a home in the spec.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 35)** — Designer note on `order-history-empty`: **the space above the render and the space from it down to the text must be equal.** They were not, and by a lot — a pixel scan of the rendered card measured **70px of air above the drone against 41px below it**. The cause is that the placement was set on the *box* while the eye reads the *ink*: the inline JPEG carries roughly 24px of white margin above the drone at this `background-size`, so `background-position: center 92%` was balancing a rectangle whose subject sits low inside it. Fixed by placing the ink instead — `center 16px` puts the drone at rows **55…126** of the card, leaving **55px above it and 55px from it to the title's cap line**. Nothing else moved: the box stays 150px, the card keeps its height and its 16 / 16 / 32 padding, and the render shifted 15px. Two notes for whoever touches this next. **(a)** The lower gap is measured to the title's **cap line**, not to its line box — the 17px title sits in a 23.8px box, so the box starts 4px above the first inked row, and balancing box-to-box would have left the card reading bottom-heavy by exactly that much. **(b)** `16px` is a placement, not a rule: it is derived from this bitmap's white margin at this size, so changing the render, the box height or the title's type re-opens the number. Re-cutting the render as a transparent PNG — the fix §6.3 already asks for — would make the ink and the box the same thing and retire the constant.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 37)** — Designer note on the rev 36 glyph: *"it should have three arcs, and colour with transparency so it's clear there's no signal — as it is, it looks like a broken icon."* Correct, and the failure is worth naming: rev 36 conveyed *absence* by **removing** an element, and a removed element does not read as meaning — it reads as a drawing that went wrong. The glyph is now **whole again — three concentric arcs, the innermost standing where the status bar's source dot sits — and carries its meaning in the ink instead: `opacity .55`**. Nothing is lit, so nothing is arriving. No slash either: a slash says *switched off*, and live tracking is not off, it is not reaching us. Five uniform fades and three graded ones were rendered at 26px on the danger wash and compared in place inside the whole alert card. The graded versions (source solid, outer arcs faint) look better in isolation but say *one bar*, not *none* — the strength convention counts what is lit — so uniform won on meaning. **The measured pair, and the reason it is accepted:** `--danger-ink #9A3115` at .55 over `--danger-wash #F2D8CF` computes to **2.43:1**, under the 3:1 of WCAG 1.4.11. That criterion covers graphics *required* to understand the content; this one is not — it sits in an `aria-hidden` well and the meaning is carried in full by the visible title *"Live tracking lost"* and the body copy, so the glyph is a decorative reinforcement and the threshold does not bind. It is also a deliberate trade: the fade **is** the semantic, and at the .70 that would clear 3:1 the icon just looks like a slightly pale wi-fi — the thing the designer rejected. **.70 / 3.20:1 is the value to switch to if the icon is ever made load-bearing** (given a label of its own, or the title removed). Nothing else on the card moves: same `.msg-ph.danger` well, same tokens, same 1.7 stroke, `currentColor`, `fill:none`. §0.2's **States** row records both the glyph and the fact that this is the single place in the set where opacity means something.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 38)** — Designer note on `delivery-loading`: *"there should be a file-upload icon in the middle here, animated — the arrow animated to show the file going up."* The empty well now carries the **upload glyph, centred and moving**, and the **1.4s opacity pulse of rev 30 goes with it**: that pulse existed only because a blank well had no other way to say *loading*, and two loops on one element would be two things asking for the same attention. **Motion thesis** — the tray holds still and **only the arrow leaves it**, because what this screen is waiting on is a file going *up*, off the operator's device; **1.6s** is the cadence of an upload rather than of a spinner; and the arrow rests **visible** at 0% and 100%, so a dropped animation leaves a legible icon instead of a blank well. One `<g>`, transform and opacity only, one element on the screen. Sampled through the cycle to confirm it does what it claims: rest at **0 / opacity 1**, out to **−3.2 user units / opacity 0** by 880ms, re-entering from **+2.6** and settling back by 1600ms. **The size decision is the one worth recording.** At 40px the glyph is right for a 180px well, but a 24-grid path scaled to 40 draws its 1.7 stroke at **2.83px** — half again heavier than any other icon on the screen. Five combinations were rendered and compared (26@1.7, 40@1.02, 40@1.7, 56@0.73, 56@1.7); the answer is **40px with `stroke-width:1.02`, which renders at exactly 1.70** — the set's own weight. §0.2's rule is one *rendered* line weight, not one declared number at every scale, and the page's other glyphs already land between 1.20 and 2.27 as their boxes vary. Ink is `--slate` on `--media`, **5.51:1**, already in the table. **WCAG 2.2.2 does not bind** — a progress indicator's motion *is* the information it carries — but **Reduce Motion does**, and there the arrow simply rests in the tray. The declaration lives in all three delivery `<style>` blocks by contract, though only `-loading` draws the element; re-verified byte-identical at **11 957 chars ×3**.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 39)** — A full audit of `concept.html`, `order-history`, `tracking` and `delivery` against this document, worked through end to end. Every pair, size, radius and asset below was measured in the browser, before and after. **The shadow work was checked and deliberately not touched:** no card on any of the four surfaces casts a shadow, and none was added back.
  - **Type scale — the document was wrong, not the build.** §03 declared eight steps (32/24/19/16/14/13/12/10.5) and the product used six sizes that were not among them. Rather than force screens onto a scale invented before them, the scale is rewritten to **ten steps that are all in use** (§5), and only two genuine strays were snapped because neither had a role of its own: alert body **13.5 → 13** and the mono hint **11.5 → 11**. `display` 22 and `number` 20 are kept apart on purpose — a screen title and a figure inside a card, never on the same surface.
  - **Icon stroke — one rule, no exception.** §0.2 now says explicitly that `1.7` is declared once on the 24-grid and the **rendered line follows the box** (0.85 at 12px, 2.27 at 32px), which is how SF Symbols scales weight and is optically correct. rev 38's compensated upload glyph was the only place holding a constant rendered 1.70, so the rule existed in exactly one place; the compensation is reverted and the glyph moved to **32px — the size `.success-mark` already uses on that same page**. §0.2's "two implementations" paragraph also still said 1.75 twice, a leftover rev 27 missed.
  - **Disabled is built, not dimmed.** `.btn:disabled` was a blanket `opacity:.42`, which took charcoal-on-green from **9.46:1 to 2.35:1** — WCAG 1.4.3 exempts inactive controls, so not a failure, but `.42` had no pair and the result was never measured. §4 gains `--disabled-bg` / `--disabled-ink`, which are the secondary button's own colours: **slate on `#D6D2C9` = 4.77:1**, already in the table. concept.html's contrast failures: **2 → 0**.
  - **Three shapes and two colours with no pair, all on the page that defines them.** Radii **14** (table wrap, contrast cards, component stages, elevation swatches), **9** (drone sheet) and **3** (spacing rulers) are gone — 14 → `--r-card`, 9 → `--r-input`, and the rulers to a square, because a measuring block is a rectangle. `rgba(255,255,255,.92)` on the model pill — the only white in a palette that has no white — is now `--card`. The on-photo chip's `rgba(0,0,0,.30)`, the only black among shadows that are otherwise the warm `20,18,15`, is named **`--sh-onphoto`** and warmed; §5 records it as the single exception to flat surfaces, for a chip over pixels it cannot predict. Two hairline insets doing one job (`.07` and `.08`) became one.
  - **One control, one padding.** The green primary was `0 12px` on `order-history` (it carries `.btn-sm`) and `0 16px` on `tracking` and `delivery`. Now 16 everywhere; the button is full-width in the card, so only the label inset moves.
  - **A ring against a ground that no longer exists.** `.oh-ind`'s 2px ring was `--media`, chosen when the drone sat in a `--media` well; rev 32 removed that well and the ring had been sitting on `--card` at 1.08:1 ever since. It is now `--card` — invisible by construction rather than by luck.
  - **Assets: serve the size the box draws, keep the master.** `people/operator.png` was 595 × 702 for a 117px panel (**2.54×** past a 2× budget) and `scenes/delivered-at-door.jpg` 1100 × 880 for a 326px well (**1.69×**). Both masters stay in the repo; the pages now reference `thumbs/` copies at **300 × 354** (683 → 125 KB) and **652 × 522** (305 → 129 KB). Measured after: overdraw **1.28×** and **1.00×**. **Neither picture changes** — same crop, same framing, same `cover`, same `50% 50%`, same 13px radius, aspect held to four decimals; the operator panel still measures 117 × 138.1 flush at 0.0 / 0.0.
  - **Verified false positives, recorded so they are not re-found:** the EN/UA toggle measures 38.5 × 28 in a browser holding a cached `_wf-shell.js` — the file and `origin/gh-pages` both carry 44 × 44; `naturalWidth 0` on concept.html's fleet is `loading=lazy` below the fold; Arial in the font census appears only on `<svg>`, `<path>` and icon-only buttons with no text; the `�` in headings was a console, not the file (charset declared, 49 em-dashes decode clean, zero U+FFFD); `.badge`'s two grounds and `.tl-dot`'s three are documented states, not drift.
  - **After:** zero text-contrast failures on all four surfaces, radii on concept.html reduced to the six of the ramp plus circles, no black or white left outside the palette, and both shared `<style>` blocks byte-identical — delivery **12 023 chars ×3**, tracking **12 959 ×4**.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 40)** — Designer note on `delivery-loading`: *"put the icon in my salad shades."* The waiting mark leaves the neutral family. It was the `--media` well with a `--slate` clock, on the reasoning recorded at the time — *nothing has arrived yet, so nothing is green*; the designer's call replaces that reasoning. It now takes the **`--green-wash` well with the `--ink` glyph, 14.75:1** — the same recipe the delivered mark uses, because it is the only green tint this language has. **A paler step of its own was drawn and measured first, and it does not exist.** `--green-wash` is already only **1.085:1** against the page; every mix paler than it lands at **1.03–1.05 against the page** and **1.03–1.07 against the wash** — a disc nobody can see, beside a state nobody can tell apart. Recorded here so the idea is not proposed again as new. So waiting and delivered share one well and the difference sits where this language already puts it: **the glyph** — clock against check — and the screen title. WCAG 1.4.1 is satisfied by shape and text, not by colour, and the two states are on different screens in any case. **The green budget is comfortable, measured on the loading screen: zero solid-green elements** (its action is the grey secondary) and two tints, this mark and the active tab — so nothing on the screen competes with it. Geometry untouched: 64px disc, 32px glyph, rendered stroke **2.27**, the same value `.success-mark` and the upload mark already carry on this page family. The `.waiting` override is deleted rather than restated, so one component keeps one declaration; the class stays in the markup as the semantic hook. `<style>` blocks re-verified byte-identical at **12 708 chars ×3**. **If the two states should read further apart**, the lever inside the rules is a green *ring* on the delivered mark — a fill shape, which §0.2 allows, unlike a green stroke — not a second tint.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 40)** — Designer note on `concept.html` §01: **the card variants become the real cards.** The four invented demos — Order · live, Operator, Parcel, Status — are gone, and in their place stand four components copied out of the prototype: `order-history`'s **live order** and **delivered order**, `tracking`'s **operator card**, and `delivery`'s **details card**. The markup is unchanged, only the hrefs are re-pointed at `../wireframes/`; the rules are the same values those files carry, scoped under `.prodcards` so the page's own demo classes cannot reach into a ported card and it cannot reach out. Anything already defined on this page and identical in both places — `.nd`, `.sbi`, `.pill` — is deliberately not repeated, because the wireframes took those values from here in the first place. **Shown at production size, and verified as such:** the column is a fixed **341px**, the width the phone frame gives a card at 375 (375 − 2×1 border − 2×16 `main` padding), and after restating the frame's own **15px / 1.4** leading on the ported cards — this page runs 16px / 1.55, and inherited leading is what decides a card's height — the four measure **388.7 / 263.6 / 138.1 / 147.0px**, matching the same cards measured inside the prototype to the tenth of a pixel. Below 420px the grid gives up the fixed column rather than giving the page a horizontal scroll. Two rules died with the demos and were removed rather than left to rot: `.cardgrid` and `.card-cap`. **What this costs, stated plainly:** §01 now holds a *copy* of production markup, and a copy drifts. These cards are a snapshot of the wireframes as of 2026-08-01, not a live mirror — so a change to one of those cards has to be walked into §01 in the same commit, the same discipline rev 30 wrote down for skeletons: the spec expires when the thing it specifies changes.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 41)** — Designer note on `concept.html` §06: **the last demo card goes too.** The "Card · A-4" cell in *Three live DRON components* held a hand-built card — icon well, `#DR-4821`, a `Live` pill, `~14 min to arrival` — and now holds the **delivered order card from `order-history.html`**, the same port rev 40 made in §01. What made that reusable: the scope was split, so `.prodcards` keeps only §01's grid and the component rules moved to **`.prod`**, a class any container can wear — §01 is `class="prodcards prod"`, the §06 stage is `class="stage prod"`. The card renders at the stage's **311px** rather than the frame's 341, and its height is unchanged at **263.6px**; 311 sits inside the width the card already takes on real devices (321px on a 360px phone), and the title still holds one line — measured, not assumed. The cell's attribute line and note were rewritten to describe what is on the stage now instead of what used to be. **Two things this exposed, both worth keeping.** **(a)** The first splice cut at an 8-space `</div>` and matched the *indentation* of a nested 12-space one, which left the old card's `.foot` block orphaned below the new stage. It rendered — the browser recovered the broken nesting silently — and was caught by screenshotting the cell, not by reading the diff. Splices into hand-indented HTML get verified by rendering. **(b)** With the demos gone, `.foot`, `.grow`, `.pill`, `.rt`, `.lbls`, `.sg`, `.stat3`, `.media` and `.dot` have no markup left on the page. They are **kept**, unlike `.cardgrid` and `.card-cap` which rev 40 deleted: those two were layout scaffolding for the demos, while these are the language's own component definitions, and this document cites them by class — "concept `.stat3` blend", "concept `.pill.mut`", "concept `.foot` (`.big` price, `.cap` label)". Deleting a rule the wireframes quote as their source would delete the source.
- **2026-08-01 (rev 42)** — **`DESIGN.md` written at the repo root from the ten painted product pages, and this document corrected to follow them.** The direction of truth is stated once and applied throughout: the wireframes are hand-edited by the designer, so they are the design; where this document disagreed with them, this document was wrong. Every value below was measured by computed style in a browser, not read off the CSS.
  - **Type — the leading column and the face column were both fiction.** The ten *sizes* survived contact with the screens; nothing else in the table did. Leading is **1.4 by inheritance** from `_wireframe.css`, and the build declares only five departures, each with a box-level reason (`number` 1.0, `heading` / `meta` 1.2, `body` 1.3, and the two paragraph steps at 1.5 / 1.55). The old per-token leadings — 1.1 on display, 1.2 on title / micro / micro-sm, 1.5 on body — were drawn nowhere. **There is no Display/Text split:** one stack is declared once and no painted page declares a second UI family; `-apple-system` applies SF's own optical sizing, so the split happens by size on Apple and does not exist off it. And `micro` is **not** mono-only — 11px runs mono-600 for structure labels and UI-stack 600/400 for tab labels and the fee caption.
  - **The green budget was counted in the wrong unit.** G-5 promised *"≈ one green element per screen"*; the screens carry **three / two / one** solid-green elements. What holds — exactly — is the pixel clause G-5 always had beside it: solid green is **4.43% / 5.09% / 4.71%** of the frame, and the primary CTA is **81–100%** of that. So the rule becomes **one green *control* per screen, solid green ≤ ~5% of the frame**, with status marks permitted (400px² and 484px², under 6% of the screen's green) and the tint not counted at all, being 1.085:1 against the page. The eye weighs area, not marks.
  - **`--sh-onphoto` is a spec-page token.** §5 read as though the product spends two shadows. It spends one: `--sh-sm` under the green primary. `--sh-onphoto`, `var(--sh-card)` and `var(--sh-raised)` each appear **0 times** across the ten pages.
  - **The §4 seed is marked superseded value by value**, with a table of what the product actually runs — card radius 12 → 16, input 8 → 10, card padding 24 → 16/20/32, section gap 64 → never drawn, `--ok-bg` retuned to `#E7F0D2`. The seed is kept rather than rewritten because it records where the palette came from; deleting it would delete the provenance. Also recorded: `--disabled-bg` / `--disabled-ink` are declared on **no** painted page — the same pair (`#D6D2C9` + `#5A5751`, **4.77:1**) arrives through the token remap, where `_wireframe.css`'s `.btn:disabled` reads `--wf-fill` / `--wf-muted`.
  - **Icon sizes corrected to the eight the build draws** — `12 · 13 · 14 · 17 · 20 · 22 · 26 · 32`, of the declared `16 / 20 / 24 / 28` only 20 was ever used. Each size is half to three-fifths of its container, which is what evens the optical weight; the resulting **rendered** stroke range is 0.85 – 2.27, exactly what §0.2 already declares, so the one-declared-number rule is untouched.
  - **One defect found and fixed in the wireframes, at the designer's call:** the selected tab label on `order-history-empty` and `order-history-loading` was `--green-ink #3F5B18` (**7.09:1**) while the other seven painted pages carried `--ink #1A1A1A` (**14.75:1**) — a leftover of the token rev 27 deleted. Both pages now match the seven. The **focus ring** on those two pages keeps `--green-ink` by the designer's decision, both values clearing WCAG 2.4.11 (7.09:1 and 15.99:1); recorded here so the difference is a choice rather than a survivor.
  - **Verified, and left alone:** the `<style>` byte-identity contract holds — `tracking` ×4 at 13 224 chars, `delivery` ×3 at 13 042; one icon stroke, `1.7`, across all 98 SVGs on all ten pages; no `#FFFFFF` and no `#000000` on any product surface; zero text-contrast failures, the lowest pair being 4.77:1.
  - **Two §0 conflicts were raised for the designer and both were settled the same day.** **(a) Flat wins, and §0 was amended to say so** — the CoffeeTech and Fauna "Take" lines and A-1's technique all promised a soft shadow, and the build has none. Her reason is a preference and is recorded as one: *she likes flat surfaces*. §0 now reads borderless and shadowless, with the 1.11:1 warm tone step and the 16px radius named as what does the separating. **(b) The terracotta reading is dropped** — the danger family on a warm page is not the cream-and-terracotta anti-reference in her judgement, and no exception clause was added, because there is nothing to except.
  - **Radii, amended by the designer in the same pass:** the closed six govern anything the eye reads as an object — a surface, a control, a well, a chip — and **below that, any radius on the 2 / 4 / 8 scale is permissible** for an internal detail such as a skeleton bar. The closed-six rule had been written for shapes and was being applied to a 13px placeholder, which made a rule out of something nobody was choosing.
  - **`order-history`'s `.btn-sm` keeps `height:44px`** rather than the `min-height` §5 asks for. Measured, it renders at exactly 44.0px, so the HIG target is met and that is what governs; the fixed-height mechanism is left as recorded rather than corrected.
- **2026-08-02 (rev 43)** — Designer decisions taken off the back of the `ui/inventory` component audit, which counted every component across all **40 screens** (84 files with their state pages collapsed) rather than only the three painted ones. Both calls are stated as rules, not as fixes to one component.
  - **A surface is a card, not a frame.** The grayscale base carries **24 bordered surfaces**, and the painted set drops `border` to `0` fourteen times. The designer's call, in her words: *a wireframe where everything sits in frames cannot be compared with UI* — so a border does not survive the move into the Studio layer. A painted surface takes `--card #ECE9E4` at the 1.11:1 tone step with `--r-card` 16px, and rows inside it are separated by their own `--line` hairline at 1.21:1, exactly as `.kv` already does. **Consequence for `details.dropdown`:** on a card surface with hairline rows it *becomes* the row panel that opens — one component rather than two — and the same reading applies to every bordered block still waiting in grayscale.
  - **A selected row carries a checkmark, per HIG.** A settings-style list marks the chosen row with a check, not with a word. This replaces the `.tag` reading *"current"* on the mode switcher, and it is the standing answer for anything that picks one of many — the choice cards on `role-select` and `support`, and any settings list built later. The pair needs nothing new: **`--ink` on `--card` = 14.37:1**, and the glyph is the same check the walked progress node and the delivered status mark already draw. Green is not involved — the accent is never an icon stroke, and a selection mark is not the control the screen is steering toward.
  - **Recorded, not decided: nothing in the product floats.** Counted across the 40 screens — no modal, sheet, popover, dialog, scrim or backdrop, and not one `position:fixed`. `details.dropdown` opens **in flow** and pushes the page down: it is a disclosure, not an overlay, and a note earlier in this audit that called it one was wrong. So the flat-surface rule has never had to answer for a floating surface and **`--sh-onphoto` is still spent nowhere**. The designer's position for when it arrives: *a floating surface will appear in a modal, popover, drawer or dropdown menu, and there it is appropriate* — the exception has a home waiting for it rather than being a loophole.
  - **Left open for the designer: the chevron.** It is `.chev`, a typed `›` character at 22px on `role-select` / `support` / `time-slot`, and a typed `▾` inside the disclosure summary — so it is type, not an icon: off the 24-grid, no 1.7 stroke, scaling with the font rather than with its box. §0.2 specifies it (`chevron-right` / `chevron.right`) and no painted screen draws it. Replacing it with the real glyph is not in question; what is open is whether the down-chevron **rotates** when a disclosure opens, which would be the first state transition in the system that is not a button press — the four existing motion loops all carry information.
- **2026-08-02 (rev 44)** — **The chevron rotates.** Designer's call on the question rev 43 left open: when a disclosure opens, the mark turns. What that settles, and what it deliberately does not:
  - **It is 180°, not 90°.** The closed state already points **down** (`▾`), so the open state points **up** — the mark says which way the block is about to move, and there is no third position for it to take. A 90° reading would only make sense if the closed state were the right-pointing `›`, which is the *navigation* chevron on `role-select` / `support` / `time-slot`, a different job on a different row.
  - **It joins the button's transition, it does not become a fifth loop.** The four named animations — `oh-ping` 1.8s, `skPulse` 1.4s, `skUp` 1.6s and the button's own `.15s / .12s` — divide into loops that carry information and a transition that answers a touch. A chevron turning on open is the second kind: it is feedback for an action the user just took, so it takes the button family's timing (`transform .12s`) rather than an easing of its own. **The motion inventory stays at four entries**; this is a new use of one, not a new one.
  - **Reduced motion: the glyph still turns, it just does not travel.** `prefers-reduced-motion` switches the transition off, not the state — the open block must still point up, because the direction is information and the animation is only how it gets there. This is the same distinction rev 38 drew on the upload arrow, which rests **visible** so a dropped animation leaves a legible icon.
  - **Not settled here, and not blocking:** the exact easing. The button uses the browser default and the loops use `cubic-bezier(.22,.61,.36,1)`; whichever the chevron takes should be the button's, since that is the family it joined. Confirm when the component is drawn.
  - **Still true, and unchanged by this:** the chevron is type today, not an icon — a `›` character at 22px and a `▾` in the summary. Replacing it with the real 24-grid glyph at `stroke-width 1.7` was never in question and remains the first step; the rotation is what happens to it afterwards.
- **2026-08-02 (rev 45)** — **A field has no border.** Designer's call on the first and largest of the open questions, and it reaches further than the field itself.
  - **The reading that settled it.** WCAG 1.4.11 asks for 3:1 from *"visual information **required to identify** a component"* — it governs what is drawn, and **nowhere requires that a boundary exist**. So a borderless field identified by its label, its placeholder and its row separator has nothing to measure and cannot fail. What *does* fail is the opposite: the grayscale `.control` draws `border:1px solid var(--wf-border-2)`, which resolves to `--line` in the painted frame — **1.21:1**, a boundary that is present, that identifies the control, and that is under 3:1. **The failure was never the missing border; it was the one already drawn.** It does not survive painting.
  - **HIG agrees, and got there first.** The native iOS form pattern is a grouped row: label, value, separator, no box — `UITextField.borderStyle = .none`, and SwiftUI's `Form` renders `TextField` as a plain row. HIG asks a text field for a 44pt height, a placeholder where the label is not self-evident, and a visible focus state. It asks for no boundary at all. So the choice is the platform convention rather than a concession to it.
  - **The structural consequence: there are no standalone fields.** A borderless field on the page would be invisible, so **every field lives inside a row panel** — which makes the field *the row panel that accepts typing*, exactly as rev 43 made the disclosure *the row panel that opens*. That is the third component folding into `.kv`, and the pattern is now worth naming: the row panel is this system's universal container for a fact, whether the fact is read, opened or typed.
  - **Measured, and passing with room.** Typed value `--ink` on `--card` **14.37:1**; label and placeholder `--slate` on `--card` **5.95:1** — both past the 4.5:1 of 1.4.3, placeholders included. Focus is unchanged and needs nothing new: the painted ring, `2.5px --ink`, is **14.37:1** on a card and **15.99:1** on the page. Error text `--danger-ink` is **6.14:1** on a card and **6.83:1** on the page, and must be carried by words or a glyph, never by a red boundary alone (1.4.1).
  - **One exception, and it is also HIG's:** the **search field** is not in a grouped list, so it keeps a ground — a filled rounded rect on the page, `--media` at **`--r-input` 10px**, no border, which is what the iOS search bar is. Value `--ink` **13.33:1**, placeholder `--slate` **5.51:1**. This finally gives `--r-input` its one home: the token has been declared since §4 and drawn nowhere.
  - **The stated fallback was not needed, and would not have worked.** The instruction was: if it fails, give the field a white ground and the darkest text. The condition never triggered — but for the record, white is not available for two reasons. The palette has none by rule, and more usefully, **it would not do the job**: `#FFFFFF` against the `#F7F5F2` page is a **1.09:1** step, *smaller* than the card's own 1.11:1, so a white field would be less visible than the card it sits on, not more. The contrast the instinct was reaching for is already spent — the page is nearly white to begin with.
- **2026-08-02 (rev 46)** — **Shape says how many.** Designer's call, made while reading the built `withdraw` screen: a **radio is a circle**, a **checkbox is a square**. Circle = a set of mutually exclusive options, exactly one on. Square = independent on/off, several at a time. What it settles, what it supersedes, and the defect it names:
  - **It is `HIG · Radio Buttons / Checkboxes` verbatim** — "use radio buttons to let people choose one item from a set of mutually exclusive options; use checkboxes for independent on/off values". The rule was not derived from the guideline; the designer stated it and the guideline agrees, which is worth recording as the direction it happened in.
  - **iOS diverges, and is deliberately not followed here.** iOS has no radio control at all: one-of-many in a grouped list is marked with a trailing checkmark and nothing else. DRON is **mobile web rendered in a browser**, with a desktop surface held to WCAG 2.1 AA — so the control a user meets is a web control, and the circle/square convention is what they read. This is a **conforming departure from the iOS pattern**, recorded as a decision rather than filed as a defect.
  - **It supersedes rev 43 in scope, not in substance.** Rev 43 wrote *"a selected row carries a checkmark — governs every one-of-many list"*. That sentence was too wide. The checkmark keeps the **disclosure row**, which is a menu of routes and not a question being asked; it gives way to the circle wherever a **form** asks the question. Rev 43's measured pair, `--ink` on `--card` at **14.37:1**, is unchanged where it still applies.
  - **The defect it names, on two built screens.** `payment` and `withdraw` hold `<input type="radio">` inside `.check-box` — a **square** with `border-radius:6px`, the same box `operator-profile-setup` uses for a multi-select. The only visual difference is the glyph inside: a 4px bullet against a 4px check. That is **`WCAG 3.2.4 Consistent Identification` (AA)** — components with the same functionality must be identified consistently, and the inverse holds too: one shape cannot carry two functions. The programmatic side is already correct (`type="radio"` in a `name` group), so this is a visual failure, not a semantic one.
  - **The correct circle already exists in the product.** `listings-filters` draws it: 20px, `border-radius:999px`, checked adds a filled dot at `inset:4px`. Nothing has to be invented — it has to be applied. The kit size moves to **24px** so the circle and the square share the row's rhythm.
  - **An unchecked outline is `--slate`, not `--line`.** A box that is empty is identified by its boundary alone, so `WCAG 1.4.11` applies at **3:1**. The grayscale `1px --line` resolves to `#D9D5CE` on `--card` — **1.21:1**, failing. `--slate` `#5A5751` on `--card` is **5.95:1**. This is the **same 1.21:1** rev 45 found under the form field, and the answers differ because the components do: a field's border was removable, because HIG's form row has none; a checkbox **is** its box, so it is darkened instead of dropped.
  - **The checked fill stays open, and it is a budget question, not a contrast one.** Two pairs, both conforming: `--green` + `--on-green` = **9.46:1**, which repeats `.tl-dot.done` — already painted exactly that way — and `--ink` + `--card` = **14.37:1**, which is what the grayscale build holds. Both clear 1.4.11's 3:1 with room. The tension is §5's **one green control per screen**: on `payment` that is already the "Pay" button, so a green radio beside it would be the second. Recorded as `ui/inventory.md` §4 Q1.
  - **The choice card was misfiled and is corrected.** `.choice-card` on `role-select` and `support` is an `<a href>` that leaves the screen — navigation, not a picker. It has no selected state, so the open question rev 43 left behind ("does the card change behind the check?") is **closed as wrongly framed**, not answered. The component needs a card's set: `--card`, `--r-card` 16, a 44pt row, a chevron and a keyboard-visible focus ring.
  - **A separate `WCAG 4.1.2` gap, found in the same count and not fixed here.** `job-checklist`, `operator-profile-setup` and `order-setup` draw the box as a static `<span aria-hidden="true">` with **no `<input>` at all** — no role, no state, nothing for assistive technology. On `job-checklist` that is defensible: it is a progress readout, not a control. On the other two it is a real defect.
  - **The wireframes are not being changed for any of this.** The designer's instruction stands — nothing in `wireframes/` is touched. The rules are recorded in `ui/inventory.md` and applied when those screens are painted.
- **2026-08-03 (rev 47)** — **Checked is green.** The designer's call, made looking at the kit specimens side by side: a checked checkbox and a selected radio are a **`--green` fill behind an `--on-green` glyph** — 9.46:1, the exact pair the walked progress node paints, and the icon rule's own sentence: *"active / done is a green shape behind a charcoal glyph."* The kit's first draft had charcoal, reasoned from §5's one-green-control budget; the correction resolves that tension by **reading**, not by exception: a checked box is a **status mark**, like the node and the live disc — it reports a state, it is not the screen's action — and §5 already lets status marks be solid green inside its ≤ ~5% measure. The screen's one green *control* stays the primary CTA. What does not change: the unchecked outline stays `--slate` (1.4.11 at 5.95:1), the shape still says how many (rev 46), and nothing else inside card UI may take the accent — this reading covers marks that report state, never surfaces or actions.
- **2026-08-12 (rev 48)** — **The green that may speak: `--green-ink #4F6B22`.** Two of the designer's calls converged on the same missing rung. On `account`'s mode picker she asked for the current row's mark to be *"just a green checkmark, no background — this is neither a checkbox nor a radio"* — iOS's own current-item-in-a-list mark, which rev 46 had already reserved for the disclosure row. And on `listings-filtered` she asked for a *text button* in the primary green (pending her confirmation on the preview). Both want green **standing alone** — as a lone glyph, as a word — and Signal Green cannot do it: **1.69:1** on the page, **1.52:1** on the card, against `WCAG 1.4.3`'s 4.5:1 for text and `1.4.11`'s 3:1 for a sole state indicator. The answer is not an exception but a **new rung cut from the same hue**: `#4F6B22`, the lightest green of the family that passes both grounds — **5.58:1** on the page, **5.01:1** on the card. It completes the pair grammar the palette already keeps (`--warn`/`--warn-ink`, `--danger`/`--danger-ink`) and it **narrows nothing**: `--green` itself remains fill-only, never text, never a stroke. First home: the disclosure's current-row checkmark (`account`). The §5 budget is untouched — an ink is not a fill and joins no green-area count.
- **2026-08-12 (rev 49)** — **The text button lands: `.dr-btn--text` in `--green-ink`.** The confirmation rev 48 was waiting on arrived — the designer's word on the built page: *a text button in the primary green, `#9bcf4a`*. The literal hex stays impossible as text (**1.69:1** on the page against `WCAG 1.4.3`'s 4.5:1 — the number the fill-only rule was written on), so the ask lands on the rung rev 48 already cut for exactly this: rest `--green-ink` **5.58:1** on the page, **5.01:1** on the card; hover and press darken to `--ink` and take the kit's 1px dip — the tertiary's own grammar, so the two text actions differ in ink and in nothing else; disabled and the focus ring are the universal ones, inherited, zero new CSS beyond the pair. Spent first on `listings-filtered`'s **Clear all**, which closes that control's day-long walk: slate tertiary → green primary (morning) → slate tertiary (afternoon) → green text (now, the standing state; each turn logged in `ui/inventory.md`). The §5 budget is untouched — an ink is not a fill, so the screen's one-green-control slot stays empty and solid green on the frame remains the status marks alone. `DESIGN.md`'s palette note, contrast table, buttons and the don'ts are corrected to carry the variant; the proposal page `preview-text-button.html` has served its purpose and awaits the designer's word to be deleted.
- **2026-08-12 (rev 50)** — **The mode checkmark returns to Signal Green.** The designer saw the `--green-ink` mark in place and called the brand hex: *«`#9BCF4A`, this colour»* — with the number already on the table from rev 48: **1.52:1** on the card, against `1.4.11`'s 3:1. Recorded as an **accepted below-3:1 non-text mark**, not corrected, on the line of precedent the system already holds — the live status disc (1.52:1) and the pager's active dot (1.69:1): colour is not the carrier of the state; the mark's *presence* is (one row holds a checkmark, the others hold nothing), and `aria-current="true"` speaks it programmatically (`4.1.2`). Entered into `DESIGN.md`'s below-3:1 table with its stated reason, beside the disc it echoes. The fill-only rule gains its **one recorded exception** and is otherwise unchanged; `--green-ink` loses the disclosure home the day it gained it and keeps the text button (rev 49) as its one spend. A rejected idea is not re-proposed: the ink-rung checkmark was tried, seen, and set aside by the person the system belongs to.
- **2026-08-13 (rev 51)** — **The exit is a chip: All leads the applied row, Clear all comes off.** The designer's call on the built page: *delete the Clear all button, add an "All" chip first instead*. The applied-filter row on `listings-filtered` now opens with an **All** chip that exits to the full list — the job Clear all carried — and the text button goes. The chip is **`.dr-fchip--all`**: the fchip's own pill, but on the secondary button's ground, because an exit is an action, not an applied filter, and must not wear the applied `--green-wash`; `--ink` on `--btn2` is the measured **11.54:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓). No ✕ — nothing to remove — symmetric 12px padding, and its floor is the sibling chip's content box (the 18px ✕ plus the two 5s = **28px**), so the row stands level. Targets: 28px clears `WCAG 2.5.8`'s 24px AA floor and sits under `HIG`'s 44pt exactly as the row's built chips do — the departure the row already carries, recorded again with the grouptab's `--pill-bleed` hit-area extension named as the open option. Palette consequence: rev 49's Clear all was `--green-ink`'s one spend, so `.dr-btn--text` stays in the kit **declared and spent nowhere** — beside `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto` on the bench of rungs holding for a home; the rung itself and the fill-only rule do not move. The control's full walk — green primary → slate tertiary → green text → replaced by the All chip — stays logged in `ui/inventory.md`, each turn on the designer's word. `DESIGN.md`'s palette note, contrast table and buttons are corrected to match the frame.
- **2026-08-13 (rev 52)** — **The All chip takes the ✕.** The designer's feedback on the built row: the leading chip gets the same close control its siblings carry — one mark, the row over. Rev 51's «no ✕ — nothing to remove» is overturned on her word and logged, not overwritten: the chip *is* the remove-them-all, and it now says so the way the siblings say it — the fchip's own `.dr-fchip__x`, a `--green` pill behind an `--on-green` cross, **9.46:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓), a status-mark spend. The ground does not move: an exit is an action, so the chip keeps `--btn2` (`--ink` at **11.54:1**) and still refuses the applied `--green-wash`. One pair is new and measured before use — the green pill's edge on `--btn2` is **1.22:1**, beside **1.56:1** on the siblings' wash; on both grounds the edge is not the carrier, the charcoal cross at 9.46:1 is, the reading the component was settled on (2026-08-03). Geometry: the 18px pill returns to the content box, so `.dr-fchip--all` sheds its `min-height` floor and its symmetric-padding override — the modifier reduces to the ground swap alone, and the chip stands the sibling's own **28px** (`WCAG 2.5.8` ✓ at ≥ 24px; under `HIG`'s 44pt, the row's recorded departure, unchanged). Budget: the All ✕ adds one 18px pill, **324 px²**; with every ✕ pill and the count badge the frame's solid green stays **under 0.5%** against the ~5% ceiling — status marks all, the one-green-control slot empty. (The pill count rides the applied filters — a same-day parallel pass took the sort chip off as this landed, so the build measures three pills + the badge, **1 296 px² ≈ 0.4%**.)
- **2026-08-13 (rev 53)** — **The action-bar Total line comes off: the price is stated once, and the bar is the button.** The designer's feedback on the built `order-review` — *remove the ₴180 total from the bottom bar throughout the project* — and three bars carried it: `order-review` (`Total · ₴180` above `Pay ₴180`), `order-review-loading` (`Total · …` above the transient `Continue`), and `order-setup` (`Total · locked, no extra fees · ₴180` above `Continue to review`). All three drop the `.dr-price` line and the bar reduces to its block CTA. This **supersedes half of the 2026-08-03 Total ruling** — "in both places the fact appears" — while keeping the other half exactly as sized: the breakdown's last row remains the Total's one home, label 15/600 and figure at the money step 20/700 tabular, and the CTA's own verb still names the amount on the tap. **Voice P3 read against the change, plainly:** the full locked price appears before the commitment — on `order-review` the breakdown states ₴90 + ₴70 + ₴20 = **₴180**, the hint says "This is the final price. Nothing is added at checkout.", and the button says `Pay ₴180`; what changes is that **`order-setup` now states no price at all**, so the locked number arrives one tap later, on the review screen, which is where the core flow already puts "See price" (Choose service → Enter details → *See price* → Pay). RJ-C3's proof therefore lives on one screen instead of leaking across two — `order-setup`'s sidebar and comment annotations are corrected in the same pass. **Conformance:** the removed line was static text restating a fact stated elsewhere — not a control (`HIG` asks nothing of it; no target is lost) and not a sole carrier of information (`WCAG 1.4.3` loses no pair that said anything unique). **Not swept, on purpose:** `payment`'s bar line (`Pay with Apple Pay · ₴180`) and `time-slot`'s (`Selected · Wed 2 Jul, 09:00 · ₴800` / `No slot selected`) share the `.dr-price` markup but are a method line and a selection summary, not totals — whether they follow the Total off the bar is the designer's word, held open. Kit consequence: **`.dr-price--total` is declared and spent nowhere** — it joins `.dr-btn--text`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto` on the bench of rungs holding for a home; `.dr-price` itself keeps its three non-total spends. `microcopy.md`'s master table shed the three label strings first; `ui/inventory.md`'s Total row is narrowed and its log carries the same entry.
- **2026-08-13 (rev 54)** — **The counter row comes off, and the error mark gets its glyph.** Two designer calls on the built `listings-error`, both landing on the whole client list — one screen, one structure, so all five states move together (`listings`, `-filtered`, `-empty`, `-error`, `-loading`). **(a)** *«Remove this and sorting — I have filters instead»* on the results counter: the sorting half fell in the same day's sort-removal pass (`_screens.md`: the Price dimension was a sort by another name); this call takes the row itself. The count was the list restating its own length — three cards above «3 services» — and the state rows restated the block beside them («Couldn't load» above *Couldn't load services*). No contrast pair moves; nothing new is drawn. `.dr-counter` stays declared in `ui/kit.css` and is now applied to nothing — on the bench beside `.dr-btn--text` and `--sh-onphoto`. **One consequence recorded, not hidden:** the loading state's `role="status"` line went with the row, so `listings-loading` now names what loads nowhere on the frame (voice.md › loading; `WCAG 4.1.3` status messages) — the skeletons alone carry the state, and whether the name should return somewhere is the designer's open call. **(b)** *«Add alert icon»* on the `ERROR` mark: the last painted error state still wearing the wireframe's uppercase placeholder joins `delivery-error` and `tracking-error` — `dr-mark--danger`, the alert triangle drawn in the one icon system (24-grid, `stroke-width:1.7`, `currentColor`, the filled `r=.9` dot the Help glyph already carries), `--danger-ink` on `--danger-wash` at **5.49:1** (`WCAG 1.4.11` ✓ at ≥ 3:1). The mark is `aria-hidden` — the `role="alert"` text beside it carries the message (`1.1.1` ✓), exactly as on its two siblings. Strings retired in `microcopy.md` in the same pass. *(Housekeeping: this entry was written beside a second session working the same feedback queue — the sort-removal pass and revs 51–53 are its work; if the numbering collides, the texts are intact and only the labels clash, per the rev-32 precedent.)*
- **2026-08-15 (rev 55)** — **The between-cards gap is chosen by what surrounds the list, and the 2026-08-12 rule is finally swept.** The designer's note on the built `listings-filtered`, against the two-card service list: *«fix spaces between cards through all the project — it should be close, according to the proximity rule»*. **What was built:** `ul.dr-list` at `--sp-list` **28px**, inside a `.dr-main` that separates whole zones by `--sp-screen` **16px** — measured on the frame, the list stood 341 × **252** = 112 + **28** + 112. So the two cards of one list sat *further apart than the applied-filter row sat from the list itself*: siblings further apart than strangers, proximity grouping backwards, and the list stopped reading as one object. **This was not a new finding.** `ui/inventory.md`'s List-container row already carries the rule and its measurement, written 2026-08-12 when the same defect was caught on `support` — but it was applied to `support` alone and never swept, so five further lists met the stated condition and were still at 28. This pass closes that gap; no new value, no new token, the existing `.dr-list--snug` rung. **Moved to `--snug` 12 — the list is one zone among several:** `listings`, `listings-filtered`, `listings-loading`, `inspection-report`, `inspection-report-loading`. **Left at 28, and correctly so — the list *is* the screen, with nothing above or below it to be confused with:** `order-history`, `order-history-loading`, `time-slot`. After: between cards **12**, between zones **16**, so the step reads 4:3 in the right direction; `listings-filtered`'s list closes 252 → **236**, `listings`' three-card list 392 → **360**. **The old blanket wording is superseded, deliberately and on the record.** §5 and `DESIGN.md` said *«the gap between two cards is always greater than any interval inside one»* — at 12 the list gap now equals `.dr-card--row`'s own 12 and sits under the card's 20 inset, so read literally the law breaks. It does not, because it was stated one level too coarsely: a card here has a **drawn** boundary — `--card` on `--page` at a real **1.11:1** step plus the 16px `--r-card` — so its interior is bounded by tone and radius, not by whitespace, and whitespace is free to do the *between-cards* job at a value the interior no longer needs to beat. The law holds per level, which is how `inventory.md` had already restated it: 12 clears the 4px text rhythm inside a card and stays under the 16 between zones. Where no drawn zone competes, 28 remains right and still beats the 20 inset. **Conformance:** whitespace only — no colour pair moves (`WCAG 1.4.3` unaffected), no control shrinks (`HIG · 44pt` and `WCAG 2.5.8` unaffected: the tap target is the whole card, whose height is untouched at 112px), and nothing is added or removed from the DOM. Gestalt proximity is not a WCAG criterion and is not claimed as one — this is `concept.md` §5's own rhythm, corrected against itself. **No-reflow contract kept:** both `-loading` skeletons step with their loaded pages in the same commit, so `listings`/`listings-loading` and `inspection-report`/`inspection-report-loading` stay the same height at the swap. **The narrow tier, checked and left alone:** `--sp-list` steps to **22px** in `:root` under 389px — a global step, not the `order-history`-only one `DESIGN.md` implies — so the three lists still on `--sp-list` step with it. `--sp-snug` does not step, so the five moved lists hold **12 at both tiers**, which is right: 12 is already at the floor the narrow tier was reaching for, and stepping it further would collide with the card's own 4px rhythm. Both `-loading` pairs therefore stay matched at both widths, not just at 375. **Open, for the designer's word:** whether the three single-zone lists (`order-history`, `order-history-loading`, `time-slot`) should also come in. They conform to the rule as written — nothing competes with them on those screens — so this pass left them; the note said *«through all the project»*, and if that meant all eight rather than all that break the rule, the three are one class away. `DESIGN.md`'s spacing scale now records **two list rungs with the condition that picks between them**, and `ui/inventory.md`'s List-container row logs the sweep.

The evidence is in her own snapshot: `background rgb(228, 225, 218)`, `padding 12px`, textarea height **351px**, which is `--half`'s floor *without* the sr-only correction, i.e. the row as it stood on 2026-08-12. So the open question was the other three, and her call was to strip them: **`order-setup`, `report-issue`, `contact-support`**. What comes off is the `--media` well at `--r-input` 10 with its 12px inset, granted 2026-08-12 on the reading that an empty textarea has no label-value anatomy to identify it; the reading is not overturned so much as **outweighed by the nesting** — the row's card at `--r-card` 16 held a second painted rectangle inside itself, which is what §5's flat-surface rule spends tone to avoid. **Conformance, measured, not asserted.** `WCAG 1.4.11` governs a boundary that identifies a control and asks 3:1; the well measured **1.08:1** against `--card`, so it never was the identifier — it was affordance, and the identifier stays what it has been since 2026-08-02: the label, the placeholder and the focus ring (`2.5px --ink`, 14.37:1). Both text pairs get *better* off the well: placeholder `--slate` **5.51:1 → 5.95:1**, typed `--ink` **13.33:1 → 14.37:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓ both, the row panel's own recorded pairs). `HIG · Text fields` is satisfied on all three without a departure — each keeps its **visible** label (*Notes for the operator*, *What happened?*, *Your message*), so the placeholder-as-label departure recorded for `rate` on 2026-08-13 stays `rate`'s alone and does not spread with the ground. **Geometry, from the build:** typed text moves **28 → 16px** from the card edge and finally stands level with its own label — the same double-inset fault the 2026-08-12 pass fixed for the *row* (32 → 16), now fixed for the *input*; the rows are unchanged at **111 = 12 + 21 + 10 + 56 + 12**, all four frames hold **812**, and no page overflows horizontally. **`rate` does not move by a pixel — 382 / 406 / 410**, the exact figures rev 54's entry recorded, so the composition the 2026-08-13 pass tuned is untouched. **Kit consequence:** `.dr-field--bare` is **retired rather than left on the bench** — unlike `.dr-btn--text`, `.dr-price--total`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto`, which are rungs waiting for a home, this modifier described a *difference between areas*, and once every area is bare there is no difference left for it to name. The half-screen floor it used to carry re-keys to **`:has(> .sr-only)`**, which names the real cause — `rate` is the one compose card whose label is spoken but not drawn — on the precedent `.dr-rows:has(> .dr-field)` already set in this kit. And `--media` at `--r-input` returns to **the search bar alone**, which is where 2026-08-02 put it and where Apple's own split (grouped form `1:301` vs search field `1:424`) keeps it; verified on `listings` at `#E4E1DA`, r10, 44px. **No string moves** — every label and placeholder stands as written, so `microcopy.md` is untouched by this pass. *(Numbering note: revs 54 and 55 were both taken the same day by a parallel session working the `listings` and card-gap queues; this entry took 55 for a few seconds and stepped to 56 when that landed, per the rev-32 precedent.)*
- **2026-08-15 (rev 57)** — **The upload zone stops holding a button and becomes one.** The designer's feedback on the built `account-edit`, against a Figma reference showing a glyph over a single line: *«improve upload area — write just with text»*. **What was built:** each of the two document cards held a centred `.dr-upload` — 32px tray glyph, `--slate` on `--card` **5.95:1** — with an ordinary `.dr-btn--secondary` under it, `--ink` on `--btn2` **11.54:1**, its pill measuring **1.25:1** against the card it sat on.
