The action reads as `.dr-upload__action`, the button's own type step and weight (**15/600**) in `--ink` on `--media` at **13.33:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓ at ≥ 4.5:1), so the label does not change size, weight or family — only its ground leaves. **This is the build half of a call already recorded:** `microcopy.md`'s 2026-08-13 row says *«the upload action becomes a text action and the link names its object»*, and settles the two strings as **Add drone documents** / **Add insurance**; the strings were written down that day and never drawn. No string moves in this pass. **Conformance, measured before it was built.** `HIG · 44pt` — the target grows from the 44px button to the **325 × 140** zone, so the smallest dimension goes 44 → 140 and the departure risk moves the right way; `WCAG 2.5.8` clears its 24px floor by the same number. `WCAG 1.4.11` — the zone's `--media` ground is **1.08:1** on the card, below 3:1, and is **not claimed as the identifier**: the identifier is the glyph at 5.51:1 on the well, the verb phrase at 13.33:1, and the focus ring, exactly the reading rev 56 took on the multiline areas the same day. The ground stays anyway, because unlike a textarea this element has no label-value anatomy to fall back on — it is affordance for a target that would otherwise be an unmarked 140px of card. **Two gaps in the kit closed by making the zone the control.** `.dr-upload[role="button"]` had **no `:focus-visible` rule at all** — a `tabindex="0"` control with no visible focus indicator, `WCAG 2.4.7` failed on `report-issue` since 2026-08-03 and inherited here the moment the zone took the role; it now takes the universal ring (`2.5px --ink`, offset 2, **13.33:1** on the well). It had **no hover state** either; it now darkens `--media` → `--btn2`, the same one-rung step `.dr-btn--secondary` takes in the other direction. Both land on `report-issue` too, which is a fix on that page and no change to it at rest. **The dashed frame is not re-drawn, and the number is the reason.** The Figma reference frames its zone in a dashed line; this language removed exactly that at rev 45 — `--line` on `--card` is **1.21:1** against `1.4.11`'s 3:1 — and §5's flat-surface rule has since been reaffirmed twice (rev 43, rev 56). **If the frame is wanted for its own sake it can conform:** dashed in `--slate` measures **5.95:1** on the card and **5.51:1** on the well. Held for the designer's word rather than taken either way. **Accessible name:** it now computes from the visible line (name-from-contents), so `WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name` holds by construction, and the hint below is bound with `aria-describedby` instead of sitting unassociated in the panel (`1.3.1`). **One defect the change itself created, caught by measuring and fixed in the same pass.** `.dr-upload` is a flex item and had always been shrink-to-fit; that was invisible while both zones held the *same* string — `Add document` sized them both to about 185 — and giving each its own object made the pair ragged: measured **214 against 156** on a 332px card. A target sized by the length of its label is not a target anyone chose, so `.dr-rows--center .dr-upload` takes `flex-basis:100%` and both zones now measure **300 × 140**, standing at the row's own 16px inset on each side (332 − 2×16). Scoped to the centred document card, which is the rule's own 2026-08-12 context, so `report-issue`'s zone keeps the width it has. **One pre-existing defect found beside this and left alone, per the standing rule:** both hints are direct children of `.dr-rows`, not of `.dr-field`, so they miss the row's `0 16px` inset — the wrapped drone-documents hint measures **2px** from the card edge against the **16** every other line on the card stands at, and the render shows it running the full width. Reported, measured, not swept. *(Noted in passing, kit-wide and not this change's: `--focus-w` is declared `2.5px` and Chrome computes the outline at `2px` on every control that spends it — still over `WCAG 2.4.13`'s 2px floor, so recorded rather than filed.)*
- **2026-08-15 (rev 58)** — **The first cool colour in the system: `--trust-wash` / `--trust-ink`, and the Insured badge stops being a supporting fact.** Two designer calls on the built `order-confirmed-empty`. **(a)** *«remove this sentence»* on the reassurance line — *"Every operator is licence-checked and insured by DRON before their first job — reviews build from here."* It goes, and the screen loses nothing it was carrying: `voice.md`'s empty-state pair is still answered on the frame — the **why** by *New operator · no reviews yet*, the **way out** by *Track anyway* and *Ask for another operator* — and `flows.md`'s RJ-C1 "platform-backed reassurance" moves from a sentence to the two badges beneath the name, which is where **P2** wants it: *show the proof, not the adjective*. The sentence was the adjective; it restated in prose what the chips already assert. **(b)** *«зроби бейдж голубим кольором і справа іконка галочка що означатиме що страховка підтверджена»* — the **Insured** chip takes a light-blue ground and a trailing checkmark, so it reads as *confirmed*, not merely *claimed*. **This is a new colour in a palette that had none, so it was measured before it was drawn, and named for what it means.** The pair is **`--trust-wash #D8E6F2` / `--trust-ink #2A5C7D`**, and the ink on its own wash measures **5.65:1** — not a number chosen freely but the family's own rung, the exact figure `--warn-ink` on `--warn-wash` already stands at, with `--danger-ink` on `--danger-wash` at 5.49 beside it. `WCAG 1.4.3` ✓ at ≥ 4.5:1 for a 12/600 chip, which is not large text under any reading. The wash sits in the family's luminance band too — **1.05:1** against the card, where `--green-wash` is 1.03, `--warn-wash` 1.01 and `--danger-wash` 1.12. Only the two used rungs are declared: no solid `--trust` is cut, because nothing needs one, and `--danger` has been sitting declared-and-undrawn since rev 25 as the standing example of why. **The "one accent, fill only" rule is not touched, and this is not a second accent.** The palette's own comment already says *four warm surfaces, two inks, one accent, **two semantic washes***; this is the third semantic wash, joining `warn` and `danger` in the pair grammar those two established. Signal Green remains the single accent and remains fill-only. **The anti-references were checked, and none of them bars blue** — `PRODUCT.md`'s four are the OLX/classifieds look, DJI-hobbyist adventure orange, warm-neutral SaaS cream and *the exact Bolt/Glovo colour language*; §0's three are cream-plus-terracotta, gradients standing in for photographs, and icon-less screens. The one place "no blue" is written is **rev 6's** description of the three *direction* proposals as reflex-dodging *(no blue/cyan/green/orange)* — a constraint on picking the **brand accent**, and one already overtaken on its own terms, since **green was on that same dodge list and became the accent**. Recorded rather than quietly stepped over. **What the chip now is, and what it costs.** `.dr-chip--trust` is the third job for one geometry: the green tint carries a **claim**, the blue tint carries a **confirmed fact**, the warm grey carries a plain **supporting fact** — the component comment is corrected to say three rather than two. The checkmark is the kit's one icon system, 24-grid at `stroke-width:1.7` in `currentColor`, rendering **0.99px** in the chip's 14px box — inside the recorded 0.85–2.27 range — and it strokes in `--trust-ink`, which the fill-only rule permits because that rule governs `--green`, on the precedent `--danger-ink` set when it took the alert triangle at rev 54. It sits **after** the word, per the designer's *справа*. `WCAG 1.4.1` ✓ — the mark is never the sole carrier; the word **Insured** stays and the chip rule (*never reduced to a colour*) is kept. The check is `aria-hidden`, as every glyph in this kit is, because the word carries the meaning to AT; **if *confirmed* should be spoken and not only drawn, the string becomes `Insurance confirmed`** and that is a `microcopy.md` change, held for her word. **One number named rather than hidden:** the blue wash is **1.03:1** against the grey `--media` chip beside it — near-identical in luminance, separated by hue alone, which is exactly the case `1.4.1` exists for and exactly why the checkmark she asked for is doing real work rather than decorating. `1.4.11` is not engaged: a chip is a label, not a control, and the grey chip has stood at 1.08:1 on the card since 2026-08-02 on that same reading. **Scope, and the line drawn through it.** The Insured chip lives on **five painted pages**. `order-confirmed` and `order-confirmed-empty` both move in this pass, because `_conventions.md` §12 requires sibling states to hold the same DOM structure and changing one alone would break it. The **three tracking pages are left, and deliberately**: their Insured chip already carries a **leading shield**, so a trailing check would give one chip two glyphs, and whether the check replaces the shield or joins it is a design question, not a sweep. `operator-account.html` is still grayscale `.badge` and is out of the painted set entirely. The drift is measured and recorded here so the One-Component-One-Value rule has the count in front of it: **one component, three builds — no glyph (2), leading shield (3), trailing check (2 after this pass)** — and closing it is one class away in either direction.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 59)** — **The price reassurance comes off `order-review`: the sum is the promise.** The designer's word on the built page: *remove this text* — **"This is the final price. Nothing is added at checkout."** The screen's third pass in three days, each one taking a restatement off it: rev 53 took the action bar's Total line because the button already carries the figure, and this takes the hint line because the breakdown already carries the arithmetic. What is left states the price as a fact the way `voice.md` **P3** asks — **Base fare ₴90 · Distance · 7.4 km ₴70 · Service fee ₴20 → Total ₴180**, and **Pay ₴180** on the bar — a sum the reader can check rather than a sentence asking to be believed. It is the same correction the same day's `order-confirmed-empty` edit made for **P2** (`microcopy.md`, rev 58's pass): a sentence that restates in prose what the structure already shows is the adjective, not the proof. **Measured, not asserted:** `.dr-main` has a 597px content box; the frame used **567.2px** of it with the hint and **535.8px** without — free space below goes 29.8 → **61.2px**, no overflow before or after (`scrollHeight == clientHeight` on both), and no other element moves, the hint being the last of five children in a 16px-gap column. **The no-reflow contract is not engaged here, and the numbers say why:** `order-review-loading` is not a skeleton mirror of this screen — it draws 3 summary rows against 5 and 3 price rows against 4, using **392.2px** to the loaded screen's 567.2, so the two frames already differ by ~175px and neither measures the other. Its own `.dr-hint` — *Calculating the final price…*, `role="status"` — is a live region naming what loads, a different job from the promise this line was making, and it stays. **Recorded as a real loss, not a neutral one:** this was the only place the product *undertook* that nothing would be added later; the breakdown proves today's sum but promises nothing about the payment screen, so that undertaking now rests on `payment.html` charging ₴180 and not a hryvnia more — a build guarantee instead of a written one, against CJM Drop-off #2 (*"any 'plus fees' or surprise on the payment screen = abandonment"*). **Left open for the designer, and not edited on my initiative:** `voice.md`'s P3 carries exactly two worked examples and **both are now unbuilt** — the other, `Total · locked, no extra fees — ₴180`, went with rev 53. The principle is intact and still satisfied on the frame; only its illustrations are orphaned, and whether they stay aspirational or are re-cut from the built screen is her call.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 60)** — **The Documents zone takes a name, and the card stops holding a box.** Two designer calls on the built `account-edit`, landing together. **(a) The heading.** *«зроби окремо заголовок над зонами загрузки: "Documents"»* — and because her closing line («не роби заголок "Documents" спільний для двох карток») read two ways against her opening one, she was asked which, with both drawn and both shown to conform; her answer: *«зроби один заголовок Documents для обох карток»*. **One `Documents` over the pair.** The zone had carried no product name at all: `.dr-label` is the *wireframe's* structure annotation and disappears with the shell's toggle, which is how she reads the page, so with annotations off two upload cards stood under nothing. New kit rung **`.dr-sect`** — the kit's first heading above a card — SF **13/600** in `--slate` on `--page`, **6.62:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓ at ≥ 4.5:1), the tab label's own recorded pair, so no colour is invented. It is an `<h2>`, which is the point: the `<span class="dr-field__label">` it replaces named its card to the eye and to nothing else, and the group is now programmatic (`WCAG 1.3.1` ✓). Sentence case in the text face holds the line `concept.md` §0 draws between the mono uppercase that labels **structure** and the type that carries **content**. `HIG · inset grouped lists` indents its section header to the row content; these cards centre their content, so there is nothing to indent to and the heading stands flush with the card edge — a conforming departure, recorded. **The reading she was shown and overruled:** insurance is itself a document, so one `Documents` over both cards puts a document outside the group named for documents; she chose the shared heading with that in front of her, so it is a decision and is logged as one. **(b) The ground.** *«в полі загрузки прибери додатковий бекграунт … як title: "Add drone documents" and as subtitle "Regisration serial etc."»* — the nested `--media` well comes out and **the card becomes the upload surface**, carrying the 32px tray glyph, the title and the subtitle on the axis. This is rev 56's rule arriving where rev 56 explicitly declined to take it: the kit then reasoned that *«an upload zone, unlike a text area, has no label-value anatomy to fall back on»* and kept the well. **That carve-out is overturned on her word and logged, not overwritten** — because the zone now has a title line of its own, which is exactly the anatomy the carve-out said it lacked. Measured, the well was **1.08:1** against the card: affordance, never the identifier, so `WCAG 1.4.11` loses nothing — the glyph (**5.95:1**), the title's verb and rev 57's `--ink` focus ring (**14.37:1**, `2.4.11` ✓) are what identify the control. **Both pairs improve by moving onto the card:** title `--ink` **13.33 → 14.37:1**, glyph and subtitle `--slate` **5.51 → 5.95:1**. **The target grows rather than shrinks:** a 294 × 140 box inside a 326-wide card becomes the whole card — **326 × 161** and **326 × 143** (`HIG · 44pt` ✓, `WCAG 2.5.8` ✓ by three times). Name and description are wired by construction: `aria-labelledby` → the title, `aria-describedby` → the subtitle, so the accessible name is precisely the visible label (`2.5.3` ✓, `4.1.2` ✓). **Strings:** two in-card labels retire — `Drone documents` and `Insurance` — their job split between the heading above and the title inside; no other string moves, and `microcopy.md` was corrected first. **Rhythm, measured with annotations off:** Personal card → heading **24**, the kit's own zone gap; heading → card **12**; card → card **12**; the form's scroll shortens **822 → 755**. **One consequence reported and deliberately not fixed:** the group *closes* on **12**, the same gap it uses inside, because `Personal` and `Preferences` have no heading of their own to open with — the page now reads as one named group among unnamed ones, and whether the other two zones take `.dr-sect` too is the designer's call, held open. **Not swept, on purpose:** `report-issue`'s Evidence zone shares `.dr-upload` but sits inside a row panel as one field among several and carries **no title line** — there the well is still doing the identifying work, so it keeps it; `.dr-upload--card` names the difference. **Kit consequence:** `.dr-rows--center` loses its only user and goes **on the bench** — declared and spent nowhere, beside `.dr-btn--text`, `.dr-price--total`, `.dr-counter` and `--sh-onphoto`. It is kept rather than retired, which is the opposite of rev 56's call on `.dr-field--bare`, and for a stated reason: `--bare` named a *difference between areas* that ceased to exist, while `--center` still names a real shape — a row panel composed on the axis — and has merely lost the one place that used it. *(Numbering note: this entry was written as 58 and stepped to 60 as a parallel session — working the Insured-badge queue on the same day — landed its own 58 and 59, whose `DESIGN.md` cross-references already carried the number. The rev-32 precedent: the texts are intact, only the label moved, and it moved for the entry that could be changed without touching work still in flight.)*
- **2026-08-15 (rev 61)** — **`listings-empty`'s mark becomes a puzzle piece.** The designer's word on the built page: *put Broken/empty puzzle piece icon* — the sibling of the **ERROR** mark that became the alert triangle two days earlier (rev 54 — and, within hours of this entry, the wi-fi fan instead, rev 62), and the same trade in either case: an uppercase word standing in for a picture is replaced by the picture. **Drawn in the one system, not imported:** a single closed path on the 24-grid, `stroke-width:1.7`, `currentColor`, `fill:none`, round caps and joins — a rounded-square body (`3.6 → 18.4` × `4.4 → 19.2`, corner 2.2) with a **socket dipped into the top edge** and a **knob pushed out of the right**, both semicircles of radius 3.2 so the features survive the 26px render: the notch opens ~5.1px against a 1.84px rendered stroke, where the first draft's 2.5 radius closed to a blob. The node count sits with the house — a search glass is a circle and a line, the alert a triangle, a bar and a dot — rather than with a library puzzle glyph, whose four-tab bezier would have been the busiest icon in the set. **Colour does not move:** the mark keeps the base `.dr-mark` ground, `--slate #5A5751` on `--media #E4E1DA` at a computed **5.51:1**, because an empty search result is neither a warning nor a danger — `--warn` belongs to `tracking-empty`'s waiting clock and `--danger` to the two error states, and reaching for either here would have said something the screen does not mean. **Accessibility, the same reading as its sibling:** the glyph is `aria-hidden` and the block's `role="status"` text carries the state — *No services match your search* — so `WCAG 1.1.1` is satisfied by the words and `1.4.11` does not bind a decorative reinforcement; the pair passes at 5.51:1 regardless, against the 3:1 the criterion would ask. **Two alternatives were drawn, rendered at 26px and set aside** — a socket-only piece, which loses the puzzle read at mark size and looks like a bag with a bite; and a piece with a deliberately open bottom-left edge, the literal *broken* reading, which is legible but risks being taken for a rendering fault. The open-edge path is kept here so the option is not re-derived from scratch if she wants the break made explicit: `M12 19.2H5.8A2.2 2.2 0 0 1 3.6 17V6.6A2.2 2.2 0 0 1 5.8 4.4H7.8A3.2 3.2 0 0 0 14.2 4.4H16.2A2.2 2.2 0 0 1 18.4 6.6V8.6A3.2 3.2 0 0 1 18.4 15`. **Still text, and still hers to call:** `time-slot-empty` **EMPTY**, `report-issue-empty` **NO FILE**, `share-empty` **NOT SENT**, `contact-support-error` **QUEUE**, `inspection-report-error` **NO REPORT** — five marks holding words where a glyph would go. `order-setup-empty`'s **~40 MIN** is not among them: that mark is showing a value, not standing in for a picture.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 62)** — **The load error names its cause: the alert triangle gives way to the wi-fi fan.** The designer's call on the built `listings-error` — *«change icon on wi-fi signal icon»*. **This overturns her own call of two days ago and is logged, not overwritten:** rev 54 put the alert triangle on this exact mark on her word *«Add alert icon»*, replacing the `ERROR` text placeholder. The triangle says *something went wrong*, which the title already says; the screen's body says **"No internet connection. Check it and try again."**, and the glyph now says the same thing the sentence does. **Nothing new is drawn.** The three arcs are copied byte-for-byte from `tracking-error`'s mark — `M3.2 10.6a13 13 0 0 1 17.6 0`, `M6.8 14.8a8 8 0 0 1 10.4 0`, `M10.4 19a3 3 0 0 1 3.2 0` — so the set gains no geometry, and §0.2's States row gains a second state rather than a second glyph. **Two standing rules decided the treatment before it was built, and both were already written down.** §0.2 bars the slash on this glyph in as many words — *No slash (that reads as disabled)* — so the wi-fi-off idiom the message might have invited is not drawn, even though the kit does own a slash elsewhere (`delivery-error`'s missing-photo mark, `M4 4l16 16`). And §0.2 reserves opacity to exactly one state — *the one place in the set where opacity carries meaning; everywhere else a glyph is `currentColor` at full strength* — so this fan is **not** dimmed. **The difference between the two connection states is therefore the ink's strength, and it is meaningful:** `tracking-error` dims to `.55` because a live signal was arriving and stopped; `listings-error` stands at full strength because the screen is naming a connection that is simply not there. **Conformance, measured:** full-strength `--danger-ink` on `--danger-wash` is **5.49:1**, clearing `WCAG 1.4.11`'s 3:1 for a meaningful graphic by ×1.83; the glyph box is unchanged at 26px, so the 1.7 stroke renders **1.84** — inside the set's recorded 0.85–2.27. `WCAG 1.4.1` ✓ and `1.1.1` ✓ are unaffected: the mark stays `aria-hidden` and the `role="alert"` block beside it carries the message, exactly as rev 54 left it. No string moves, so `microcopy.md` is untouched by this pass. **One number found beside this and reported rather than swept:** `tracking-error`'s dimmed fan measures **2.43:1** on its wash — below `1.4.11`'s 3:1. It is not a new defect and not this pass's to take: §0.2 grants that opacity its meaning deliberately, the mark is `aria-hidden`, and the state is carried by the title *Live tracking lost* and its body copy, so nothing rests on the glyph alone. Recorded with its number so the departure is a decision on the books rather than an oversight — **if it should conform instead, `.7` measures 3.21:1 and `.8` measures 3.85:1**, and either keeps the fan visibly dimmer than this screen's. Held for the designer's word.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 63)** — **The order card reads name-first: the header row reverses.** The designer on the built `order-history`: *«in these cards change the place for title and subtitle in the left and image with statuse in right side»*. The card led with its 96 × 72 drone well and set the service name to the right of it; the name over date/time now leads at the card's own **20** inset and the well closes the row at **20**, its status mark riding the top-right corner at **26** from the card's top and right edges — measured on the build, unclipped, at both the base and the 389px tier. **The reversal is done in the DOM, and that is the decision rather than a detail.** `flex-direction: row-reverse` would have drawn exactly the same picture and left the source order behind it, which is the textbook `WCAG 1.3.2 Meaningful Sequence` failure: what the eye reads and what the reading order says would disagree, and every keyboard and screen-reader user would get the older arrangement. Swapping the elements themselves keeps the two in step, and it improves what is actually announced — *"Package delivery · Today 10:03 · In progress"* instead of the state arriving before the thing it belongs to. **No rule in the kit changed.** `.dr-order__head` already carried `flex:1` and `.dr-well` `flex:none`, so the row composes correctly from either end; the one responsive rule that touches it narrows the gap under 390px and is direction-blind. **The skeleton reversed with it**, which is where the risk in this change actually sat: `order-history-loading` measures **263.0** against the delivered card's **263.6** — the same half-pixel the no-reflow contract has stood at since 2026-08-03 — and the live card holds **376.06** against the **375.99** the designer's own browser recorded in the feedback snapshot taken before the change. Nothing else on the card moves: the four-node rail, the From ▸ To inset strip, the fee row and the card actions are untouched, and no string moves at all, so `microcopy.md` is not involved in this pass. **Not swept, on purpose:** `.dr-order__top` is drawn on these two pages and nowhere else, so nothing in the product inherits the reversal by accident — the Listing card and the Operator card keep their own image-left anatomy, and whether either should follow the order card is the designer's word, held open.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 65)** — **The system gets a spinner, and the motion inventory goes from four loops to five.** The designer's note on the built `payment-loading`, pointing at the 64px mark: *«add loading spiner»*. **What was there, and why the spinner could not simply be bolted onto it.** The mark was `<span class="success-mark">₴180</span>` — the **grayscale** wireframe component from `_wireframe.css`, standing inside a **painted** Studio frame, because `_wireframe.css` never overrides the `dr-` tokens and only supplies its own. Measured on the build: the `#EEEEEE` disc on `--page #F7F5F2` is **1.07:1**, and the `#909090` label at 11px on that disc is **2.75:1** — `WCAG 1.4.3` asks 4.5:1 and it missed by 1.75. This was not a new discovery either: `ui/kit.css`'s own header has carried the line `state mark · .msg-ph · .success-mark → .dr-mark` since the kit was written, and `delivery-loading` took that migration at rev 40. So the spinner arrives **with** the class change rather than on top of the legacy one: the mark becomes `.dr-mark dr-mark--milestone`, the `--green-wash #E7F0D2` well with an `--ink #1A1A1A` glyph at **14.74:1** — the exact recipe rev 40 set for a *waiting* mark on her own word (*«put the icon in my salad shades»*). The disc against the page moves **1.07 → 1.08:1**, which is the honest number and the right one: the well was never the identifier here, the glyph is. **Three placements were drawn and she chose one.** Spinner *in place of* the sum, spinner as a ring *around* it, or spinner in the disc with the sum moved out to a meta line — she took the first, so **₴180 comes off the screen**. That agrees with `voice.md` **P3**, which asks the price to be stated as a fact *before* the tap, and `payment.html` states it; a status mark is not a receipt. **The string was never in `microcopy.md`'s master table** — `payment-loading` carries four rows there (`Payment`, `Authorizing your payment…`, `Confirming your payment with Apple Pay. Don't close the app.`, `Continue`) and the sum is not among them — so the table is *more* accurate after the removal, not less. Recorded because an uninventoried string on a built page is exactly the drift that table exists to catch. **Cadence: 1.1s, and it sits outside the family deliberately.** The three existing loops stand at **1.8 / 1.4 / 1.6s**, and each describes a *physical* thing — a ring travelling out from a live disc, a placeholder breathing, a file leaving a tray. rev 38 said the rest of it in as many words: *«1.6s is the cadence of an upload rather than of a spinner»* — which grants a spinner its own and is the licence this entry spends. This is the first loop in the system that describes no object at all, only **system work**, and system work at 1.4s+ reads as stalled against what `HIG · Progress indicators` expects of an activity indicator, UIKit's own turning at roughly a second. **The second reason is arithmetic:** this screen also runs the 1.4s skeleton pulse on two `.dr-sk-line` bars, and two loops at the *same* period would phase-lock and read as one breathing organism. 1.1 against 1.4 re-aligns only every **15.4s**, which on a 2–4s authorisation never arrives. **rev 38's two-loop rule is answered, not stepped over.** It refused two loops **on one element**; these are two elements doing two different jobs — the mark says *the system is working*, the bars say *the layout is coming* — and the option she picked was drawn with the skeleton bars still on the frame, so they stay on her sighting rather than by omission. **Geometry — nothing new is cut.** One path on the 24-grid, `stroke-width:1.7`, `currentColor`, `fill:none`, round caps: `M12 3.5A8.5 8.5 0 1 1 3.5 12` — **270° of the same r=8.5 circle** `delivery-loading`'s waiting clock already draws, so §0.2's set gains a state and not a glyph. In the milestone mark's own 32px box the 1.7 renders **2.27**, the figure that mark already carries there, inside the set's recorded 0.85–2.27 band. The 90° gap is what makes the rotation visible at all: a closed ring turning shows nothing. Easing is **`linear`**, the only one of the five that is — an eased spin stutters once per revolution, and there is no object here whose acceleration would justify it. **Conformance, measured rather than asserted.** `HIG · Progress indicators` ✓ — an indeterminate activity indicator is the prescribed control when the wait is short and progress is unknowable, and HIG asks it be paired with text naming what is happening; the screen already carries *Authorizing your payment…* over *Confirming your payment with Apple Pay. Don't close the app.* `WCAG 1.4.11` ✓ by **×4.9** — the arc is `--ink` on `--green-wash`, **14.74:1** against the 3:1 a meaningful graphic asks. `WCAG 2.2.2` **does not bind**, on the same reading rev 38 recorded for `skUp`: a progress indicator's motion *is* the information it carries. `WCAG 2.3.1` ✓ — one turn per 1.1s is **0.91 Hz**, under the 3 Hz ceiling by 3.3×. `WCAG 4.1.3` ✓ — the section's `role="status"` was already there and is untouched, and the glyph is `aria-hidden` as every glyph in this kit is, so the live region announces the sentence and not the picture. `WCAG 1.4.3` — the failing 2.75:1 label leaves with the sum, and nothing on the mark is text now. **Reduce Motion, and the one place a spinner is genuinely weaker than an arrow.** `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` stops it, per the kit's standing contract that every loop *stops rather than degrades*. That contract is hardest here: a frozen spinner is the universal picture of a hung app, where rev 38's frozen arrow was still a legible tray. **The 270° arc is the answer to it** — at rest it is an *incomplete ring*, which reads as work incomplete rather than work stopped, and the sentence beside it inside a `role="status"` region is what actually carries the state. Recorded as this component's known weak edge rather than claimed as neutral; if it should say more under reduce, the lever inside the rules is the clock glyph `tracking-empty` already owns, swapped in at the media query — not a slower spin, which `2.3.3` would not thank us for. **Green budget, measured on the screen and unchanged by the pass:** one solid-green control (the footer primary) and one tint (this mark), the tint uncounted at 1.085:1 against the page. The disc it replaces was grey, and `--green-wash` is not a control. **Found beside this and deliberately not taken — the footer CTA.** `payment-loading` ends in an **enabled** `.dr-btn--primary` reading *Continue*, while the page's own annotation legend says *"disabled action"*. This is not a new find and not this pass's to close: it is `microcopy.md`'s open decision **D5** — *"Transient loading-advance vs the real CTA"*, suggested resolution a disabled copy of the real verb (a disabled `Pay ₴180`), status **structural, later** — and it covers four pages, `order-review-loading`, `payment-loading`, `signin-loading` and `operator-signup-loading`. Closing one page's worth of a four-page structural decision would be the worse outcome. Recorded with its number so the queue keeps it. **One stale count reported and not silently corrected:** `ui/inventory.md`'s Milestone-mark row reads **13 · 1**, but the two classes now stand on **19 screens** between them — `.success-mark` on 8 and `.dr-mark` on 12, `payment` being the one screen that carried both. This pass moves `payment-loading` across, so the split becomes 7 and 13; the row's own total predates several painting passes and wants a re-count that is not this entry's to make. *(Numbering note: written as 64 and stepped to 65. rev 63 went to a parallel session's `order-history` header reversal, and that session then took 64 as well for the route-strip skeleton — a number already baked into its commit message `86daf32`, which cannot move. Mine lived only in files I could still edit, so it moved, per the rev-60 precedent: the texts are intact, only the label shifted, and it shifted for the entry that could change without touching work in flight.)*
- **2026-08-15 (rev 64)** — **The route strip's skeleton collapses to one bar, and the card does not move by a pixel.** The designer's call on the built `order-history-loading`, against the `From ▸ To` inset of a skeleton card: *«прибери усі блоки з цього прямокутника лише один прямокутник»* — take all the blocks out of this rectangle, only one rectangle. **What was built:** each `.dr-inset` held **five** pieces — two `.dr-sk-stop` columns (a 38px label bar over a place bar at 58% / 72%, and 22px over 72% on the To side) with the 26px `.dr-sk-dir` chip between them. Measured on the frame, that is five pulsing shapes inside one 292 × 67 panel, rehearsing a level of detail the loaded strip does not reward: what actually arrives is two short place names. **What is built now:** one `.dr-sk-route` bar spanning the panel's content box. **The height is the whole point of the change, and it is not a magic number.** The three-piece column measured `--sk-lbl-h` **12** + `--sk-stop-gap` **7** + `--sk-place-h` **16** = **35px**, and the new bar is declared as exactly that sum rather than as 35, so it stays tied to the pieces it replaces and follows them if they ever move. The inset therefore holds **67**, the card holds **263** and the list holds **845** — the identical figures measured before the change, so the **no-reflow contract** is kept by construction, which is the reason the kit's skeleton block gives for reusing the loaded card's own containers in the first place. Against the loaded page the gap is unchanged at **0.58px** (loaded inset 67.58, card 263.58) — the same sub-pixel drift `DESIGN.md` already records on the fee row, neither introduced nor closed here. **Why the panel itself is not the one rectangle.** The literal reading of *only one rectangle* would empty `.dr-inset` and let the ground stand alone, and it was rejected on the kit's own written test — *the question is never "should this pulse" but "does what loads replace this surface, or sit inside it"*. The From/To text **sits inside** the strip; the strip is chrome and stays solid, so something inside it must carry the load. An emptied inset would also collapse to its two 16px paddings, **32px**, taking 35px out of every card and **105px** out of the list — the exact reflow the contract exists to prevent. Recorded here so the reading is on the books and not re-proposed as new. **Conformance:** nothing about this is text — the whole `<ul>` is `aria-hidden="true"` and the page names its own state in a `role="status"` line, *Loading your orders…* (`WCAG 4.1.3` ✓, and this screen never lost that line, unlike `listings-loading` at rev 54). `1.4.11` is not engaged by a skeleton bar: it identifies no control and carries no state that the status line does not say in words. The bar takes the set's own values and introduces none — `--btn2` on the `--inset` plane, `--r-detail` 4px, and it joins the same `dr-pulse` at `--pulse-floor .5` and the same `prefers-reduced-motion` stop as every other bar (`2.2.2` ✓, `2.3.1` ✓ — one loop, well under three flashes a second). **Kit consequence:** `.dr-sk-stop`, `.dr-sk-lbl`, `.dr-sk-place`, `.dr-sk-dir` and the `.dr-inset__stop--to .dr-sk-lbl` override are now **declared and spent nowhere** — `order-history-loading` was their only home. They join `.dr-btn--text`, `.dr-price--total`, `.dr-counter`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto` on the bench. **Nothing is deleted**: `--sk-lbl-h`, `--sk-stop-gap` and `--sk-place-h` are still doing work, since the new bar's height is computed from them, and whether the five orphaned rules come out is the designer's word, not this pass's.
- **2026-08-15 (rev 65)** — **Tracking's progress rail turns horizontal and moves under the map.** The designer's word on the built `tracking`: *«зроби цей прогрес бар: "Accepted, on-site etc" горизонтально розмісти під зображенням з картою»* — lay the milestone bar out horizontally and put it under the map image. **This closes G-3**, open since rev 27's audit (*"tracking's rail is still vertical and outside a card"*), left standing then by her instruction not to move elements on cards already worked through. **Nothing new is drawn, and that is the point:** the kit has carried a horizontal rail since the `order-history` pass — `.dr-rail`, four grid columns with the 2px track drawn behind the circles from the centre of column 1 to the centre of column 4, and `--dr-fill` covering the walked span. `tracking` is exactly the case its `--at2` modifier was cut for (*live order: walked step 1 → 2 of 4*), so the screen adopts the component rather than growing a second one; `.dr-node` is the same 22px circle in both, unchanged. **Measured on the build, before and after:** the vertical `.dr-track` stood **142.0px** (4 × 22px nodes + 3 × 18px gaps) and the horizontal rail stands **45.7px** — the block gives back **96.3px**, and `tracking`'s scrollable `main` falls from ~773 to **677px** against a 577px viewport. The walk is drawn right: the fill measures **81.5px** of the track's **244.5px** span, exactly one third, which is step 1 → 2 of four. Labels sit one line each in an 81.5px column (47.1 · 44 · 37 · 26px measured), so nothing wraps at 375. **The gain the instruction was actually after:** the progress was the *last* block on the screen, under the operator card and below the fold; under the map it is now inside the first viewport in all three states (`railInFirstView` true, measured) — RJ-C2 is *know where the operator is right now*, and the milestone is half of that answer. **Applied to the three states that carry it** — `tracking`, `-empty`, `-error` — not to the one she named alone, because one screen may not hold two layouts; `-loading` has no progress block and is untouched. In `-error` there is no map to sit under, the lost-signal message occupies that slot, so the rail takes the same structural position and the three states stay parallel rather than rearranging between them. **The one real cost, recorded not hidden:** the step label drops from `.dr-track__text` at **14px** (`--t-body-size`) to `.dr-rail__lbl` at **10.5px** (`--t-microsm-size`), which is under `HIG`'s *"avoid text smaller than 11 points"*. It is not a new departure — the rung is `DESIGN.md`'s own tenth step and `order-history`'s rail has shipped on it since 2026-08-03 — but this change extends it to a second screen, and it is the kind of thing that should be decided rather than inherited. `WCAG` is met either way: `--slate` on the page is **6.62:1** and the current step's `--ink` **15.99:1**, both past `1.4.3`'s 4.5:1, and `1.4.4` is unaffected since the type scales. **State is not carried by colour:** the green fill is `1.69:1` on the page, under `1.4.11`'s 3:1, and it does not need to clear it — the walked node is a filled disc with a checkmark, the current one a ring with a halo, the future ones empty circles, the current step says `aria-current="step"`, and each label names itself. Same reading the live disc and the pager dot already hold in `DESIGN.md`'s below-3:1 table. **Semantics kept, and better than the component's first home:** the rail here is a `<ul>` of `<li class="dr-rail__step">`, so the four milestones stay a list for a screen reader (`WCAG 1.3.1`), where `order-history` builds the same rail from `<span>`s because it sits inline in a card. No kit change was needed for that — `.dr-rail__step` sets `display:flex`, which blockifies the `li` and leaves no marker box; verified in the browser rather than assumed. **Consequence for the kit:** `.dr-track` now has **no product user** — only `kit.html`'s specimen — and joins `.dr-btn--text`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto` on the bench of declared-and-unspent rungs. It is left declared, not deleted; the vertical form is the right one for a longer or variable-length sequence, and the operator side has not been designed yet.

Readout **14.37:1**, end labels `--slate` **5.95:1**, the Insured chip **5.65:1** (`1.4.3` ✓ all three). Targets: thumbs are `--sz-box` **24px**, exactly `WCAG 2.5.8`'s AA floor, standing in a **44px** rail so `HIG · 44pt` is met by the lane the thumbs sit in. The same thing happened at rev 56 and is worth naming twice: what she is reacting to is the *placeholder*, and the instruction stands either way — this call supersedes rev 61, whose entry stays on the record rather than being rewritten. **The reference is a photograph and the slot is an icon, so what landed is the drone drawn in the one system.** §6.3 puts the floor for a render at **~96px** and the mark is **52 × 52** with a **26px** glyph inside it; a photographic cutout at 26px is not small imagery, it is mush, and it would put two registers in one component — every other state mark on the product is a 24-grid outline. So this follows rev 61's own stated trade, *drawn in the one system, not imported*, and the drone is a glyph. **Chosen by rendering, not by argument — seven candidates at final size and again at 6×, the method rev 38 used.** Front-view drone (read as a crossed-out bag); X-quad with blade bars (busy, the strike lost); X-quad with a horizontal strike (the line joined the airframe instead of cancelling it); X-quad unstruck (the best *drone*, but nothing negates it); X-quad with a **steep 61°** strike, which was built, committed to the page, and then rejected on its own 6× render — it cleared the arms but ran tangent to two rotors and merged into the top-right one; and the one that landed, the **plus-armed quad with the kit's own 45° slash**. **No departure in the end, and that is precisely why the airframe is a plus.** The obvious drone is an X-quad and it was drawn first; but on an X the kit's slash lies **along an arm** and cancels nothing, which forced a 61° variant, which in turn ran tangent to two rotors. Turning the airframe onto the axes empties the diagonal, so the strike is `M4 4l16 16` — **byte-identical to `delivery-error`'s, unchanged since 2026-08-03** — and it clears all four rotors by **4.53** against their `r 1.8`. The system gains no new angle and no new exception: the geometry was fitted to the rule rather than the rule bent to the geometry. **And the slash is correct here, in the one place §0.2 bars it.** That rule is written on the wi-fi fan — *no slash, that reads as disabled* — and it is glyph-specific, not general: on a **signal**, a slash says the signal is switched off; on an **object**, it says the object is absent. `delivery-error` has crossed out a picture since 2026-08-03 on exactly that reading, and *nothing matches your search* is an absence, not a disablement. **Conformance:** the mark is the neutral `.dr-mark` — `--slate` on `--media`, **5.51:1**, comfortably over `WCAG 1.4.11`'s 3:1 — and correctly *not* `--danger`, because an empty result is not an error. The glyph is 26px in the 52px slot, so the declared 1.7 stroke renders **1.84**, inside the set's 0.85–2.27. It stays `aria-hidden` with the `role="status"` block beside it carrying *No services match your search* (`1.1.1` ✓, `1.4.1` ✓). Two paths and five circles, `currentColor`, `fill:none`, round caps and joins — no new token, no new size, no new colour. **Open, and worth her word:** §6.2 assigns **`drone-10.png`** to *"Empty states — nothing ordered yet"*, so a real render for an empty state is already planned in the concept. If she wants **that** — the photograph rather than the glyph — it is a different element, not a different icon: the 52px mark becomes a well at §6.3's ~96px floor or larger, and *striking through a photograph* is unbuilt ground (`--sh-onphoto` is the only over-a-photo token this system has, and it is spent nowhere). Held, not taken.

Cards stand equal at **161px**, the frame holds **812**, nothing overflows. **Still a measure and never a `<br>`** — the rule `.dr-display--balance` set on 2026-08-13 for rate's question, kept for the same reason: the Ukrainian string re-balances itself, where a hard break would strand a line in translation. Contrast and targets are untouched: the note is `--slate` on `--card`, **5.95:1**, and the card is the tap target as before — a line count is not a criterion in either standard, so `HIG` and `WCAG` are silent here and both readings conform; this is a taste call, recorded as one. The designer's own viewport is 2256 wide, where the frame is the full **375** and the column **241**; her feedback snapshot's card width of **341.539** confirms which of the two views is hers. - **2026-08-15 (rev 84)** — **Back was dead on 24 screens, and the fix is the HIG rule itself.** The designer's call: *«пропрацюй навігацію на усіх wireframes і статусах… кнопка іконка "Back" — подивись як у HIG, бо я не пам'ятаю точно: вона має повертати на попередній скрін чи у вікно звідки прийшов юзер… Не повинно залишитись ніде два заголовка»*. Swept across all **84** pages — 46 painted, 38 still grayscale — and the honest report is that one half of the ask was almost entirely already right and the other half was broken in a way nobody could see from the markup. **The answer to her question, plainly: in HIG the two options are the same option.** `HIG · Navigation bars` gives the back button one job — return to the **previous screen in the navigation stack**, which *is* the screen the user came from — and its label is that previous screen's **title**. There is no third behaviour to choose between. The project already implements the label half: `microcopy.md`'s D1 settled Variant A on 2026-08-03, and the sweep confirms **44 of 84** pages carry a back whose label is the title of the screen it returns to (`‹ Order review` on Payment, `‹ Sign in` on Verification, `‹ Help` on Support), with one deliberate exception — `operator-fee-terms` keeps a bare `‹ Back` because the screen behind it, the onboarding slider, has no title to name. Tab roots carry no back; `listings-filters` carries `×`, which is right, because HIG gives a **sheet** a Cancel/close control and not a back chevron. **What was broken:** `_wf-shell.js` wires back controls to `history.back()` — but its selector is `.wf-frame .back`, the *grayscale* class. When the painted layer renamed it `dr-back`, the selector was never widened, so **24 painted screens shipped a `<button class="dr-back">` with no `href` and no handler**. Measured in the browser rather than inferred: on `contact-support`, arrived from `support` with `history.length 3`, the shell's selector matched **0** elements and clicking Back left the URL unchanged. Twenty-four dead controls, invisible in review because the button looks and styles correctly. **The one-line fix is also the correct one, not merely the cheap one.** The selector becomes `.wf-frame .back, .wf-frame .dr-back`, so every back returns to the previous entry in real history — literally HIG's definition — and an `<a href>` still follows its href when a page is deep-linked with no history. **This matters most where a screen has more than one way in**, which is exactly the case a hardcoded `href` cannot serve: verified on `contact-support` entered from `support` → Back lands on `support`, and entered from `order-history` → Back lands on `order-history`. A fixed href would have sent the second user to the wrong screen. Three trips re-run after the change, all correct, including one page that already had an href. **The second half of the ask — no screen says its own name twice — was one screen.** All 84 were checked for a bar title repeated by a content heading, and for a bar holding two title-like spans. The bar-holds-two count is **zero**. The duplicate count is **one**: `delivery` carried `Delivered` as the nav-bar `<h1>` *and* `Delivered` again as the display line under the success mark. The display line goes; the bar keeps the name, which is the job she assigned it (*«текст показує на якій сторінці я знаходжусь»*) and the job HIG assigns it. Nothing is lost — the line beneath, *Your parcel arrived at 10:07.*, is the same fact with the time in it, which is what P1 prefers anyway. The sibling states keep their display lines, having never duplicated: *Delivery photo is missing*, *Waiting for the delivery photo…*. **Found and reported, not swept:** the bar title is an `<h1>` on **8** pages and a `<span>` on **33** — one component in two semantics. No frame holds more than one `<h1>`, so nothing fails today, but the inconsistency is real and picking one is a decision about heading structure across the whole product rather than a navigation fix, so it is left for her word.

Rev 58 built `--trust-wash` / `--trust-ink` and applied it to `order-confirmed` and `-empty` only, holding the three tracking states back on a stated reason — *their Insured chip already carries a leading shield, so a trailing check would give one chip two glyphs, and whether the check replaces the shield or joins it is a design question, not a sweep*. **She has answered the colour half and only the colour half**, and the condition she attached is the one the component already encodes: blue is for a **confirmed** credential, and the green claim beside it is what makes the pair read as claim-plus-confirmation rather than as two unrelated tints. **Applied to `tracking`, `tracking-empty`, `tracking-error`** — every remaining page where an Insured chip sits beside a green Verified. So the same component now ships in two builds again — shield-plus-check on three pages, check-only on two — and the One-Component-One-Value rule wants one. Both readings are defensible (the shield says *insurance*, the check says *confirmed*), which is exactly why it is hers: **one class or one `<svg>` closes it in either direction.**

The project's black is `--ink #1A1A1A` — the palette has no `#000000` anywhere and says so — so the mark moves `--green` → `--ink` and measures **14.37:1** on the card, verified in the browser. **This overturns rev 50, which was itself an overturn, and the record now closes cleanly.** Rev 48 cut `--green-ink` as *the green that may speak* and gave this checkmark as its first home; rev 50 took it back to Signal Green on her word — *«#9BCF4A, this colour»* — with the number named at the choice, **1.52:1** on the card against `1.4.11`'s 3:1, and entered as an accepted below-3:1 mark on the precedent of the live disc and the pager dot. Three pages first reported a clipped element and each turned out to be an `.sr-only` span at 1px, which is what `.sr-only` is for — a false positive from the check, not a defect on the frame, recorded so the next sweep does not chase it. **§5's rhythm is unchanged in kind, only in size:** the between-cards gap and the interval inside a card keep the order rev 55 set, since every value moved up together and none crossed another.

The toast is created on demand — verified absent from the DOM until the first click — and reused after. **One defect found by looking at the render rather than the numbers.** The first build offset the toast by a tab-bar height, and `contact-support` has an **action bar**, which is taller: the toast landed squarely on top of the green primary that raised it. Motion is one fade-and-rise on the kit's own `--dur-base` / `--ease-out`, switched off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. **The string is `voice.md`'s, not a celebration:** **Issue marked resolved.** — the fact, full stop; the vocabulary's own word *issue*; no *successfully*, which the Never list bars; no exclamation. **Open, and it is a flow question rather than a component one:** the button still carries `href="listings.html"`, and `flows.md` still reads *Mark resolved → back to orders*, but the click now shows the toast **and stays**, because a toast on a page you immediately leave is a toast nobody sees. card edge to the first radio **16 / 18 → 16 / 26** left / top; the separator **52 from the leading edge in both**, and **0 → 16** short of the trailing one; the row's hit area **326 → 294 × 44** (on the 375 frame, whose scroll area carries a 15px desktop scrollbar — 341 → 309 with an overlay one), so `WCAG 2.5.8`'s 24 × 24 floor is cleared **×12.25 and ×1.83**, and the 44 `HIG · 44pt target` asks for untouched. **One number gets worse and is stated rather than buried:** the Price card's foot, where the range sits, goes **22 → 16px** below the `₴0 / ₴1,000+` labels, because the band's own `0 16px 14px` would otherwise have doubled to 32 at the sides and 30 at the foot — the single place where "equal on all sides" would have visibly failed. **And what 16 does not buy, said plainly:** the *optical* inset to the radio is 16 at the sides and **26** top and bottom, since the box keeps its 10px of row slack; A sighted mouse user saw an affordance that did nothing; everyone else had no control on the page. **Swept before it was fixed, because "по всіх wireframes" was the standing instruction:** every painted `.dr-box` in the product was counted against the `<input>`s on its page — `listings-filters` **11 boxes / 11 inputs**, `payment` **3 / 3**, `order-setup` **1 / 0**. Neither pass checked that the thing being styled was wired, because the question put to me was about looks each time. **A control should be proved to work before it is made to look good** — the cheap check is one line, counting inputs against boxes, and it now exists as a sweep.
