Текст має бути різний відповідати функції»*. **Every `<button>` and every `.dr-btn` inside a `.wf-frame` was enumerated — 84 files, 234 controls — rather than sampled**, because the honest answer to "where else" is a count, not an impression. **The rule the sweep applied, and it is now `voice.md`'s Toast row and `_conventions.md` §11.2:** a toast belongs to an action that finishes **on the screen you are standing on** and leaves **no other trace**. Most controls fail the second test, not the first. **Two strings, and they differ because the functions differ — which is the instruction taken literally.** `share` · **Copy link** → **`Link copied.`**, one sentence because there is no second fact; this is the purest case in the product, since a sheet cannot show that a copy happened and without the toast the tap has no answer at all. `order-setup-empty` · **Notify me when available** → **`Saved. We'll tell you when an operator is free.`**, two sentences on rev 90's pattern — the fact, then the promise that the user does not come back and check. It does **not** reuse rev 90's sentence: that button waits on a **region DRON has not launched in**, this one waits on **a person who is busy**, and `flows.md` uses `Saved` at this node where it uses a launch phrasing at the other. **One behaviour changes, and `flows.md` is the authority, not taste:** `order-setup-empty`'s primary was `<a href="order-setup.html">` — tap *notify me* and the app drops you into a working order form, answering a request with a non-sequitur — while the flow node is **terminal** (`"save for later" → Saved: notify me when an operator is free`, `:::done`). A terminal node on a screen looks like a toast. The `href` stays as the no-JS fallback on rev 89's `Mark resolved` precedent, and that inconsistency is flagged rather than swept. **The component gained one modifier, and both reasons for it are forced rather than stylistic.** `share`'s sheet is `aria-modal="true"`, so a toast appended beside it is **hidden from assistive technology entirely** — it is therefore parented **inside the dialog**; and the sheet owns the bottom edge, so a toast measured up from the frame's floor lands under it — `.dr-toast--over-sheet` floats it clear of the sheet's **top** edge at `bottom: calc(100% + var(--sp-snug))`. **Measured on the build, not asserted:** `share` toast **125.55 × 43.60**, exactly **12px** above the sheet, no overlap with the button that raised it, `--page` on `--ink` **15.99:1** unchanged, and its text **present and not ignored** in the accessibility tree inside the dialog named *Share DRON*. `order-setup-empty` **325.40 × 63.20**, **20px** above its action bar, and the click does not navigate. **Regression checked rather than assumed:** rev 89's and rev 90's toasts still fire, still 20px above their footer, still stay put — `contact-support` **202.84 × 43.60**, `listings-empty` **325.40 × 63.20**. **What the sweep refused, which is the more useful half.** The five `Add …` upload buttons get none — the file lands in its own well, and a toast there would restate what is on screen and teach that every tap owes a message. `Call support` hands off to the dialer, and nothing has happened at the moment of the tap. Pickers, service tabs, day chips and the rating stars all show their own result. `operator-listings`' availability segment already says **You are Available · receiving jobs near Podil** in a line that *stays*, and a permanent statement beats a four-second one. **No error screen gets one**, now stated as a rule: an error must remain re-readable and a toast leaves. **One finding reported and deliberately not built:** `order-history`'s three **Delete order** buttons have neither a confirm nor an undo, and `voice.md`'s *Dangerous action* rule asks for the consequence **before** the tap, not a narration after — a toast is not the fix, a confirm sheet or a toast **with an Undo** is, and both are hers. **And the boundary that limits this whole pass, said plainly:** the toast is a **painted-layer** component — declared in `ui/kit.css`, and it animates, which §3 defers in the grayscale wireframe. So the operator screens, still unpainted, take their toasts when they are painted; a `data-toast` on a page that does not link `ui/kit.css` renders an unstyled `<div>`. **Still open, and it is rev 89's question unchanged:** five real cases — `account-edit` **Save changes**, `rate` **Submit review**, `job-offer` **Decline**, `result-upload-error` **Queue offline**, and `contact-support`'s own **Mark resolved** — finish by *changing screen*, so their toast would have to ride the navigation and appear on the screen the user lands on. `data-toast` fires only where you stay. **A process note worth keeping:** the designer's original report that the rev-90 toast "did not appear" was reproduced and explained here — the local server's assets were being served from browser cache, and the page was running a `_wf-shell.js` from before rev 89. The toast was correct; the browser was old. Every measurement above was taken with the cache disabled at the protocol level, which is now the only trustworthy way to check a change on this prototype.
  *(Numbering: rev 95 was taken the same evening by a parallel session for `account`'s mode-switch hint line, committed at 00:03 before this entry was written; this pass steps to **96** rather than renumbering theirs — the rev-92 precedent, where a silent renumbering of someone else's entry proved worse than the collision it was fixing.)*
- **2026-08-16 (rev 97)** — **The fact row's value gets an axis. Every read-only card in the product now reads left, on the pattern of the card that is typed into.** The designer, pointing at `account-edit`'s Preferences card: *«пройдись по усіх ваірфреймах мого проекту знайди аналогічні картки, зроби вирівнюваня тексту по лівому краю як у цій кратці кругом в проекті»*. **The complaint had a number under it, and finding it is what made the options honest.** `account.html` holds the *same three facts* as the card she was looking at — Payment method, Saved address, Language — and drew them the other way round: `.dr-rows__row` was `justify-content: space-between` with `.dr-rows__val{text-align:right}`, so the value's **left** edge landed wherever its string happened to end. Counted across the 13 wireframes that carry the row: **46 rows standing at 24 different x**, from **131.75** (`switch-role`) to **294.78** (`order-review`'s ₴90) on a 341 card — against the single axis `.dr-field` has had since rev 45. Her eye had found the one component in the system with no column. **Conformance came before the options, and the answer was that there is no failure to fix.** `HIG · Lists and tables` ships the **trailing** value in a grouped list row; `HIG · Text fields` ships the **leading** value in a form row. Apple uses both, so neither alignment is *the* conforming one and this is a taste call, not a compliance fix — said plainly rather than dressed up as a defect. WCAG is untouched in either direction: `1.4.3` keeps its pairs (key `--slate` **5.95:1**, value `--ink` **14.37:1**), `1.3.1` keeps its DOM order, and `1.4.8` governs justified text, not trailing. **Two calls went to her, both raised by measuring the first build rather than by shipping it.** **(a) The split is not 1:1, and that is the more interesting of the two.** Matching the reference card exactly — key and value each `flex:1`, gap 10, axis **175.5** — leaves the value **149.5px**, and four strings in the product are wider than that. It put **six** one-line rows onto two, `En route to drop-off` missing by **0.48px** on the three tracking pages and orphaning the word "off" on its own line. Offered against a fixed label column, she chose the column: **`--sz-rowkey` 140** — the widest key in the system, *First time as Operator*, measures **133.34**, so no label wraps — value **159**, gap `--sp-10`, axis **166**. Two-line rows land at **3 → 5** instead of 3 → 9, and the two that were added (`switch-role`'s *Any time from Account* at 172.45, `tracking-empty`'s *~15 min (running late)* at 168.97) are strings no column of this width holds; the three that were already wrapped (268.41, 200.48, 168.14) are unchanged. **(b) The money column stays trailing** — her call, and the numeric-column convention: tabular numerals only line up on the last digit if the column is right-aligned, and left they line up on the ₴ with **₴180 running 24px past ₴90**, so the breakdown stops reading as a column that adds up. New modifier **`.dr-rows--money`** (key `flex:1`, value `flex:none`), spent on `order-review`'s price breakdown and its loading state and nowhere else; `resolution`'s *Refund issued ₴180* is a **sentence**, not a figure in a stack, and stays with the facts. **One thing the change broke on the way and was caught by measuring, not by looking:** dropping `space-between` also dropped the split the **skeleton** rows relied on — their bars are not `__key`/`__val` but `.dr-sk-line`, so the two bars collapsed side by side at the left on `order-review-loading` and `tracking-loading`. `.dr-rows__row:has(> .dr-sk-line)` keeps the trailing split, on the reasoning that the bars are **proportional stand-ins** (w40 / w50 / w30 of the *row*) and not text on an axis — pinning them to the 140 column would either blow each bar out to fill its track or shrink it against it, and the loading state would stop looking like the thing it stands in for. Verified before and after: **x16 w123.6 \| x232.3 w92.7** on every skeleton row, both pages byte-identical. **A second defect the axis created, and it only surfaced because the sweep ran over all 85 pages instead of the 13 that carry the row:** `inspection-report`'s `Signed` row holds a **badge** as its value, and `.dr-chip` is `white-space:nowrap` by construction at **164.08** — wider than the 159 the axis leaves. It could not shrink, so it pushed to **10.92** from the card's trailing edge, eating **5.08** of the 16px inset every other line on that card stands at; the same double-inset fault 2026-08-12 fixed for the field row, arriving from the other direction. Truncating a badge is not an option and letting the label shrink would break the axis for one row, so the row does what HIG does when a value will not sit beside its label — **`.dr-rows__row--stack`**, label on its own line and value at the card's full width, which is `.dr-field--area`'s shape and already in this kit. Measured after: key **16**, value **16 × 309**, chip clear of the card edge by **56.59**, row **73.8 → 81.0**. Spent on one row in the product. **Her word is open on it:** the alternative was to let the label column shrink 140 → 135 on that row alone, which keeps the row on one line and breaks the axis by 5px instead of the inset by 5.08 — 5px either way, and the inset is the rule this project has defended twice. **Measured on the build, headless, all 13 pages:** 42 fact values at **166** (one axis, from 24), the four ₴ figures unmoved at **294.78 / 271.13**, every frame still **812**, no page scrolling sideways. **Scope, stated because "кругом в проекті" deserves a count and not an impression:** one rule in `ui/kit.css` reaches `account`, `delivery`, `inspection-report`, `order-confirmed`, `order-review` ×2, `order-setup-empty`, `resolution`, `switch-role`, `tracking` ×4 — plus `ui/kit.html`, the specimen page, which picks it up for free. Two HTML files changed, and only to carry the money modifier. **Corrected in passing, per the standing rule that the frame wins:** `DESIGN.md` recorded the panel's padding as `2px 16px` where the build has said `8px 16px` since rev 88. | `ui/kit.css`, `wireframes/order-review.html`, `wireframes/order-review-loading.html`, `DESIGN.md`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 98)** — **The input group stops being a card and becomes HIG's plain list — and the border she disliked was never there.** The designer on the built `account-edit`: *«мені не подобається обводка в полях вводу та текстових полях, зроби так як робить HIG — там наче в інпутах немає обводки, коли вводиться текст просто індикатор і вспомагательний текст ледь сірим»*. **The first finding changed the question rather than answering it.** The input has **no border**, and her own snapshot says so — `border: 0px none`. Querying every drawn line in that form turns up exactly two: the `.dr-rows` **card fill**, and the 1px hairline between rows. So what read as обводка was the **panel**, and the question was never about the input at all. **Both of the styles she was comparing are HIG**, which is the part worth saying plainly rather than picking a side on: iOS ships the **grouped inset list** (Settings — a card, rows, separators, value right-aligned), which is what was built, and the **plain list / plain field** (Notes, a search bar — no box, a caret, a grey placeholder), which is what she described. Neither is more correct; they are for different screens. Three options were built live and measured — panel kept, panel off with the line kept, panel and line both off — and **her choice is the middle one**, the plain list. **Scoping was decided by the frame, not by the class name.** `.dr-rows` carries two different jobs: input groups and **read-only** detail panels (`tracking`'s ETA/Status, `order-review`'s breakdown, `account`'s preferences). Counted before anything moved: **7 pages carry input groups, 13 carry read-only panels, and 0 carry both** — so `:has(> .dr-field)` splits them cleanly and the read-only card stays exactly as it was. Verified after: `tracking` and `account` still measure `--card` behind their rows. **Two consequences that had to move with the panel, or the result would have been wrong.** The field gave up its own 16px inset, because with the panel gone the text would have stood at **32** from the frame — the main's 16 plus the field's own; measured after, label, value and separator all sit at the frame's own **16** (17 including its 1px border). And the separator dropped its indent, because there is no panel edge left to indent from. **Measured across all seven:** typed `--ink` improves **14.37:1 → 15.99:1** moving off the card onto the page, no page scrolls sideways, nothing clips. **The placeholder is the half she could not have freely, and she was told the number before she chose.** A placeholder is text, so `WCAG 1.4.3` asks it for **4.5:1**. Today's `--slate` is **5.95:1** on the card; the lightest warm grey that still passes is **#6C6963 at 4.52:1** — one small step, not a range. A shade at the "ледь сірим" of most mockups (#86827A) measures **3.16:1** and was shown on the page **labelled as failing**, so the difference was visible and the cost was explicit. **She kept `--slate`**, which leaves the product at zero text-contrast failures. On the page it now reads at **6.62:1**, up from 5.95, since it moved off the card too.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 99)** — **`order-setup`'s notes field loses its visible label — the third of four compose cards, and the first where the placeholder does not carry the naming.** The designer's call on the built page, pointing at the label: *забери цей текст*. **This answers the question rev 81 left open by name** — that entry, on 2026-08-15, took `contact-support`'s *Your message* off the screen and wrote down that the remaining two, `order-setup`'s *Notes for the operator* and `report-issue`'s *What happened?*, were her word and not a sweep. One of the two has now been answered. **The line comes off the screen; the name does not come off the control.** `<label class="dr-field__label" for="nt">` becomes `<label class="sr-only" for="nt">` — a class swap, not a deletion, the move `rate` made on 2026-08-13 and `contact-support` on 2026-08-15. **Verified in the accessibility tree, not asserted:** the textarea computes `role: textbox`, `name: "Notes for the operator"` — sourced from the sr-only label, with the placeholder present only as the separate, lower-priority property it is. `WCAG 4.1.2` ✓, `3.3.2` ✓ (the placeholder stands as instruction), `1.3.1` ✓ (the `for`/`id` pair is untouched). **The departure is `HIG · Text fields`** — *a placeholder is not a label* — and it is now on its third screen, recorded as a decision with its reason and not filed as a defect. **What is different on this screen, and is reported rather than decided.** The reading rev 54 and rev 81 leaned on was that the field is the only control on a single-purpose compose card whose nav-bar title names it — *Rate this order*, *Support* — so the placeholder reads as instruction beside a name the screen already gives. **Neither half of that holds here.** The notes field is the **fourth row of a four-row input group** whose other three keep visible labels (*Pickup address:*, *Drop-off address:*, *Parcel size*), so this is the first input group in the product where a labelled row sits beside an unlabelled one; and the nav-bar title is *Package delivery*, which names the service, not the field. The placeholder — *Floor, entrance code, what's inside…* — is **examples, not naming**: it never says *notes* and never says *operator*, which is exactly what `voice.md`'s form-hint rule asks of it (*says how, by real example; never just repeats the label* — and that line cites this very string). So `3.3.2` passes on instruction, but the *placeholder-carries-the-naming* sentence rev 81 wrote is **not available on this screen**, and whether the placeholder should start naming the field is the designer's, put to her with this entry. **Measured on the build at the 375 frame, both states toggled live on the current kit, before → after:** the row loses exactly the label line and the gap it sat on — **21 + 10 = 31px** — so the field goes **111 → 80** (12 + 56 + 12) and the input group **259 → 228**. The textarea does not move or resize: **341.4 × 56**, now starting at y 320.8 where the label used to begin. `main` measures **630 = 630** clientHeight either way, so nothing scrolls vertically that did not before, and the frame overflows by **0** horizontally. **Contrast is unchanged by this pass, because the ground is — but the ground is not the one rev 56 recorded.** rev 98 landed minutes earlier and took the input group off its card, so both pairs were **recomputed against the page** rather than copied forward: placeholder `--slate` on `--bg` **6.62:1**, typed `--ink` **15.99:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` ✓ both, and better than the 5.95 / 14.37 the card gave). **No no-reflow contract is engaged:** neither sibling state carries this field — `order-setup-empty` is the no-operator screen and `order-setup-error` carries the drop-off coverage hint — so `_conventions.md` §12 asks nothing here. **The product now stands at three placeholder-only compose cards against one labelled**, `report-issue`'s *What happened?*, which rev 81 named alongside this one and which stays untouched until she says. **Two findings this pass did not act on, reported so they are not lost:** (a) `order-setup`'s save-address row carries an inline `border-radius:6px`, which is **not one of the six** in the closed ramp (`10 · 12 · 13 · 16 · 22 · 999`) — pre-existing, from the 2026-08-15 checkbox pass, and left as built because `wireframes/` is not swept without her word; (b) `.dr-field--area .dr-field__label` and the `--half` sr-only floor still key off classes this screen no longer uses in the same way — declared, not broken. *(Numbering note: this entry took **98** and stepped to **99** when a parallel session's `rev 98` — the input group becoming HIG's plain list — landed in a commit first and swept this entry into it. Same-day collision, handled on the rev-55 precedent of 2026-08-15.)* | `wireframes/order-setup.html`, `microcopy.md`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 100)** — **The text area gets its ground back, and the focus ring gives way to the caret.** The designer on the built `rate`: *«поверни бекграунд для текстового поля, я лише не хочу щоб ти stroke добавляв коли я вводю текст — лише індикатор вводу і текст»*. Two asks, and they pull in opposite directions on purpose: **more** surface, **less** outline. **(a) The ground returns, and this is not rev 56 being overturned so much as a different question being asked.** Rev 56 stripped the area's `--media` well because a painted box was nested **inside a card**, which is what §5's flat-surface rule spends tone to avoid. Rev 98 then took the card away entirely. So the well now sits **straight on the page** with nothing behind it — the nesting that made rev 56 right no longer exists. Restored at the values it always had: `--media` at `--r-input` **10** with a **12px** inset, typed `--ink` **13.33:1**, placeholder `--slate` **5.51:1**, both measured on the build. **`rate`'s composition does not move a pixel** — the textarea still stands at **382** and its row at **406**, the exact figures rev 54 tuned and rev 56 preserved, because `box-sizing:border-box` spends the new padding inward. Applied to all four compose screens, which is the set rev 56 treated as one: `rate`, `contact-support`, `order-setup`, `report-issue`. **(b) The ring comes off — and it is allowed, in this one place, for a reason worth stating rather than assuming.** `WCAG 2.4.7` requires a visible focus indicator, and its own Understanding text names **the text cursor in a text field** as one. So on a field that shows a caret, the caret *is* the indicator, and removing the outline moves the job rather than dropping it. To make that explicit instead of leaving the one remaining indicator to a browser default, `caret-color` is now **declared**: `--ink`, **13.33:1** against the area's ground and 15.99:1 on the page. Verified with the field focused: `outline: none`, caret `rgb(26,26,26)`. **It does not generalise, and the scoping says so:** the rule is keyed to `.dr-field__input`, so **every control without a caret keeps its ring** — buttons, the checkbox, the radio, the upload zone — because they have nothing else to fall back on. **One residual risk, named:** a blinking caret is a thinner indicator than a 2.5px ring, and `2.4.13 Focus Appearance` would ask for more — but that is AAA and this project targets AA, where the caret conforms. **Found beside this and reported rather than swept, because it is the same criterion:** the `role="button"` rows on `account-edit` (Payment method, Language) have **no focus indicator at all** — no ring, and no caret to substitute, since they are not text fields. That is a live `2.4.7` failure, older than this pass and untouched by it, and it is the third of its kind found this way after `.dr-back` and `.dr-upload` (rev 84, rev 57). The pattern is worth naming: **every control this kit built by hand from a `<span>` or a `<div>` has had to be given its focus ring separately, and twice it was forgotten.**
- **2026-08-16 (rev 101)** — **The preference rows become a real picker: the menu opens, and the arrow stops travelling with the text.** The designer on the built `account-edit`, pointing at the Payment method and Language rows: *«зроби щоб відкривались dropdowns і іконка стрілка вниз в правому крайньому кутку незалежно від того який довжиною текст і іконку зміни на іншу»*. Three faults, all of them in the markup and none in the design. **(a) The control opened nothing.** The row was `<span class="dr-field__group" role="button" tabindex="0">` — a control that *announces* a menu and has none. That is `WCAG 4.1.2` at its plainest, and it had been true of both rows since the page was painted. **(b) The mark was not in the icon system.** It was the text glyph **▾** (U+25BE), a character in the running font, next to a kit whose every other arrow is a flat 24-grid outline at `stroke-width 1.7` declared once — `concept.md` §5's *icons are one system*, broken in the one place nobody re-read. **(c) The arrow travelled with the string.** The value and the glyph sat inline after the label, so the mark stood wherever the text stopped, not at the row's trailing edge — which is the whole of her third sentence. **It opens the way this product already opens things.** `.dr-picker` is a native `<details>` whose `<summary>` **is** the `.dr-field` — the mechanism `.dr-disclosure` has carried since 2026-08-02 and `account`'s mode picker has shipped since 2026-08-12: **in flow**, pushing the rows below it down, no floating surface, no scrim, no `position:fixed`. `HIG · Disclosure controls` describes exactly this shape, so the iOS pop-up button's floating menu is a **departure not taken** rather than a rule broken — and the kit's *nothing in this product floats* stands untouched at 40 screens. The options reuse the disclosure's own row: `.dr-picker__item` is declared **in the same block** as `.dr-disclosure__item`, one set of values for one shape, with only a `<button>` reset added — these options are a value, not a route. **The arrow is pinned by the shrink order, not by a magic number:** label `0 0 auto`, value `0 1 auto` with `min-width:0` and the ellipsis it already had, chevron `flex:none`. **And by `flex-wrap: nowrap`, which was found by measuring rather than by reasoning:** `.dr-field` wraps by default for the multiline area's label line, and on the 375 frame a 66-character value took the *wrap* instead of the *ellipsis* — the row grew **44 → 78** and the chevron fell to the second line, **325px** in from the edge, the exact failure she asked to fix. With `nowrap`: both rows **44 × 341**, chevron right edge **flush with the row's**, gap to edge **0**, on a 14-character value and a 56-character one alike, no horizontal overflow on the frame (`scrollWidth` = `clientWidth` = 373). **Measured pairs, all on the page or the menu card:** value `--ink` **15.99:1**, label and chevron `--slate` **6.62:1**, the menu `--card` **1.11:1** on the page, option `--ink` **14.37:1**, the current option's checkmark `--ink` **14.37:1** — rev 86's value, and above `1.4.11`'s 3:1 outright rather than on the colour-is-not-the-carrier reading. **The mark is on every option and shown on one**, held by `visibility` and never by absence, so no row moves when the pick changes; `aria-current="true"` travels with it (`WCAG 1.4.1`, `4.1.2`). **Keyboard, verified in the browser and not asserted:** the summary takes Enter with no script at all, Tab lands next on the first option, Enter there sets the value and closes the menu, focus returns to the row that now says the new value, Esc closes it; ring **`2.5px --ink` drawn inside** on the row and on the options alike (`WCAG 2.4.7`), both targets **44 × 341** (`HIG · 44pt` ✓, `WCAG 2.5.8` ✓ by 3.2×). The 30-line page script does **none** of the opening — it only makes the pick land, which is why the row still works with it removed. **Two things are reported, not decided.** The glyph itself is still the kit's chevron-down that turns 180° on open; `HIG · Pop-up buttons` would use `chevron.up.chevron.down`, and which one she wants is **her open question**, put to her with the build. And rev 46's *a form asking a question takes the circle* is not overturned here: this is the disclosure row's menu, which that entry exempts by name, so the checkmark stays — if she reads the row as a form question instead, `.dr-pick` is already in the kit and the swap is markup only. **`report-issue` still carries the same ▾ on its issue-type row and was not swept** — one page was asked for, and the twin is named here so it is a decision and not an oversight.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 102)** — **`payment`'s method line comes off the bar: the list states the method, the button states the amount.** The designer's word on the built screen: *«прибери цю фразу і суму напроти з бот бара — у нас є сума на кнопці»* ("take this phrase and the figure opposite it off the bottom bar — we have the amount on the button"), with a sweep of the project for the same shape, *«бо ми вже це фіксили на одному екрані»*. This **closes the first of the two boundary cases rev 53 left open**: `Pay with Apple Pay · ₴180` above `Pay ₴180` was a method line rather than a Total, held for her word since 2026-08-13, and it now goes the way the three Total lines went — the bar reduces to its block CTA, which is exactly what `DESIGN.md`'s action-bar spec describes (*`--page`, `--line` top, padding 12/16/24, gap 8, full-width buttons*). **Nothing on the screen was only there:** the **method** is carried by the checked radio in the list above (`Apple Pay`, first of three, its state exposed by a real `<input>` since rev 94), the **amount** by the screen's own figure under the *Amount* zone label (**₴180**) and again by the CTA's verb — so the reader still has *how* and *how much* before the commitment, `voice.md` **P3** intact. **Measured on the frame at 430 × 932, the line toggled live on the current kit, before → after:** the bar loses the `.dr-price` row (**23.8**, the 17/600 figure's line box; the 13/400 label's 18.19 sits inside it) plus the column's **8px** gap — **112.8 → 81px** — and `.dr-main` takes back precisely that, **597.2 → 629**. The CTA neither moves nor resizes: **44px**, so `HIG · 44pt target` is untouched. Content uses **336.56** of the column either way, free space below it goes **260.6 → 292.4**, and `scrollHeight == clientHeight` before (597 = 597) and after (629 = 629) — nothing scrolled, nothing scrolls, no zone reflows. **No §12 no-reflow contract is engaged:** `payment-error` and `payment-loading` carry buttons only and never drew this line, so no sibling state has to match it. **Conformance, plainly:** the removed line was static text restating facts stated elsewhere — not a control, so no target and no focus order is lost (`HIG`), and `WCAG 1.4.3` loses no pair that carried unique information (label `--slate` on `--page` **6.62:1**, figure `--ink` **15.99:1**, both merely repeated). For a screen-reader user the method now arrives through the radio's own checked state rather than through a sentence beneath it — `4.1.2` better served, not worse. **Kit consequence:** `.dr-price` is down to **one painted spend** — `time-slot`'s selection line — beside the grayscale `.price-line` on `withdraw`; `.dr-price--total` stays declared and spent nowhere, on the bench with `.dr-btn--text`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto`. **The sweep, and what it found across the 56 action-bar files:** one exact twin, **`withdraw`** (operator, grayscale) — `To Visa •••• 3318 · ₴3,240` above `Withdraw ₴3,240`, a destination line restating the account picked above it and the figure printed on its own button — **left as built and reported for her word**, `wireframes/` not being swept without it. **`time-slot` / `-empty` are a different case and are not proposed:** their line is the **only** place either screen states a price and the CTA is `Confirm slot` with no figure, so removing it would leave the screen with no price at all — the opposite of P3, and the reason is recorded here so it is a decision rather than an oversight. No third repeat of a figure between a bar line and its own button exists in the product. `microcopy.md` shed the string first; `ui/inventory.md`'s Price line row and log carry the same entry.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 103)** — **The field anatomy crosses to the grayscale layer, and the last two text fields still wearing a browser's own box are brought in.** The designer on the built `order-setup`: *«мені подобається як тут відпрацьовують інпути і текстові поля — тепер зроби по всьому проекту по усіх wireframes і стейтах так»*. **The audit first, because "the whole project" turned out to be three different situations, not one.** Text entry exists on **nine** screens. **Five were already done** — `order-setup`, `account-edit`, `contact-support`, `rate`, `report-issue` share `ui/kit.css`, so revs 98–100 had already reached every one of them and nothing was owed there. **Four were on the other layer entirely:** `operator-verification`, `operator-profile-setup`, `operator-dispute`, `result-upload` are still grayscale wireframes, and their `.control` still drew the box rev 98 removed on the painted side. **And two were neither** — single stragglers that no sweep had caught, described below. She was given the fork and **chose the grayscale rebuild** over painting the four pages: same anatomy, each layer in its own palette, so the grayscale-only rule of `_conventions.md` §4 holds and no colour enters a wireframe. **(a) The grayscale field, measured before and after.** The border that came off drew `1px solid --wf-border-2`, which composites to `#D9D9D9` — **1.42:1** against its own white fill and **1.29:1** against the `#F4F4F4` page. A drawn boundary is what identifies a control, so `WCAG 1.4.11` asks **3:1** and this failed by **2.1×**; nothing replaces it, and that is not a regression, because 1.4.11 governs a boundary that is **drawn**, not one that is **absent** — the same reading rev 98 wrote down. Measured on the rebuilt pages at the 375 frame: `border: 0px none` on all four, row **44px** (`HIG · 44pt target` ✓), label `15/400` `--wf-text-2` **6.27:1**, typed `--wf-text` **13.94:1**, the multiline ground `--wf-recessed` at `r6` with typed **13.21:1** — and `--wf-recessed` is not a new token but the one whose declared role in §4.1 has always read *"input field fill"*. Frame overflow **0** on all four. **(b) A `WCAG 1.4.3` failure older than this pass, fixed with it because the ground was moving anyway.** Every grayscale placeholder was `--wf-muted` `#909090` — **3.19:1** on white, under the **4.5:1** floor for text. Moved to `--wf-text-2`: **6.27:1** on the page, **5.95:1** on the area's ground. `_conventions.md` §4.1 now carries the caution, because the token itself is not at fault — it is correct on the uppercase placeholder-box label and the zone annotation, neither of which is part of the screen. **(c) The two stragglers, and they are the interesting part, because both were invisible to every audit that looked at CSS.** On `order-setup-error` the drop-off input carried **no class at all** — so on the one screen whose whole subject is that field, it rendered as a **native browser control**: measured in place, `border: 2px inset rgb(118,118,118)`, white fill, and **13.33px Arial** against `_conventions.md` §5's one-family rule, sitting in a plain list where no other field has a box. It also carried an inline `border-color:var(--wf-ink)` — a grayscale token on a painted page, pointed at a group whose border measures **0px**, so it had been styling nothing since rev 98. Now `dr-field__input`, measuring identically to its sibling: `0px none`, `15px` `-apple-system`, caret **15.99:1**, box **166 × 18** against the sibling's 166 × 18. On `.dr-search` the outline was killed with **nothing declared to replace it** — rev 100 set the caret rule on `.dr-field__input` and this selector was simply not carried with it. Same reading applied: `caret-color: --ink`, **13.33:1** on `--media`, verified computed. **What the pattern says, and it is the third time this log has said it:** rev 100 found `account-edit`'s hand-built `role="button"` rows with no focus ring; rev 84 and rev 57 found `.dr-back` and `.dr-upload` the same way. **A rule written against a class only reaches the elements that carry it** — the two found here were a missing class and a missed selector, not a wrong value, and neither would ever show up in a contrast table. **Reported and deliberately not acted on.** (i) `.field` (the grayscale search bar) and `.control.select` still hold the old bordered shape; both are **declared and applied to nothing**, so they are kept on the bench rather than retired — each names a real component with no page yet — and §7.1 now forbids using either as built. (ii) `.note` is `12px --wf-muted` on `--wf-bg` at **2.90:1**, a live `WCAG 1.4.3` failure on real body text (*"Review takes 2–3 business days."*, `operator-verification`) — same token, same criterion as (b), but it is not a field and `wireframes/` is not swept without her word. (iii) `order-setup-error`'s coverage message uses `.dr-field__hint` (`--slate`) where the kit declares `.dr-field__err` (`--danger-ink`, **6.14:1**) and applies it to nothing; `WCAG 3.3.1` passes either way, since the error is identified in text. *(Numbering note: this entry took **101** and stepped to **103** when a parallel session's `rev 101` (the picker row) and `rev 102` (`payment`'s action bar) landed in commits while it was being written. Third same-day collision, handled on the rev-99 precedent of this morning and the rev-55 precedent of 2026-08-15 — the later writer renumbers, the entry keeps its place in the order the work landed.)* | `wireframes/_wireframe.css`, `wireframes/_conventions.md` §4.1/§7.1, `wireframes/operator-profile-setup.html`, `wireframes/order-setup-error.html`, `ui/kit.css`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 104)** — **`withdraw` follows `payment` within the hour: on the operator side too, the bar is the button.** The twin rev 102's sweep found was put to the designer with the standards before the options — `HIG` asks nothing of an action bar that repeats its own button's amount, and `WCAG 1.4.3` loses no unique pair either way, so it was taste rather than rule — and of the three offered (take the whole line, keep the destination and drop the figure, keep as built) **her call was the first: take the whole line off**, the same edit `payment` had. `To Visa •••• 3318 · ₴3,240` comes off `withdraw`'s bar. **Both halves keep better carriers than the line was:** the **destination** is the checked radio in the list above — *Instant to card •••• 3318*, with its own note *Arrives in under a minute* — and the **amount** is the screen's `.amount` figure (**₴3,240**, subtitle *Full balance*) and the CTA's verb, **`Withdraw ₴3,240`**. So the operator still reads where the money lands and how much moves before the tap; what goes is the third telling of both, in the smallest type on the screen. **Measured at 430 × 932, the line toggled live — and the grayscale base behaves exactly as the painted kit did, to the tenth:** bar **112.8 → 81px** (the 23.8 `.price-line` row plus the 8px column gap), `main` **597.2 → 629**, CTA unmoved at **44px** (`HIG · 44pt target` ✓), `scrollHeight == clientHeight` before (597 = 597) and after (629 = 629), frame **812** either way — nothing scrolls, nothing reflows. `withdraw-error` and `withdraw-loading` never drew this line, so no sibling state has to match it. **Conformance:** static text restating facts stated elsewhere; not a control, so no target and no focus stop is lost, and for AT the destination now arrives through the radio's own checked state rather than a sentence beneath it (`4.1.2` better served). **Kit consequence:** the grayscale rule `footer.actionbar .price-line` (`wireframes/_wireframe.css:439–441`) is now **declared and spent nowhere** — left in the stylesheet rather than deleted, because `wireframes/` is not edited beyond what was asked, and it joins `.dr-price--total`, `.dr-btn--text`, `--danger` and `--sh-onphoto` on the bench. The painted `.dr-price` is down to **one spend**, `time-slot`'s selection line. **Both of rev 53's boundary cases are settled now:** `payment` (rev 102) and `withdraw` closed on her word; `time-slot` / `-empty` kept, because their line is the only place either screen states a price and the CTA `Confirm slot` carries no figure — taking it would leave the screen priceless, against `voice.md` **P3**. Across the product's **56 action-bar files**, no bar now repeats a figure its own button prints. `microcopy.md` shed the string first; `ui/inventory.md`'s Price line row reads the measured **1 · 1** and its log carries the same entry.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 102)** — **The picker's list leaves the page and becomes a drawer — and rev 101's in-flow disclosure is retired the same day it was built.** Shown the four ways iOS actually opens a list — a menu floating over the row (`HIG · Pop-up buttons`), Safari's own wheel tray under a native `<select>`, a pushed screen (`HIG · Lists and tables`), and the in-flow disclosure rev 101 had built — the designer chose the third and split it by length: *«для мобільного зроби 3 варіант, причому для [довгих] списків тап виводить новий екран що вїжджає справа, а для коротких виїжджає як дровер знизу»*, with the boundary confirmed at **six**. **The rule, and it is a count rather than a feeling: ≤ 6 options → a drawer from the bottom edge; ≥ 7 → a new screen pushed in from the right, with a back to the row.** `HIG · Action sheets` and `HIG · Lists and tables` draw the same line — a short mutually-exclusive set belongs in a sheet, a long one needs a screen that can scroll and search. **Nothing in the product is long enough yet** — payment is 4, language is 2 — so the second half is a written rule with no page, waiting with `--sh-onphoto` and `.dr-btn--text`; and when it gets one, that screen needs its `flows.md` node and its `sitemap.md` row **before** the HTML, per `_conventions.md`. **A correction was owed and is recorded here rather than buried:** the argument rev 101 leaned on — *nothing in this product floats* — had already been overtaken by `share`'s sheet and was repeated to the designer as though still true. It is not a description any more, it is a choice, and the kit page now says so. The drawer costs no new surface: `.dr-sheet`, `.dr-scrim`, `--sh-raised` and `--scrim` are the components rev 43 named for exactly this — *a floating surface belongs in a modal, popover, drawer or dropdown menu, and there it is appropriate.* **The glyph is `chevron.forward` for both halves of the rule**, never a down arrow: both say the same true thing, that the list is not here and arrives from elsewhere; and it does not turn, having nothing to turn into. **What survives from rev 101 unchanged is the part her first sentence was actually about** — the chevron pinned by the shrink order (label `0 0 auto`, value `0 1 auto` with `min-width:0` and the ellipsis, chevron `flex:none`) plus `flex-wrap: nowrap`, the rule found by measuring and not by reasoning. Re-measured on the button: both rows **44 × 341**, chevron flush with the row's trailing edge, gap **0**, on a 14-character value and a 56-character one alike, no frame overflow. **The row is now a real `<button>`** — Enter, Space, the tab order and the accessible name come from the element rather than from attributes, which is `WCAG 4.1.2` answered by construction — carrying `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded`. **The drawer, measured open:** sheet **373** wide, bottom flush with the frame, `--r-panel` **22** on the top corners, `--sh-raised`; scrim **373 × 810**, covering the nav bar and the action bar and not merely the scroll area. It rises `translateY(100%) → 0` over **`--dur-sheet` .32s** `--ease-out` — the one new token, because `--dur-base` .15s is the button's own tick and snaps on a surface that crosses 400px, while iOS presents a sheet at about a third of a second — with the scrim fading alongside, and both `animation: none` under reduced motion: the drawer's **presence** is the information, the rise is only how it gets there. **The modal contract is kept rather than claimed.** `aria-modal="true"` is a promise to assistive technology; `inert` on everything behind is what keeps it for everyone else, and it was verified rather than asserted — `#addr` cannot take focus while the drawer is up. Focus lands on the current option **with its ring** when the drawer is opened from the keyboard, Tab cycles inside the drawer only (eight presses, eight stops, all inside), and it closes **three ways** — Esc, the scrim, and a `Cancel` button, which `HIG · Action sheets` asks for by name. **`_conventions.md` gains the distinction that made Cancel right:** a modal sheet that fills the frame and carries a nav bar takes the `×` (`listings-filters`); a drawer that rises over the screen you are still on takes a grabber and a Cancel. **Two departures from the literal guideline, both deliberate.** `HIG · Pop-up buttons` would have put a floating menu at the row with a `chevron.up.chevron.down`; the drawer was chosen instead, and the glyph follows the behaviour that was built, not the one that was not. And rev 46's *a form asking a question takes the circle* still stands for a set laid out **in** the form — these options live in a menu that arrives and leaves, so they keep the current-item checkmark, `--ink` at **14.37:1**. **One process slip, recorded plainly:** the HTML went in before `_conventions.md` was updated, which inverts the `.md`-first rule; the document was corrected within the same revision rather than left to drift.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 105)** — **The support message field is six lines — and the floor that was supposed to set its height turns out never to have set anything.** The designer on the built `contact-support`, pointing at the message textarea: *«тут де потрібно залишити відгук зроби висоту поля вводу більшою щоб 6 стрічок влізло написати»*. **The measurement came before the fix, and it moved where the fix had to go.** The field rendered **69 × 341.4** in a **93px** row — two typed lines — and the obvious reading was that `--sz-area-min` **80px** (input floor `80 − 24` = **56**) was simply too small. It is not: **56 was never reached.** A `<textarea>` with no `rows` attribute defaults to **`rows="2"`**, and its intrinsic height — 2 × **22.5** (the 15px step at `--t-para-line` 1.5) + 2 × **12** inset — is **69px**, already above both the input's 56 and the row's 80. So every area in the product has been sized by an HTML default, and the two floors the kit declares and documents have governed **nothing** on any of the four compose screens since they were written. Both are **left as declared and reported**, not quietly re-cut to match reality — the same treatment `.dr-btn--text` and `--sh-onphoto` get on the bench. **This is the fourth time this log has recorded the same shape of finding** (rev 57, rev 84, rev 100, rev 103): *a value written in CSS only governs what nothing else already governs*, the cousin of *a rule written against a class only reaches the elements that carry it*. **The height is written in lines, not in pixels.** New modifier **`.dr-field--area6`**: `min-height: calc(6 × var(--t-heading-size) × var(--t-para-line) + 2 × var(--sp-snug))` = **159px**, row **183px** — so it is still six lines if the type step or the paragraph leading ever move, and it rides on the *input* rather than the row, per rev 54's rule that a floor on a wrapped row gets dealt out across the label's flex line. Precedent and pattern are `.dr-field--half`, which carries `rate`'s half-screen review the same way. **Verified in the browser, not asserted:** six lines of typed text fit with `scrollHeight == clientHeight`, a **seventh scrolls** — the count is the built behaviour and not arithmetic; computed `min-height: 159px`, `line-height: 22.5px`, ground `--media` `rgb(228,225,218)` at `--r-input` **10** and inset **12** all unchanged from rev 100, so nothing about the area's surface moves. **The screen absorbs it with room to spare:** the field grows **69 → 159** (+90), the form **165 → 255**, and `main` still does not scroll — `scrollHeight == clientHeight`, **161.3px** of slack left below the form on the 375 × 812 frame, frame **812**, horizontal overflow **0**. **Conformance, and both standards are quiet here.** `HIG · Text views` prescribes no line count — it asks that the view show a useful amount of content and scroll beyond it, which 6-then-scroll is exactly. `WCAG` sets no criterion on field height; **1.4.4 Resize text** is the one that could bite, and it does not — the area scrolls rather than clipping at 200%, and `resize:none` (rev 56) is on a field that is not the page's only path to its content. `2.5.8` / `HIG · 44pt` are cleared by **3.6×**. The pairs are untouched and re-read on the build: typed `--ink` **13.33:1**, placeholder `--slate` **5.51:1**, both on `--media` (`1.4.3` ✓). **`rows="2"` is left in the markup rather than raised to `rows="6"`** — the kit owns geometry in this project and a one-off number in one page's HTML is the thing `DESIGN.md` exists to prevent; the CSS floor wins over the intrinsic height either way. **Spent on `contact-support` alone, and the other two are her open call:** `order-setup`'s *Notes for the operator* and `report-issue`'s *What happened?* still render 69 / 93. Both were measured against the six-line floor before this entry was written and **both fit** — slack below the form would be **210.6px** on `order-setup` and **87.8px** on `report-issue`, no scroll on either — so the question is taste and not room, and `wireframes/` is not swept without her word. `rate` is unaffected: `--half` sets its own 382px floor and outranks nothing here. **Found beside the change and not acted on**, since neither is in this field's path: `ui/kit.css:992` carries an `<img>`-shaped placeholder flagged by the design hook, and `ui/kit.css:46` a **2px** radius outside the closed ramp of six — both older than this pass. | `ui/kit.css`, `wireframes/contact-support.html`, `DESIGN.md`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 106)** — **`Call support` leaves the message form and joins the action bar, and the bar carries three controls without the frame tightening.** The designer on the built `contact-support`, pointing at the secondary button: *«добав кнопку до ботом бару»*. **The move is a relocation and nothing else** — the string is untouched (`voice.md` A2 keeps `Call support` for the phone-only action, distinct from `Contact support` for messaging), the element stays a `<button type="button">` rather than becoming an `<a>`, and no token, radius or colour changes. It now sits **third in a descending stack**: green primary `Mark resolved`, secondary `Call support`, tertiary `Ask a senior agent` — the arrangement `contact-support-error` already uses for primary + secondary block, with `report-issue`'s tertiary-last ending. **Measured on the build, not asserted** (headless, cache cold): the action bar goes **133 → 185**, its `12 / 24` padding and `8` gaps unchanged, and all three controls render **341 × 44**. `.dr-main` gives back exactly what the bar takes — **577 → 525** — and the content, whose six-line field from rev 105 is **unmoved at 341 × 159**, ends at **419.08**, leaving **105.92px of slack** under the form: **no scroll on a 375 × 812 frame**, and the field did not have to shrink to pay for the button. **`HIG · 44pt target`** wants a tap target of at least 44 × 44; all three are **44.0**, and `--h-control` is a `min-height`, so a long label grows the button instead of squeezing it. **`WCAG 2.5.8 Target Size (Minimum)`** asks 24 × 24 at AA — cleared by **1.83×** on each side, with the 8px gap keeping the undershoot rule out of play. **No contrast pair is new, because the button did not change surface** — it sat on `--page` inside `.dr-main` and it sits on `--page` in the bar: `--ink` on `--btn2` **11.54:1**, beside `--ink` on `--green` **9.46:1** and `--slate` on `--page` **6.62:1**. **The green budget does not move:** `Mark resolved` is still the screen's only green control at **341 × 44 = 15,004px²** of a **304,500px²** frame — **4.93%**, inside §5's ~5% ceiling — because the button that joined it is grey. **One alternative was built out on paper and not taken, and it is hers:** `resolution.html` pairs its secondary and tertiary side by side in a `.dr-actionbar__row`, which would hold the bar at **133** and hand the 52px back to the page; it also halves each of those two targets' width to **166.5** and makes `Call support` visually equal to `Ask a senior agent` rather than a step above it.
