- **2026-08-16 (rev 107)** — **The six-line area crosses the compose set: `order-setup`'s notes and `report-issue`'s description join `contact-support`.** The designer on the call rev 105 deliberately left open to her: *«зроби так само 6 рядків»*. **Nothing new was written to say it** — both frames take the existing `.dr-field--area6`, so the height stays declared once, in tokens (`6 × --t-heading-size × --t-para-line + 2 × --sp-snug`), and three areas cannot drift apart on a later type change. **Measured after (headless, cache cold):** the input is **159 × 341.4** on both, content box **135.00 = 6.00 line boxes** at 22.5, six typed lines fit (`scrollHeight 159 == clientHeight 159`) and a seventh scrolls (**182 > 159**). **The row is not one number, because the label is not:** `order-setup`'s label is `sr-only`, so its row goes **93 → 183**; `report-issue`'s is drawn, so **124 → 214** (+21 label line, +10 gap). The same input in two rows is the field anatomy working, not a discrepancy to reconcile. **Neither screen pays for the height with a scroll:** `main` **630 / 578**, `scrollHeight == clientHeight`, **210.6px / 87.8px** of slack under the form, horizontal overflow **0** — both landing exactly on the figures rev 105 measured in advance, which is the whole point of having measured them then. **No contrast pair is new, because only a floor moved:** ground `--media` `rgb(228,225,218)`, `--r-input` **10**, inset **12**, typed **13.33:1**, placeholder **5.51:1**. **`HIG · Text views`** prescribes no line count — show a useful amount and scroll beyond it, which this does; **`WCAG`** sets no height criterion, and **`1.4.4 Resize text`** passes because the area scrolls rather than clips. **Still three of four, not four of four:** `rate` keeps `.dr-field--half` at **382**, already taller than six lines, and pulling it onto `--area6` would make the review field *shorter*. | `wireframes/order-setup.html`, `wireframes/report-issue.html`, `ui/kit.css`, `ui/inventory.md`, `DESIGN.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 108)** — **The payment picker gets brand marks, and the one-accent rule yields to four brand centres.** The designer on the built `account-edit`, pointing at the payment drawer: *«добав в усі види оплати з лівого краю іконки Visa, MasterCard, Apple pay, Google pay»*. **The question was never contrast, and stating that first is what made the choice a choice.** `WCAG 1.4.3` and `1.4.11` both *exempt* logotypes by name, so no ratio was owed on any mark in either direction; `1.1.1` and `HIG · 44pt` pass under every option. What actually collided was **§5's icon system** — flat outline, 24-grid, `stroke-width:1.7`, `currentColor`, no fill — against **Visa Brand Center, Mastercard Brand Center, `HIG · Apple Pay Marks and Buttons` and the Google Pay brand kit**, which each say the same thing: use the supplied artwork, in full colour or an approved one-colour variant, and do not redraw it. Charcoal marks would have kept §0's one-accent rule and **failed Visa and Mastercard**, whose one-colour variants are Visa Blue / white and the reversed symbol, not `#1A1A1A`. Three options went to her with that stated — full colour, charcoal, or a generic outline card glyph — and **her call was full brand colour**. **Recorded as a departure, not corrected and not generalised.** Ten colours outside the palette now land on one list: `#1434CB` · `#EB001B` `#F79E1B` `#FF5F00` · `#000` · `#4285F4` `#EA4335` `#FBBC05` `#34A853` `#5F6368`. They are written **inline on the artwork and are not tokens**, scoped to `.dr-paymark`, and no DRON surface may pull them. **The green budget is untouched** — the drawer holds no green control, so §5's ~5% ceiling is not spent here at all. **Measured anyway, because §07 measures everything, and four of them would fail if the shape were ours:** on `--card` `rgb(236,233,228)` — Apple `#000` **17.34:1**, Visa **7.28:1**, Google's wordmark `#5F6368` **5.00:1**, Mastercard `#EB001B` **3.81:1**, Google `#EA4335` **3.24:1**, `#4285F4` **2.94:1**, Mastercard `#FF5F00` **2.52:1**, Google `#34A853` **2.52:1**, Mastercard `#F79E1B` **1.76:1**, Google `#FBBC05` **1.41:1**. They pass by exemption and, to the point, **by redundancy**: the brand *name* is written beside every mark in `--ink` at **14.37:1**, the artwork is `aria-hidden`, and colour therefore carries nothing on its own (`1.4.1`, `1.1.1`). **A fixed box, so a row does not depend on how wide a logo happens to be.** All four marks sit in a **40 × 20** box, each anchored left inside it; measured on the build at 430 × 932, every mark box starts at **x = 16** (the row's own `--sp-screen`) and **every label starts at x = 64**, all four rows **44px** (`HIG · 44pt target` ✓, unmoved), horizontal overflow **0**. **One defect was created by the change and caught by it.** The row's value is read back into the closed field with `textContent`, and three of the four marks now contain `<text>` — so picking Visa would have written *«VISAVisa •••• 4921»* into the field. Every option is now wrapped in **`.dr-picker__label`**, the script reads that node, and both drawers carry the span so the block has one rule rather than two; verified live, picking Google Pay writes **"Google Pay"**. **Written down as an approximation, which is the honest status.** The Apple and Google symbols are the brands' own path data; the three wordmarks (`VISA`, and `Pay` twice) are set in `--font-ui`, not each brand's proprietary face, and `.dr-paymark text` is **the only place in the kit that sets type inside an SVG**. At handoff all four are replaced by artwork downloaded from the brand centres — that is a task, not a defect, and it is recorded here so it is not discovered later. **Reported, not swept:** the **closed** row above the drawer still reads `Payment method · Visa •••• 4921` with **no mark**, and `payment.html`'s radio list (`Apple Pay` / `Google Pay` / `Visa •••• 4921`) is the same list on the pay step, also unmarked. Both are the obvious twins and both are hers to call — `wireframes/` is not swept without her word. **Found beside the change and untouched**, both older than this pass: `ui/kit.css:46` carries a **2px** radius outside the closed ramp of six, and `ui/kit.html:236` a `4<` radius; the design hook flags both. | `wireframes/account-edit.html`, `ui/kit.css`, `ui/kit.html`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 109)** — **The Evidence field loses its label and its separator: the well is the field.** Two designer calls on the built `report-issue`, given together on the same row: *«видали "evidence"»* and *«видали лінію девайдер і так зрозуміло що там поле вводу а нижче загрузка»*. **(a) The label named nothing, and had never named anything.** `<label class="dr-field__label" for="ev">Evidence</label>` pointed at an id that does not exist in the document — verified in the browser, not read off the markup: of the page's two `for` attributes, **both** are orphans (`Evidence→ev`, `Issue type→it`). So the row label was a heading to the eye and, to a screen reader, a label attached to no control. Taking it off **closes** that defect rather than opening one, and the zone's accessible name never came from it: `aria-label="Add a photo or note"` is unchanged (`WCAG 4.1.2` ✓, `1.3.1` ✓ — one orphan of two now gone). **What carries `WCAG 3.3.2` is the second half of the criterion — *labels **or** instructions*.** The zone keeps its visible line, **"A photo makes your claim stronger. Order #DR-4821."**, which names the object to attach, above it the 32px tray glyph (`--slate` on the `--media` well, **5.51:1**), and `2.5.3 Label in Name` stops applying the moment there is no visible text label to disagree with the name. **Reported and deliberately not fixed, because it is hers to decide:** what a sighted reader gives up is the **verb**. `account-edit`'s two zones carry theirs *inside* the well (`Add drone documents` / `Add insurance`, `.dr-upload__action`), and **rev 60 kept `report-issue`'s `--media` well for exactly this reason** — *«it carries no title line, so the well is still doing work there»*. That sentence is now load-bearing: the well and the glyph are the whole of what identifies the control to the eye. If she wants the verb back it belongs **inside the zone** as `.dr-upload__action`, not as the row label that just came off. **(b) The separator is keyed to the control, not swept off the page.** `.dr-field:has(> .dr-upload)::before{content:none}` — it matches **one field in the product**, the only `.dr-upload` that lives inside a `.dr-field` (account-edit's two are `.dr-upload--card` and stand on their own). Measured, the line it removes was 1px `--line` on `--page` = **1.34:1** — below `WCAG 1.4.11`'s 3:1 and permitted there only because a list separator is decoration and never the carrier of structure, which is also why removing it costs the criterion nothing. **Her argument measures:** the description's well ends at **473.19** (`--media`, **1.20:1** on `--page`, `--r-input` 10) and the upload's well begins at **485.19** (`--media`, **1.20:1**, `--r-media` 13) — two drawn shapes **12px** apart, where before there was one 1px line between a label and a well. `1.3.1` is untouched by construction: the grouping is the DOM, not the paint. `HIG · Lists and tables` draws a separator between plain-list rows, so this is a **departure recorded with its reason** — neither of these two rows is a plain list row; both are filled wells with their own edge. **The row above keeps its line:** `Issue type` is a bare row with no shape of its own, which is the case the separator is for. **(c) The zone is not sized by its string.** With the label gone the well became the only item in the flex row and would have shrunk to the hint's measure — rev 57's lesson arriving one page later — so `flex-basis:100%` is declared beside the rule. **Target unchanged at 341 × 140** (`HIG · 44pt` ✓, `WCAG 2.5.8` ✓ by ~7×). **Composition, measured on the 375 × 812 frame:** the field **171 → 140px** (the 21px label line and the 10px row gap), the form column **664.2 → 633.2**, slack under the form **49.8 → 80.8px**; `scrollHeight == clientHeight` (**577 = 577**) before and after, so nothing scrolled and nothing scrolls. **Strings:** one row leaves `microcopy.md` — `report-issue · Report form · Evidence · Field label`. **States:** `report-issue-empty` and `-loading` carry neither the label nor the field group, so no sibling has to match. **Not swept, and named so it is not re-found as new:** `operator-dispute` still carries a grayscale `Evidence` field label — a different screen and persona, and `wireframes/` is not swept without her word. **Found while measuring and reported, not fixed:** `report-issue`'s `Issue type` row is still the shape rev 101 retired on `account-edit` — a `<span class="dr-field__group">` that announces and opens nothing, carrying the text glyph **▾** (U+25BE) instead of the kit's 24-grid chevron, plus the second orphan `for`. `WCAG 4.1.2` / `1.3.1`, and `concept.md` §5's *icons are one system*.

- **2026-08-16 (rev 110)** — **The city leaves the nav bar and becomes a three-level place pick inside the filter — and the `≥ 7` half of rev 102's picker rule finally gets its first pages.** The designer on the built `listings` top bar: *«помісти вибір міста у фільтр де юзер обирає регіон, спочатку він має обрати місто область потім вже район міста»*. **Depth was hers and was asked before anything was built** — two steps (City ▸ District) or three (Region ▸ City ▸ District), shown as the two row-stacks side by side; **her choice is three**. **Conformance came before the options and the honest answer was that nothing was failing.** `HIG · Navigation bars` says the bar shows where you are and moves you, so a filter control does not belong in it — but neither `HIG` nor `WCAG` was being broken by the button, and that was said plainly rather than dressed up as a defect. What *was* a real finding is that `_conventions.md` §12 had already written that on a listing screen **the mark stands alone**, and `Kyiv, UA ▾` had been sitting beside it undocumented on five pages; the move brings the build back to its own rule rather than making a new one. **The rule that had been waiting since rev 102 is now spent:** `≥ 7` options push a screen, and the lists are **25 regions** and **11 districts**, so three pages arrive — `listings-filter-region`, `-city`, `-district` — with their `flows.md` nodes and their `sitemap.md` row written **first**, which is the condition rev 102 attached to them. **Two calls the first build forced, both recorded as decisions and not as defects.** **(a) A dependent list takes one shape for all its values.** The count rule was written for lists the product fixes forever (payment 4, language 2); a dependent list's length is *data* — City holds **one** row under `Kyiv City` and forty under `Lviv Oblast`. Letting the same row open a drawer or a screen depending on the parent would make one control behave two ways, against `HIG · Consistency`, so all three levels are screens and the departure from the literal count is written down. **(b) Search is a measurement, not a feeling:** Region is 25 × 44 = **1100px** in a ~640px area, so it scrolls and takes the search field `HIG · Lists and tables` asks for; District is **484px** and fits whole, so a search field there would have no work to do. **One kit rule was narrowed, and the narrowing was verified before it was made.** rev 98 stripped the card from a field group because *«мені не подобається обводка в полях вводу»* — a sentence about rows that are **typed into**. A group of rows that are only **tapped** is iOS Settings, which is a stack of grouped inset cards. So `.dr-rows:has(> .dr-field)` splits: every field group still gives up its horizontal padding, but only a group holding a **non-action** field gives up the card. Checked first that `.dr-field--action` lives on exactly one page, `account-edit`, whose Preferences group carries an `<input>` between its two action rows — so it still matches and still renders the plain list. **Measured after: `account-edit` unmoved — both action rows 326.2 × 44, chevron gap 0, the input row 326.2 × 44 at padding-left 0, group transparent at radius 0.** The Location group keeps its card and lands on the same axis as its neighbours: **`#ECE9E4`, `--r-card` 16, x 122.9, w 326.2 — identical to the Time and Price cards**, rows **44** each, chevron on the card's 16 inset on all three. **A real defect was found by measuring and it was invisible to the eye.** `.dr-main` is a flex column of fixed height, so the region list's default `flex-shrink:1` squeezed it from its natural **1100 → 618.4**, and with `.dr-picker__list`'s `overflow:hidden` above it **11 of the 25 regions were clipped** — while `main.scrollHeight === clientHeight`, so nothing could be scrolled to them. Eleven options unreachable by any input. Fixed with `.dr-main > .dr-picker__list{ flex: none; }`, scoped so the **drawer's** copy inside `.dr-sheet` is untouched (verified: it keeps `flex-shrink: 1`). After: list **1100**, main scrolls **1192 > 710**, last row *Zhytomyr Oblast* reachable at **44**, no sideways scroll, frame still **812**. It is the same `flex-shrink` trap `_conventions.md` records for `listings-loading`, arriving from the other direction. **The rows are `<a>`, not `<button aria-haspopup>`** — a link goes somewhere, a button acts here — so the accessible name is the label plus the value (*Region, Kyiv City*) and `WCAG 4.1.2` is answered by the element; `.dr-field--action` gained `text-decoration:none`, the one thing a `<button>` never needed. Back is the kit's 44 × 44 chevron with `aria-label="Back to Filters"`, titles centred at **0.00px** off and unclipped. **The district names changed with the level, and this is not `voice.md` P4 being overruled:** the flat filter read *Podil / Obolon / Pechersk*, which are **neighbourhoods**, and Podil is one part of Podilskyi raion. The control now selects a district under a named city, so the rows carry the districts' own names — the user's word for *this* control. The applied chip on `listings-filtered` follows to **Podilskyi**, and it is where the location now shows, the bar having given it up. **One thing left open and reported rather than decided:** under `Kyiv City` the City screen is a picker with **one** option — honest to the data, thin as a screen. It carries a plain line and a `Change region` button so it is not a dead end, but whether that row should instead be **disabled** when its region holds one city is hers. **Numbering, said out loud:** this was written as rev 107 and renumbered to 110 on finding that parallel sessions had taken 107–109 the same day; their entries were left alone, on rev 92's rule that silently renumbering someone else's entry is worse than the collision. | `wireframes/listings-filters.html`, `wireframes/listings-filter-region.html`, `wireframes/listings-filter-city.html`, `wireframes/listings-filter-district.html`, `wireframes/listings.html`, `wireframes/listings-empty.html`, `wireframes/listings-error.html`, `wireframes/listings-loading.html`, `wireframes/listings-filtered.html`, `wireframes/_wf-shell.js`, `ui/kit.css`, `wireframes/_conventions.md`, `wireframes/_screens.md`, `wireframes/_critique.md`, `sitemap.md`, `flows.md`, `microcopy.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 111)** — **The picker rule goes project-wide: every dropdown in the product now opens, and the behaviour is written once instead of five times.** The designer: *«зроби дропдауни по всьому проекту на мобілці як ми домовились — малий список відкривається дровером знизу, великий сторінкою, усі можливі дропдауни що є на проекті»*. **The inventory first, because a sweep that is not counted is not a sweep.** Every control in `wireframes/` that reads as a dropdown — a row with a value and a mark that promises a list — was found by four separate searches (the text glyph `▾`/`&#9662;`, the chevron-down path `M5 9l7 7 7-7`, `.dr-field__chev`, and `<select>`, of which the product has none). **Six controls on seven pages, and this is the whole set:** `account-edit`'s payment and language (already converted, rev 102), `order-setup` and `order-setup-error`'s **Parcel size**, `report-issue`'s **Issue type**, `account`'s **Mode**, and `operator-account`'s Mode. Already conforming and left alone: `listings-filters`'s **Region · City · District**, which are the ≥ 7 half of the rule already built — three rows that push three real screens. **Five converted, one deliberately not.** `operator-account` is a **grayscale** page — it does not link `ui/kit.css` — and `_conventions.md` §3 defers colour, icons and shadow on an unpainted wireframe. Putting a painted drawer on it would break that contract, and inventing a grayscale drawer would add a component nobody chose. It is named here so the gap is a decision and not an oversight; it converts the day the page is painted, or sooner on her word. **Counts, and every one is under the six:** parcel size 3, issue type 3, mode 2, payment 4, language 2. Not one list in the product reaches seven, so the drawer half of the rule carries the entire sweep and the screen half stays exactly where it already was, on the filter. **THE BEHAVIOUR MOVES OUT OF THE PAGE AND INTO `ui/kit.js` — the kit's first script.** The kit is CSS, and it stays CSS with one named exception: a floating surface that opens, traps focus, makes the screen behind it unreachable and hands the focus back cannot be built out of stylesheets. Written five times it is five chances to drift, so it is written once, and the pages that carry a picker link it. Its markup contract is documented at the top of the file; `account-edit`'s inline copy was deleted in the same pass. **One bug the move surfaced, and it is a real one:** the naive `item.textContent` reads a wordmark drawn with SVG `<text>` — rev 108's Visa mark makes the row say *"VISAVisa •••• 4921"*. The shared script reads `.dr-picker__label` and falls back to the item only when there is none. **An option is a `<button>` when it is a value and an `<a>` when it is a route** — the distinction the mode picker forced. Its two options *leave the screen*, so they navigate and nothing is written back into the row; every other picker sets the value, moves `aria-current` and closes. The script knows the difference from the tag, not from a flag. **`account`'s mode picker changes anatomy, not meaning:** the in-flow `.dr-disclosure` becomes a row — label **Mode**, value **Client** — and its two routes move into the drawer with their copy intact. The row sits in a group of pure action rows, so **rev 107's own rule gives it the card** and the chevron stands at the card's 16px inset rather than at the frame edge: measured **44 × 341**, gap **16**, which is `HIG · Lists and tables`' grouped inset list exactly. This leaves **`.dr-disclosure` with no user in the product** — recorded, not deleted; it goes to the bench with `--sh-onphoto` and `.dr-btn--text`, because it still names a real shape. **`report-issue` loses a line of copy, and the loss is the point.** *"Also: Damaged item · Wrong location"* sat under the row **because the picker did not open** — the only place those two types could be shown. A caption that lists a control's own options is a caption for a control that does not work; the drawer holds them now. This also closes what **rev 110 found and reported without fixing** the same day. **Measured across all five, and identical on every one:** row **44 × 341**, `<button>`, chevron flush at the row's trailing edge (gap **0**; **16** on `account`, whose row is in a card), no frame overflow, drawer and scrim hidden at rest, options **44** tall, focus landing on the current option, everything behind `inert`, closing by Esc / scrim / Cancel with the focus returned. **Zero console errors on all five.** **New copy, recorded in `microcopy.md`:** *Medium — up to 5 kg* and *Large — up to 10 kg* — the parcel sizes the picker needed and the product had never written down, since only *Small — up to 2 kg* existed as a value with no siblings.
- **2026-08-16 (rev 112)** — **A button on the bottom edge is a filled button: the five ghosts in the action bars take the secondary fill, and the rule is written down.** The designer on the built `report-issue`, pointing at *Submit without evidence*: *«зроби як секондарі сіра кнопка. проглянь проект де є в ботом барі дві або 3 кнопки вони мають бути filled як праймері і секондарі»* — so the call was hers twice over: the one control, and the rule behind it. **Conformance first, and the honest answer is that nothing was failing.** The tertiary's label was `--slate` on `--page` at **6.62:1** (grayscale `--wf-text-2` on `--wf-surface` at **6.90:1**), both over `WCAG 1.4.3`'s 4.5:1, and the target was already **341 × 44** — `HIG · 44pt` ✓. `1.4.11` was never owed on either shape: a text-labelled control is identified by its label, so a fill of 1.39:1 and a border of 1.41:1 are exempt in the same sentence that exempts having no fill at all. **What the change buys is not contrast, it is affordance** — `HIG · Buttons` asks that a control look tappable, and on the bottom edge a borderless line reads as the caption under the button rather than as the second control, which is exactly the confusion between *Submit report* and *Submit without evidence* that the frame had. That argument is stated as the reason, because the ratios are not one. **Five controls on the bottom edge, and this is the whole set** — found by reading every `footer.dr-actionbar` and `footer.actionbar` in `wireframes/`, not by guessing: painted `report-issue` *Submit without evidence* and `resolution` *Contact support*, grayscale `operator-dispute` *Payment still held?*, `operator-fee-terms` *Maybe later*, `operator-verification-loading` *Back to Jobs*. **Not one pixel of geometry moves, by construction:** `.dr-btn--tertiary` and `--secondary` differ in `background` and `color` and in nothing else, and `footer.actionbar .btn{width:100%}` had already been giving the grayscale ghosts the full column — so `--block` was added for the markup to say what the CSS was doing, not to change it. **Measured after (headless, 375 frame):** painted secondaries **341 × 44**, `--r-btn` 12, 15/600, `--btn2` `#D6D2C9` fill with `--ink` label at **11.54:1**, no shadow — the primary keeps the system's one shadow, so the pair still differs in two signals and not only in hue (`WCAG 1.4.1`). `resolution`'s row is two secondaries at **166.5 × 44** each, both labels **11.54:1**, no overflow. Grayscale secondaries **341 × 44**, `r6`, `--wf-text` `#252525` on `--wf-surface` at **15.33:1** inside the 1px `--wf-border-2` box, against a `#111111` primary at **18.88:1** — the two are 18.88 vs 1.00 apart in fill, so the grayscale layer separates them by value as §4 requires. `overflowX 0` on all five; `main.scrollHeight == clientHeight` on four, and `operator-dispute` scrolled before this pass and scrolls the same **758 > 577** after, the form being long and untouched. **Green is untouched:** the new fills are warm grey, so `report-issue` and `resolution` still spend exactly one green control each and §5's ~5% ceiling is not approached. **The rule is now in the two contracts rather than in this entry** — `_conventions.md` §11 and `DESIGN.md` § Buttons both say a bar with two or three actions is primary + secondary (+ a second secondary), never a ghost, and both name where tertiary still belongs: the top-bar action (`listings-filters` · *Clear all*) and links inside a card. **The one screen the rule did not cover was asked about and answered in the same pass.** `operator-dispute-error`'s action bar holds a **single** button — *Back to Jobs*, the screen's only exit — and her call is **primary**: the only button on the bottom edge is the main action by definition, even when it is only a way out. It is also what the grayscale set already did everywhere else a bar held one control (`inspection-report-error`, `job-brief`, `withdraw`). **Measured after: 341 × 44, `r6`, `--wf-ink` `#111111` fill, white label at 18.88:1, `overflowX 0`, `main.scrollHeight == clientHeight` (629 = 629).** Both contracts carry the one-button half of the rule, and with it **the grayscale layer draws no tertiary at all** — the variant survives only where it belongs, on the painted top bar (`listings-filters` · *Clear all*). | `wireframes/report-issue.html`, `wireframes/resolution.html`, `wireframes/operator-dispute.html`, `wireframes/operator-fee-terms.html`, `wireframes/operator-verification-loading.html`, `wireframes/operator-dispute-error.html`, `wireframes/_conventions.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `ui/inventory.md`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 113)** — **The place pick gets a fourth level: the filter can finally say which country it is filtering.** The designer on the built `listings-filters`, pointing at the Location group: *«добав ще Cauntry»*. **The row was not a missing option so much as a hidden constant.** `CLAUDE.md §Geography` is *Ukraine **and** European countries*, and the panel stated the country **nowhere** and could change it **nowhere** — Region, City and District were all Ukrainian by assumption, which is a filter with an unfilterable premise inside it. **Three things the existing rules decided before I did, and one that was hers.** Position (broadest first, above Region) and the reset cascade (a country clears the region, the city and the district) follow the rule already written for region→city→district; the level takes **its own pushed screen** because `_conventions.md`'s picker rule is a count *and* because the same day's dependent-list precedent says every level of one dimension takes the form its **longest** case needs — so all four match whatever any one of them holds. What was hers: **what the list holds**, because `CLAUDE.md` names no countries and Region's list is a **coverage** list (*where DRON operates · 25*), so inventing an atlas would have invented a business fact. Three readings went to her — a coverage set, Ukraine alone, or all ~44 of Europe — and **her call was a coverage set of 8**. The rejected reading is recorded so it is not re-proposed: the **full atlas asserts nothing about coverage but lets a client pick a country DRON does not serve and land in `listings-empty`** — a dead end dressed as a choice. **The eight are a placeholder set** (Czechia, Germany, Hungary, Moldova, Poland, Romania, Slovakia, Ukraine) pending the real launch markets, and that is written into the page rather than left to be assumed. **Measured before the build and again after, and the prediction held to the pixel.** The list is **8 × 44 = 352px** in a **710** main area — `scrollHeight 710 == clientHeight 710`, so the screen does **not** scroll and takes **no search field**, which is the same measurement that gives `listings-filter-region` one (25 rows, 1100px) and `listings-filter-district` none (11 rows, 484px). Exactly **one** mark is visible of eight, `--ink` **14.37:1** on `--card` — the row ink re-read on the build, not copied. Frame **812**, horizontal overflow **0**, no page errors. **The panel absorbs the row without a new problem, because it already had the old one:** `listings-filters`' main was **577 client / 809 scroll** *before* this change — a sheet that already scrolled by 232px — and is **577 / 874** after. The Location group goes **148 → 192** (+44, the row itself); the remaining **+21** is the zone **annotation** label wrapping to a second line, not product. **Order is alphabetical with the current pick in its own place**, carrying the mark — the way `Kyiv City` sits among the 25 regions. The approved preview showed Ukraine floated to the top; it is not, and the reason is `HIG · Consistency` plus the sibling screens: a list that reorders itself around the pick cannot be scanned the same way twice. **`.md` went first, as the content rule requires** — `flows.md` (two new nodes and an edge), `sitemap.md`, `wireframes/_screens.md`, `wireframes/_conventions.md` §12, `microcopy.md` — and the HTML followed. The page is registered in `_wf-shell.js` and in the state switch of every sibling that carries one. **One string was written and then deleted before it shipped:** a note under the list saying *"Changing the country clears the region, the city and the district."* It is true and it is the most expensive reset of the four — but **Region resets two levels and says nothing**, so adding it to Country alone would have made one level speak and three stay silent. **Reported instead, and hers to call: the cascade reset is invisible on all four levels.** **Two other things found and left alone, both older than this pass:** `listings-filters`' own state switch lists `filtered / empty / error / loading` but **none** of the place-pick sub-views, where region, city, district and `listings` all list them; and the annotation column's 6px / 8px radii sit outside the closed ramp of six on every wireframe page — the design hook flags them, and §4 puts that column outside the product. | `wireframes/listings-filter-country.html` *(new)*, `wireframes/listings-filters.html`, `flows.md`, `sitemap.md`, `wireframes/_screens.md`, `wireframes/_conventions.md`, `microcopy.md`, `wireframes/_wf-shell.js`
- **2026-08-16 (rev 114)** — **The city level's way out moves to the bottom edge: an escape that was floating in the flow becomes the screen's action bar.** The designer on the built `listings-filter-city`, pointing at *Change region*: *«зроби у ботом барі кнопку»*. **What the button was doing there in the first place.** Under `Kyiv City` the City level holds **one row**, so without a route to a different set of cities the screen is a dead end — `_conventions.md`'s *no dead-ends* rule. Rev 113's build gave it that route as an inline `.dr-btn--secondary` under the note, which put the screen's only control at **y 281 on an 812 frame** — three quarters of the height above the thumb, and at a height that is not fixed but is wherever the list happens to end. **Conformance first, and the honest answer is again that nothing was failing.** The label was `--ink` on `--btn2` at **11.54:1** (`WCAG 1.4.3` asks 4.5) and the target was already **341 × 44** (`HIG · 44pt` ✓) — both before and after, because `.dr-actionbar` changes where the button is and not what it is. `WCAG 1.4.11` is not owed on the fill for the reason rev 112 wrote down: a text-labelled control is identified by its label, so **1.39:1** of fill against `--page` is exempt, and the ratio is identical in the bar and in the flow since `.dr-actionbar` and `.dr-main` stand on the same ground. What the move buys is `HIG · Buttons` and `HIG · Layout` — **a screen's action belongs on a fixed bottom edge within reach**, which is where the other 26 `footer.dr-actionbar` pages in the product already keep theirs, and the bar adds a `--line` hairline the inline button never had. **Secondary, not primary, and that is the rule rather than a preference:** the screen's real action is picking a row, so green is not spent on an escape — the frame carries **zero** green fills before and after, and §5's one-green-control ceiling is not approached. `_conventions.md` §11 supplies the rest: a button on the bottom edge is filled, never a ghost. **The note does not follow it down.** *"Kyiv City holds one city. To serve elsewhere, change the region."* explains the **list**, so it stays in `main` with the list; the bar carries the control and nothing else, which is true of every action bar in the product — not one holds prose. The sentence and the label still connect, verbally: the label repeats the sentence's last two words. **Measured after (headless, 375 frame):** bar **373 × 81**, ground `--page` `#F7F5F2`, `1px` `#D9D5CE` hairline on top, padding **12 / 16 / 24**; button **341 × 44** at **x 16** inset, `--r-btn` **12**, 15/600, `--btn2` `#D6D2C9` with `--ink` at **11.54:1**, no shadow, **25px** clear of the frame's bottom edge. `main` goes **710 → 629** and still does not scroll — `scrollHeight 629 == clientHeight 629` — so the one-row list needs no search field and gains no overflow, which was the measurement rev 113 used to decide the same thing. Frame **812** exactly (bar bottom 811 + hairline), horizontal overflow **0**, zero page errors. **Only this level changes, and that is not an inconsistency.** `listings-filter-country`, `-region` and `-district` have **no note and no escape button** — they never needed one, their lists being 8, 25 and 11 rows — so they get no action bar either. The bar appears where the data produces it, which is the same rule that governs a state page. **Three findings the design hook raises on this file and none is product:** `6px`, `6px`, `8px` radii at L47 / L55 / L63 are the **annotation column** (`.meta .flow`, `.state-switch a`, `.wf-frame`), which `_conventions.md` §4 puts outside the product and rev 113 already reported on every wireframe page; unchanged. **`microcopy.md` is untouched on purpose** — the string, its screen and its type are all the same; only its position moved, and the master table's zone column does not track the action bar (`listings-filters`' *Apply filters* is filed under *Location*). | `wireframes/listings-filter-city.html`, `wireframes/_conventions.md`

- **2026-08-16 (rev 114)** — **The value axis reaches the card it was copied from: `account-edit`'s Preferences group, the reference rev 97 swept the whole product on, was itself standing on three left edges at once.** The designer on the built page, pointing at *Visa •••• 4921*: *«зроби вирівнювання тексті з Podil&English по лівому краю»*. **Rev 97 moved 46 fact rows onto a 140 column on the strength of this card and never measured the card itself.** Measured before, headless at the 375 frame (a 326 row — the classic scrollbar; an overlay-scrollbar browser reports 341, and every *trailing* figure below moves with that width while the axis after it does not): the two picker values carried `margin-left:auto`, so their left edge stood wherever their string ended — *Visa •••• 4921* at **192.84**, *English* at **244.86** — while the typed *Podil, 21* sat on `.dr-field`'s 1:1 split at **168**. Three rows, three axes, **76.86px** apart, in one card. **One axis only holds if the column is fixed**, and that is why this could not be answered by moving the values: the rows have different trailing anatomy — two carry a chevron, one does not — so `flex:1` on the label leaves **152.5** on a picker row against **165.5** on an input row, and “left-aligned” would still have been two axes. **Put to her as a taste call with both options conforming** — `HIG · Lists and tables` ships the trailing value, `HIG · Text fields` the leading one, and `1.4.3` (key `--slate` 5.95:1, value `--ink` 14.37:1), `1.3.1` and `1.4.8` are untouched by either, `1.4.8` governing justified text and not an axis: **(a)** `--sz-rowkey` **140**, the column the fact row already stands on, or **(b)** `calc(50% - 5px)`, the split *Podil, 21* was already on, which moves nothing in the reference row. **Her call is (a)**, so `.dr-field__label` goes `flex:1` → `flex: 0 0 var(--sz-rowkey)` and `.dr-field--action`'s two overrides retire — the label no longer needs `0 0 auto`, and the value takes `flex:1` in place of `0 1 auto` + `margin-left:auto`. **The product now has one value axis: read, typed and chosen all start 150 from their row's left edge.** **Measured after, before → after:** `account-edit` **192.84 / 168 / 244.86 → 150 / 150 / 150**; `account`'s *Mode* **254.53 → 166** and `listings-filters`' four place rows **197.23–252.08 → 166** (those two groups hold only action rows, so by rev 107 they keep their card and its 16 — 166 in the frame is the same 150 in the row); `order-setup` and `order-setup-error` **175.5 / 177.83 → 150**; `report-issue`'s *Issue type* **83.05 → 150**; and the fact rows on `order-review`, `tracking` and `account` are unmoved at key **0** / value **150**, which is the point. **The typed track widens where it was needed most:** 165.5 → **191** on the two address rows that carry the longest strings in the product. **Nothing wraps and nothing overflows** — every affected row still measures **44**, the widest field label is now *Payment method* at **135.64** and then *Drop-off address* at **134.47**, both inside the 140 that the fact row's own widest key (*First time as Operator*, 133.34) sized, **4.36px** clear; `order-setup-error`'s 73.59 drop-off row is its `.dr-field__hint` and is identical before and after; no page scrolls sideways. **One cost, shown to her before the change and recorded rather than corrected:** `report-issue`'s *Poor quality — not as expected* measures **231.95** and fit its old 239 track by 7px; the fixed column leaves it **165**, so it now takes the ellipsis `.dr-field__value` has always declared. **One row is left off the axis and is hers to call:** `share`'s *Referral link* carries an inline `style="margin-left:auto"` on its value and so still stands at **193.88** — the only field value in the product not on the axis. It is a one-line edit inside `wireframes/`, which is not touched without her word. | `ui/kit.css`, `DESIGN.md`, `ui/inventory.md`, `concept/concept.md` rev 114

- **2026-08-16 (rev 115)** — **The save-address row leaves the flow for the bottom edge: the product's first action bar that carries a control which is not a button.** The designer on the built `order-setup`, pointing at *Save this address for next time*: *«це так відірвано я думаю було б логічно розмістити біля ботом бару що про це каже HIG»*. **What "відірвано" was measuring, before any opinion was offered.** The row stood **12px** under the input group (`--sp-snug`, the form's own gap) and **226.6px** above the action bar, with **239.4px** between it and the button — twelve above, two hundred below. It was also the only element on the screen belonging to no list: the four rows above are hairline-separated by `.dr-field + .dr-field::before`, and a `.dr-pick` draws its hairline only against another `.dr-pick`, so it had none. Not far from anything; simply bound to nothing. **The standards were read before the options, and they pointed the other way.** `HIG · Toolbars` gives the bottom bar to **actions on the current view**, and a persistent preference is not one — there is no HIG line that puts a settings control in a toolbar. `HIG · Toggles` is the placement rule that does exist for this control: iOS has no checkbox at all, its equivalent is the switch, and a switch belongs **in a list row** (rev 92 put the switch to her as option E; she chose the checkbox drawing, and the placement half of the rule reads the same either way). `HIG · Layout` asks that a control sit beside what it affects — this one governs the two **address** rows and sat after *Parcel size* and *Notes*. So HIG's own answer was **upward, into the list**, not downward to the bar. **Conformance was not the argument, and saying so is the honest part:** the row failed nothing where it stood — **44** target (`HIG · 44pt` ✓), **24** box (`WCAG 2.5.8` ✓), edge `--ink` on `--bg` **15.99:1** (`1.4.11` asks 3:1 ✓) — and not one of those numbers moves with position. **Four placements were built live in the browser and measured before she was asked**, none of them written to disk: **A** as built · **B1** in the list under *Drop-off* (void 238.6, checkbox → CTA 486.4) · **B2** last row of the list (259.4) · **C** in the action bar above the CTA (**8**). All four fit the 812 frame without scrolling, so the choice was never about room. **Her call is C**, and it is recorded as a **departure from `HIG · Toolbars` with its reason, not as a defect**: the bar is where the order is committed, and *save this for next time* is a rider on that commit — it is read at the moment it takes effect, which is also the reading `ui/inventory.md` has carried since 2026-08-02 (the row is a checkbox and not a switch precisely *because* it sits above a **Continue** button and does not act on flip; that sentence is now literally true rather than figuratively). **The two costs the move creates are paid in the markup rather than left implicit.** `WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2` — leaving `<form aria-label="Order details">` would have made it a control of no form, so the form takes `id="order-details"` and the input takes `form="order-details"`; verified, not assumed, by reading `form.elements` on the built page: `pk · dp · (parcel) · nt · save-address`, the checkbox still the form's fifth control from outside it. `WCAG 2.4.3` — it is placed **before** the button in the DOM, so reading order still equals visual order; verified by keyboard, Tab from the notes field lands on the checkbox and the next Tab lands on *Continue to review*. **Measured after (375 × 812, headless):** bar **80.8 → 132.8** (12 / 16 / 24 padding, `--sp-within-lg` 8 gap), `main` **629.6 → 577.6** and still does not scroll — `scrollHeight 578 == clientHeight 578` — horizontal overflow **0**. Row **341.4 × 44**, box **24 × 24** at **x 122.9** — the same left axis as the button and as every field label above, so the bar reads as one column and not as a button with a note stuck on it — **8px** to the button, **24.8px** from the button to the frame's bottom edge, so the home-indicator inset `HIG · Layout` asks for is untouched. **Behaviour re-verified after the move, because rev 94 is the reason this file is distrusted:** a click on the row toggles it (fill `transparent → #9BCF4A`, glyph `--on-green`), **Space** on the focused input toggles it, and the ring paints on `:focus-visible` at **2.4px `--ink`, offset 1.6** — the box's own ring, unchanged by living in a footer. **The green budget does not move, but the adjacency is new and is hers to look at.** Area is identical to before — the same two shapes, only relocated: CTA **4.93%** of the frame, checked box **0.19%**, **5.12%** total, with the CTA **96.3%** of the green (§5 asks 81–100% ✓, and rev 47 already counts a checked box as a status mark rather than as the screen's one green control). What changed is that the two green shapes now stand **18px apart** on the bottom edge instead of half a screen apart; the render reads as one column, not one blob, but it is the first time in the product that green sits next to green and it is named here rather than discovered later. **The bar's contents are now a set of three, and the set is counted rather than guessed:** every `footer.dr-actionbar` in `wireframes/` holds buttons; **one** holds a caption (`order-setup-error` · `.dr-note`), **two** hold a price summary (`time-slot`, `time-slot-empty` · `.dr-price`), and this is the **first interactive non-button control** in any of them. The rule that governs it is written into `_conventions.md` §11 rather than left in this entry, and it deliberately keeps rev 114's sentence intact: prose that explains something in `main` stays in `main`; what may join the button is a control that **belongs to the same commit**. **No sibling to sync:** `order-setup-empty` (no operator) and `order-setup-error` (drop-off coverage) carry no such row — counted, 0 and 0 — so `_conventions.md` §12's no-reflow contract asks nothing here. **`microcopy.md` is untouched on purpose** — same string, same screen, same type; only the position moved, which the master table's zone column does not track (rev 114's own reading). **Reported for the third time and still not swept:** the label carries an inline `style="border-radius:6px;"` — dead (the row has neither background nor border to round) and **not one of the six** on the closed ramp. Named at rev 94, again at rev 99, and raised by the design hook on this pass; it is one line inside `wireframes/`, which is not swept without her word, so it is left exactly as built and asked about instead. **Also hers, and unrelated to placement:** the string says *this **address*** singular while the form holds two, *Pickup* and *Drop-off*. | `wireframes/order-setup.html`, `wireframes/_conventions.md`, `ui/inventory.md`, `concept/concept.md` rev 115

  *(Numbering note: **114 is used twice** — the city level's action bar and the value axis, two parallel sessions on the same day — so this entry takes **115** and neither of them is renumbered, on the rev 92 precedent that silently restamping someone else's entry is worse than the collision it fixes.)*

- **2026-08-16 (rev 116)** — **`report-issue-empty`'s NO FILE becomes the alert triangle, and the standing placeholder count drops to three.** The designer's word on the built page: *«put icon alert»*. This is the **sixth** run of the same trade — an uppercase word standing in for a picture, replaced by the picture, drawn in the one icon system rather than imported — after `delivery-error`, `tracking-error`, `listings-error` (rev 62), `listings-empty` (rev 67) and `contact-support-error` (rev 83). **Nothing was drawn that the set did not already own.** The Warning glyph has stood in `concept.html` §0.2's icon sheet since the sheet was built and rendered on **no product page** — grepped across `wireframes/`, one hit, and it was the sheet: the outline `M12 3.8 → 2.6 20 → 21.4 20 Z`, the stem `M12 10v4.2`, the dot `cx 12 cy 17.4 r .9` filled `currentColor`. So this pass imports nothing, invents no path and adds no token; it spends a glyph that was already specified. **The triangle coming back is not a rejected idea re-proposed, and the difference is worth writing down** because §0.2's own rule is that the picture must say what the copy says. Rev 62 took the triangle **off** `listings-error`: there it marked a dead connection, where a triangle says only *something is wrong* and the wi-fi fan says the thing. Here the copy **is** a caution — *a weaker claim → manual review only → takes longer* — which is the amber row's own definition in §07 (*delay, action needed*). The glyph names the consequence, and the consequence is what the two sentences are about. **One defect closed in passing, the same one rev 83 closed and for the same reason.** The mark carried **no `aria-hidden`** while it held the string `NO FILE`, which is correct for a word and wrong for a picture. It takes `aria-hidden="true"` now, so the `role="status"` region announces **"No evidence attached"** and its body and nothing else — verified on the build, `mark.textContent === ""` (`WCAG 1.1.1` ✓, `4.1.2` ✓, `4.1.3` ✓). The state also stops opening on two words that were never written for a person. **Conformance, measured on the build and not asserted (375 × 812, headless).** Mark **52 × 52**, `--r-media` **13px**, `--media` `#E4E1DA` well carrying `--slate` `#5A5751`: **5.513:1**, against `WCAG 1.4.11`'s 3:1 for a meaningful graphic — and the criterion is arguably not even owed here, the glyph being `aria-hidden` and duplicative of the title, so the pair clears the strict reading and the lenient one both. `WCAG 1.4.1` is untouched: the meaning is in the title, not in the shape or the hue. Glyph **26px** in the 52px slot, so the declared `stroke-width:1.7` renders **1.842** — inside the set's 0.85–2.27 band, and the same number rev 83's paper plane measured. Well on card is **1.078:1**, a surface step and not a text pair. `main` **scrollHeight 577 == clientHeight 577**, frame **812**, horizontal overflow **0** — the block was the same height as a word and is the same height as a glyph. **Green is untouched** — the edit adds none and the bar keeps the screen's one green control, *Contact support*. **The glyph was read at 2.5× before it shipped**, the test rev 83 established: at 65px the base clears the dot and the stem clears both, and the shape is unmistakably a caution triangle rather than a play mark. **Drawn in the neutral `.dr-mark`, and that is the conservative half of the change rather than a settled call.** Her word named the glyph, not the well, so the well is left exactly as built — which is also what the two most recent conversions did (`listings-empty`, `contact-support-error` are both neutral; only `tracking-empty` is `--warn`, and the three error screens are `--danger`). **The tone was put to her with both ratios and her call is neutral:** the options were `--slate` on `--media` at **5.513:1** or `--warn-ink` `#7A5200` on `--warn-wash` `#F6E7C4` at **5.650:1** — **both conform**, both clear `1.4.11` by more than 1.8×, so the choice was meaning and not contrast. Amber was the arguable one, §07 defining it for exactly this (*delay, action needed*), and it is recorded as the rejected reading rather than left to be re-proposed: **a weaker claim is not a failure**, and the well the two most recent conversions already stand in keeps the state from reading as one. **One stale sentence found and reported rather than swept:** `ui/kit.css:780` still says *«--danger-ink already strokes the alert triangle (rev 54)»*, which rev 62 made untrue the same week — the triangle strokes `--slate` here and `--danger-ink` nowhere. It is one comment line and it is left as found. | `wireframes/report-issue-empty.html`, `microcopy.md`, `concept/concept.md` rev 116

- **2026-08-16 (rev 117)** — **Location leads the filter panel; Time drops to second.** The designer's word on the built `listings-filters`, pointing at the place card: *«помісти картку самою першою на сторінці»*. **Nothing was failing, and that was said before the options were.** `HIG · Layout` asks that what matters most sit where the eye lands first, and `WCAG 1.3.2 Meaningful Sequence` asks only that the reading order carry the meaning — neither names which filter dimension should lead, so **both orders conform** and this is a priority call, not a correction. **The zone travels whole:** its `.zlabel` moves with its `.dr-rows` card, so three orders are identical after the move exactly as they were before it — DOM, visual, and tab (`WCAG 1.3.2`, `2.4.3`). Measured in the tab order: `×` · *Clear all* · **Country · Region · City · District** · the four Time radios · the three Price bands · the two range thumbs · *Clear all* · *Apply filters*. **Nothing crossed the fold, and that was measured rather than assumed.** The panel is a 16px-gap column in a **566px** window: `Location 16–208 · Time 224–432 · Price 448–724.5`, scroll height **741**, so **175px of Price** stands below the fold — precisely what stood below it when Time led at `16–224` and Location sat at `240–432`, because the two cards are **192** and **208** and a column's total is order-blind. What changed is which card is read first, and only that. **The reason is hers and is recorded as hers.** What the build can say for it: place is the dimension the other two depend on — Time and Price narrow a set that Location has to produce — and no value inside any of the three zones was touched. The `.md` went first, as the content rule requires: `_screens.md`'s sub-view entry and `_conventions.md` §12 were corrected to *Location / Time / Price*, and `microcopy.md`'s row block moved with them.

- **2026-08-16 (rev 118)** — **The filter's fourth Time option stops being a word and becomes a date picker: a calendar drawer, and the count rule learns that a date is not a list.** The designer on the built `listings-filters`, on the row reading *Pick a date & time…*: *«при кліку відкрий дата пікер як у IOS»*. **iOS ships three date pickers and they are not interchangeable, so the fork was put to her with the measurements before the options** — `HIG · Pickers` calls a date picker *an efficient interface for selecting a specific date, time, or both* and asks for it *in a popover or a sheet when space is limited*, which a 375 frame is, but it does not choose between compact, inline and wheels. **Her call is the calendar grid in a drawer** — `UIDatePickerStyle.compact`'s expansion, the shape Apple's own Calendar and Reminders open. **The other two were refused by number, not by taste, and the numbers are on the record so neither is re-proposed later as new:** a **wheel** row is **34pt**, which clears `WCAG 2.5.8`'s 24 × 24 and **fails `HIG · 44pt target` by 10** — Apple's own drum is exempt because the column is *scrolled* rather than tapped, and a web build inherits the fail and not the exemption; **unfolding in place** (`.inline`) adds no modal layer but takes the panel's scroll height from **741 to ~1,050** against a **566** window, and the zone it pushes down is Price, already 175px below the fold after rev 117. **THE RULE GAINED AN EXCEPTION RATHER THAN THE COMPONENT BENDING TO IT.** rev 102's picker rule is a count — ≤ 6 options a drawer, ≥ 7 a pushed screen — and counting a date gives **365**, which would send it to a screen. A date is not a list of options; it is a value with a shape, and `HIG` gives it its own control. `_conventions.md` now says so in as many words, so the next person meets the exception instead of rediscovering it. **THE GRID CLEARS 44 BY ARITHMETIC, NOT BY EYE.** The sheet is the frame less its own 16 either side — **343**; `.dr-cal` keeps `.dr-rows`' 8/0 inset, so seven columns divide **327** into **46.71** each (measured **46.48**) and the day circle is a fixed **44 × 44** centred in it — `HIG · 44pt` ✓ with **2.48** of slack per column. **Six week rows always**, because a grid that changes height makes the sheet jump between a 5-row month and a 6-row one. **THREE STATES, THREE CARRIERS, AND NOT ONE OF THEM COLOUR ALONE (`WCAG 1.4.1`).** *Selected* is `--green` under `--on-green`, **9.46:1** — the pair the checked radio and the walked node already paint, so nothing new was invented. *Today* keeps its `--ink` numeral at **14.37:1** and takes a **4px `--sz-today-dot`** beneath it in `currentColor`, which inverts to `--on-green` at **9.46:1** the moment today is also the pick; **iOS marks today in the accent as TEXT, and §0 forbids that** — `--green` as a word is **1.52:1** on the card — **so the accent becomes a shape. A departure, recorded and kept conforming, not corrected away.** *Unavailable* — any date before today, because this filter asks when the service *should happen* — is `--slate` on `--card` at **5.95:1** with the element's own `disabled`, **built and never dimmed**, §0's own rule. **THE GRID IS WAI-ARIA APG'S DATE PICKER DIALOG WITH ONE DOCUMENTED VARIANT: SELECTION FOLLOWS FOCUS.** APG moves focus with the arrows and takes the value on Enter; here an arrow both moves and picks, so what is focused is always what is chosen — and the strict reading is worse in this exact control, where arrowing to a date and pressing Done hands back the date you left behind. APG allows the variant for grids. Verified in the browser rather than asserted: `→` **Sun 16 → Mon 17**, `↓` **→ Mon 24**, `PageDown` **→ September 2026**, five `↑` back to **August**, `‹` **disabled** in the current month, **15 of 31** August days `disabled`, **six rows**, the month name `aria-live="polite"` (`WCAG 2.1.1`, `4.1.2`). **THE TIME IS TWO NATIVE `<select>`s, AND `<input type="time">` WAS BUILT FIRST AND MEASURED OUT.** iOS renders a `<select>` as its own **wheel drum**, so the time *is* the iOS picker and arrives with the arrow keys, the exposed value and the accessible name for free — the same reason `.dr-range` is two native sliders. The time input's face, though, is drawn by the UA in the **viewer's** locale and not the document's: it read **`02:00 PM`** on a 12-hour machine against a product that writes `09:00 – 10:00` on `time-slot` and `28 Jun · 14:20` on the order card, and `lang` on the document **and** on the element both left it 12-hour — tested, not assumed. Two selects carry their own option text, so 24-hour holds on every machine. The pair takes a `--media` ground at `--r-input`, **13.33:1**, the **third** field in the system to keep a ground after the search bar and the empty multiline area, and for their reason: **there is no value beside it to say it is a control.** **THE OPENER IS A RADIO, WHICH IS WHY THE ELLIPSIS IS LOAD-BEARING.** The option has to stay mutually exclusive with *Available now / Today / This week*, so it keeps the group and the arrow keys rather than becoming a `.dr-field--action` row — but selecting it opens a dialog, and that is a change of context. **`WCAG 3.2.2 On Input` permits it only if the user was told beforehand**, and a trailing **`…`** is precisely Apple's convention for *more input is needed before this completes* (`HIG · Menus`, `HIG · Buttons`). The label was already written that way; `aria-haspopup="dialog"` says the same to assistive tech and `aria-expanded` tracks it. **Done writes the value into the option's own label** — the row stops reading *Pick a date & time…* and reads **`Sat 22 Aug, 17:30`**, which is what iOS does with a set date row and what `kit.js` already does when an option is chosen — and **Cancel puts back whichever option was checked before the drawer rose**. Both verified, with Esc, the scrim, **four** children `inert` behind the drawer, and focus handed back to the row. **The date string is built from arrays and not from `toLocaleDateString`'s `{month:'short'}`**, which returns **`Sept`** for September on current ICU — four letters where every date already written in this product gives three. **GREEN, MEASURED ON THE OPEN DRAWER:** `Done` **7,335px² · 2.44%** plus the selected day **1,521px² · 0.51%** = **2.95%** of the frame, inside the ~5% ceiling, with the primary at **82.8%** of the green — inside §0's own 81–100% band. Exactly one green **control** above the scrim; the day circle is a status mark, like the checked radio. **One new token, `--sz-today-dot` 4px** — not `--sz-dot` 10 and not `--sz-pager-dot` 8, both of which are the whole carrier of their state, where this one sits *under* a numeral inside a 44 circle. **THE BEHAVIOUR IS A PAGE-LEVEL SCRIPT AND THAT IS STATED, NOT LEFT TO BE FOUND:** `ui/kit.js` answers only to `.dr-field--action[aria-controls]` rows, this opener is a radio in a group, and one page carries the component — but it keeps `kit.js`'s modal contract **verbatim**, so the day a second screen asks for a calendar the promotion is a file move and nothing else, the same promotion `.dr-range` is still waiting for.

  *(Numbering note: `_conventions.md` §7 already spends **117** in prose, for the day the language switch left the top bar — a change that never reached this log. This entry takes **118**, and that prose is left standing, on the rev 92 and rev 115 precedent that silently restamping someone else's number is worse than the collision it fixes.)*

- **2026-08-16 (rev 119)** — **`resolution`'s two secondaries stop sharing a row and stack, and the split pair turns out to have been breaking a label.** The designer on the built page, pointing at the action bar: *«постав кнопки одна під одною»*. **Nothing was asserted before it was measured, and the measurement found a defect the eye had already caught.** The bar held `Done` full-width over a `.dr-actionbar__row` splitting *Book again — free* and *Contact support* into **166.5** each. A `.dr-btn` is `padding: 0 16px`, so the label had **134.5px** of room; *Book again — free* renders at **134.81px** — **short by 0.31px**, so it **wrapped to two lines** while its neighbour stayed on one. Read off the built page with a `Range`, not eyeballed: `lines: 2` against `lines: 1`. A pair drawn as equals that is ragged by a third of a pixel is not a pair. **Neither layout was failing a criterion, and that is said first.** `HIG · 44pt target`: every button is `min-height: 44` and measured **44** in both — the wrap cost no height, two 21px lines being 42. `WCAG 2.5.8`: 24 × 24, cleared by both. `WCAG 1.4.3`: charcoal on `--btn2` **11.54:1**, charcoal on `--green` **9.46:1** — the same two pairs before and after, no colour cut and none gained. `WCAG 2.4.3`: the DOM order was `Done · Book again · Contact support` and still is, so reading order equals visual order — verified in the tab walk, `Back to Help · EN · UA · Done · Book again — free · Contact support`. **What the stack is worth is legibility and a pattern, not conformance.** Each secondary goes **166.5 → 341**, the wrap disappears (`lines: 1` on all three), and the bar stops asking the eye to weigh two grey buttons against each other side by side when they are not siblings — *Book again* opens a new order, *Contact support* escalates. `HIG` gives the two-across shape to **two mutually exclusive choices of equal weight** (the alert's Cancel/Confirm, which is exactly what `account-edit` still uses it for); three actions of unequal weight are a column. **This invents nothing — it makes the product's two three-action bars identical.** `contact-support` has carried `primary--block` over two `secondary--block` since it was built; `resolution` now carries the same three classes in the same order, byte for byte. Counted across `wireframes/`: **9 of 12** multi-button bars already lead with the primary, so the column is the built convention and not a new one. **`.dr-actionbar__row` is not deleted and its CSS is untouched** — it now stands on **one** page, `account-edit` (*Cancel* / *Save changes*), which is the genuine equal-pair case, and `ui/kit.css:481–482` is left exactly as written. **Measured after (375 × 812, headless):** bar **133 → 185** (12 / 16 / 24 padding, 8 gap — `13 · 8 · 8 · 25` from the bar's top edge to the frame's bottom, so the home-indicator inset `HIG · Layout` asks for is untouched), `main` **577 → 525** and **still does not scroll** — `scrollHeight 525 == clientHeight 525` — horizontal overflow **0** on the frame and on the document. **Green does not move.** `Done` was already `--block`, so it is **341 × 44** before and after: **15,004px² · 4.93%** of the frame, one green control, **100%** of the screen's green — inside §0's ~5% ceiling and its 81–100% band. The milestone mark is `--green-wash` `#E7F0D2` at **1.08:1** on the page and is not counted, per §0's own rule. **`microcopy.md` is untouched on purpose:** same three strings, same screen, same type steps — only the geometry moved. **One stale line found and reported rather than swept:** `ui/inventory.md:83`'s Action bar row reads *«Secondary above primary, so the thumb reaches the primary first»*, which the build contradicts **9 times out of 12** and has since long before this pass. It is hers to settle — correct the sentence to the built convention, or change the three bars that disagree — and it is left as found. | `wireframes/resolution.html`, `concept/concept.md` rev 119

If the group should be named again it comes back as a heading above the pair in its new place, and `microcopy.md` decides it first. **MEASURED, 375 × 800, ANNOTATIONS OFF, PRODUCT VIEW.** The heading's own **18.2** plus its **12** `margin-top` plus the stack's **12** row gap = **42.2**: the form goes **652.2 → 610** and `.dr-main`'s scroll **684 → 642** against a **618** window, so the overflow drops **66 → 24**. Every block now sits on the flat **12** the stack spends — Personal card **104**, Preferences card **148**, upload **161**, upload **161** — and the content closes **15.6** above `.dr-main`'s own 16 bottom padding. **THE MOVE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE SPACE IT SAVES.** Before it, the two 161px zones stood third and fourth and pushed the Preferences card to **673–821** against a fold at **770.8**: `Language` and part of `Saved address` were reachable only by scrolling, on a screen whose job is editing them. This is the same 4.93% `resolution`'s `Done` carries, and unlike rev 121's drawer nothing goes over. **`job-offer` loses `.row2` the same way** — *Accept* / *Decline* **166.5 → 341** each, DOM order untouched, and `main` **still does not scroll** (`scrollHeight 577 == clientHeight 577`); under an offer that expires in 8s the thumb should not have to weigh two equal boxes.
